283 — Temporal Logic Evaluator

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a temporal logic evaluator: parsers for LTL and CTL (plus past-time LTL and finite-trace LTLf); an automata engine that translates LTL to generalized Buchi automata (GPVW on-the-fly tableau), degeneralizes to Buchi, products with a Kripke structure, and checks emptiness by nested depth-first search; explicit-state CTL model checking by the iterative labeling algorithm; finite-trace and lasso evaluation; and a runtime monitor based on formula progression with 3-valued (LTL3) and RV-LTL 4-valued verdicts. Evaluate formulas over traces, monitor event streams, and model-check Kripke structures with lasso counterexamples; differential- test against Spot. It evaluates temporal logic — not a symbolic/BDD model checker (#281) nor a general model checker (#279).

Project Number: 283

Project Name: Temporal Logic Evaluator

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal: Build a from-scratch, explicit-state / automata-theoretic temporal logic evaluator in Rust — parse LTL & CTL (plus past-LTL & finite-trace LTLf), model-check Kripke structures, and monitor event streams at runtime — delivered as seven embeddable tl-* crates plus the tl CLI, validated to verdict-parity with Spot.

1.1 Precise engineering definition (what is built)

A workspace of seven Rust crates implementing the full trace + automata pipeline for propositional temporal logics. Concretely, the system:

# Capability Mechanism Surface
1 Parse LTL, CTL, past-LTL, LTLf → typed AST tl-syntax lexer/parser; precedence-climbing Formula
2 Normalize / classify NNF (negation only on atoms), simplify, closure, Kind well-formedness nnf, closure, Kind
3 Model words & structures finite + lasso-shaped Trace; KripkeStructure State, Trace, KripkeStructure
4 Evaluate finite-trace LTL (LTLf) formula progression (the LTL derivative) folded over a finite Trace eval_ltlf
5 Model-check CTL explicit-state iterative labeling (EX preimage / EU lfp / EG gfp via SCCs; derive EF/AG/AF/AX/A[U]) CtlChecker::sat, ::holds
6 Model-check LTL (automata-theoretic) GPVW on-the-fly tableau ltl_to_ngba → degeneralize → product(¬φ × Kripke) → emptiness (nested DFS) → lasso CE check_ltl, Lasso
7 Monitor a stream at runtime incremental progress, 3-valued LTL3 + RV-LTL 4-valued RvVerdict, monitorability, monitor-automaton synthesis Monitor::step
8 Evaluate past-time LTL dynamic programming (per-state past-obligation vector from prior state) Y / O / H / S

Core invariant (correctness contract): K ⊨ Aφ ⟺ L(product(Nba(¬φ), K)) = ∅, i.e. the product has no reachable accepting cycle. Emptiness is decided by nested DFS; a witness cycle is returned as a Lasso { stem, cycle } counterexample. CTL holds ⟺ every init state ∈ sat(f).

1.2 System boundaries (in scope)

  • Logics: propositional LTL, CTL, past-time LTL, finite-trace LTLf. (CTL* parsed/classified only where the spine's Kind distinction requires it; full CTL* model checking is not a goal.)
  • Inputs: formula text + one of {Trace, KripkeStructure, event stream}. Outputs: bool / per-state sat labeling / RvVerdict / Lasso counterexample / automaton (HOA or dot).
  • Models: explicit-state — APs as a Bitset, states/transitions enumerated. No symbolic state-set representation.
  • Reference equivalence: automaton acceptance & model-check verdicts must match Spot (ltl2tgba) on the standard LTL pattern formulas + random formulas (Phase 13 gate).

1.3 Explicit NON-goals (out of scope — sibling portfolio projects)

Excluded Why / owner
Symbolic / BDD model checker This engine is explicit-state / automata+trace, not BDD-symbolic → that is #281.
General model-checker front end + modeling language #279.
Theorem prover #280.
SAT / SMT core #277 / #278.
Probabilistic / timed / real-time logics (MTL, TCTL) Out of scope (stretch only; see §1.7).
LTL synthesis (formula → reactive controller) Out of scope (stretch; §1.7).
GUI / IDE / web front end Out of scope; tl is a CLI only.

These exclusions are stated once and referenced by #NNN thereafter — no re-derivation.

1.4 Deployment model & users

  • Form factor: six library crates (tl-syntax, tl-formula, tl-trace, tl-automata, tl-eval, tl-monitor) are no_std + alloc and embeddable (link into other verification tools, RV runtimes, or #279/#281); only tl-cli is std.
  • tl binary subcommands: eval (formula + trace → verdict), to-buchi (formula → automaton; HOA / dot), check (formula + Kripke → holds / counterexample), monitor (stream → streaming RvVerdict).
  • Audiences:
    • Verification / RV engineers — embed tl-monitor for online property checking; use tl check for offline model checking.
    • PL / formal-methods researchers — tl-automata as a clean, hackable GPVW + nested-DFS reference; HOA interop with Spot.
    • Students — readable, exhaustive-match AST + labeling + automata code to learn the automata-theoretic approach.

1.5 Phase-0 deliverable — the seven tl-* crates

Phase 0 (## 12) stands up the workspace with these seven members (LOCKED dependency order):

  1. tl-syntax — Lexer, Token, Parser, ParseError/ParseErrorKind; LTL/CTL/past-LTL/LTLf → Formula; pretty-printer. no_std + alloc.
  2. tl-formula — Formula AST + nnf, simplify/rewrite, closure, Kind well-formedness (state-vs-path); Ap = u32. no_std + alloc.
  3. tl-trace — State { aps: Bitset }, Trace { prefix, loop_start } (lasso if Some), KripkeStructure { states, init, edges }; validation. no_std + alloc.
  4. tl-automata — Ngba, Nba, Guard, Lasso; ltl_to_ngba (GPVW), degeneralize, product, emptiness (nested DFS). Deps: tl-formula, tl-trace.
  5. tl-eval — CtlChecker (labeling), eval_ltlf (progression), check_ltl (product+emptiness), past-LTL DP. Deps: tl-formula, tl-trace, tl-automata.
  6. tl-monitor — Monitor, progress, Verdict, RvVerdict (LTL3 / RV-LTL), monitorability, monitor-automaton synthesis. Deps: tl-formula, tl-automata.
  7. tl-cli — the tl binary (eval / to-buchi / check / monitor). Deps: all. std.

Phase-0 also ships: shells for Formula/Trace/KripkeStructure/Nba/Verdict, error types, CI + Makefile + Dockerfile, and a smoke test — parse G p, eval on a 1-state trace where p holds → true.

1.6 What later phases unlock

Phase (doc heading) Unlocks
1–2 (## 13–## 14) LTL & CTL parsing + NNF + Kind — front end usable.
3 (## 15) Models (State/Trace/KripkeStructure) + validation.
4 (## 16) tl eval on finite traces (LTLf progression; weak/strong next).
5 (## 17) tl check for CTL (labeling EX/EU/EG).
6–8 (## 18–## 20) tl to-buchi + tl check for LTL (GPVW → degeneralize → product → nested-DFS lasso CE).
9 (## 21) tl monitor (LTL3 / RV-LTL streaming verdicts).
10 (## 22) Past-LTL (Y/O/H/S) DP evaluation.
11 (## 23) LTLf → DFA (alternating-automaton / progression-to-DFA) + minimization.
12 (## 24) Optimizations: rewrite rules, state caching, symbolic guards, on-the-fly product.
13 (## 25) Differential testing vs Spot, fuzzing, benchmarks — the done gate.

Gated behind PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — not built in this blueprint; the full feature/phase/trigger mapping lives in the Deferred Features Register (back matter):

  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — MTL / timed logics (TCTL); dense-time semantics.
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — LTL synthesis (formula → reactive controller / Mealy machine).
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — probabilistic logics (PCTL).
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — symbolic/BDD state-set encoding (owned by sibling project #281, not rebuilt here).
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — parametric / first-order temporal logic.

Each stub panic!s with its PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") tag so unimplemented surface is unmistakable and grep-able.

1.8 Estimated timeline

Milestone Effort
Phase 0 (skeleton + smoke test) 6–14 weeks (1 engineer)
Front end + models (Phases 1–3) +2–4 weeks
CTL + LTLf evaluators (Phases 4–5) +3–5 weeks
Automata pipeline (Phases 6–8) +6–10 weeks (the hardest, riskiest block)
Monitoring + past-LTL + LTLf-DFA (Phases 9–11) +4–7 weeks
Optimizations + validation (Phases 12–13) +4–8 weeks
Full system to the Phase-13 done gate ≈ 6–9 months

1.9 Recommended language — Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78)

Automata construction + graph emptiness + trace evaluation are pointer/graph-heavy and hot. Rust is chosen because:

  • Enums + exhaustive match model Formula, automaton states, and Verdict/RvVerdict as zero-cost tagged unions — a missing Formula variant in NNF / labeling / progression is a compile error, not a runtime mis-verdict.
  • Bitsets for AP valuations (State.aps) + arena-allocated automaton states → cache-friendly nested DFS with memory safety.
  • The canonical reference (Spot) is C++ and maps cleanly to Rust, while Rust removes the manual-memory hazards of on-the-fly automaton construction (dangling/aliased graph nodes).
  • no_std + alloc on the six libraries keeps them embeddable.

Rejected: C/C++ (memory-unsafe automaton graphs); Python/Java (nested DFS + product too hot for GC + boxing); Haskell (strict mutable DFS-coloring + on-the-fly construction fight laziness).

1.10 Required prerequisites (reader / contributor)

  • LTL & CTL semantics — path vs state formulas; X/U/R/F/G; the CTL path-quantifier+temporal-op fusion.
  • ω-automata & Büchi acceptance — Nba / Ngba, ω-words, accepting runs, lasso (stem + cycle), language emptiness.
  • The automata-theoretic approach — Vardi–Wolper; GPVW (Gerth–Peled–Vardi–Wolper 1995) on-the-fly tableau; degeneralization.
  • Fixpoints — least/greatest fixpoint characterization of EU/EG (Clarke–Emerson–Sistla 1986).
  • Graph algorithms — DFS, SCC decomposition, nested DFS for memory-efficient emptiness (Courcoubetis–Vardi–Wolper–Yannakakis).
  • Runtime verification — formula progression / derivative (Roşu–Havelund); LTL3 / RV-LTL, good/bad prefixes, monitorability (Bauer–Leucker–Schallhart); LTLf finite-trace semantics (De Giacomo–Vardi).

Gravest failure class (motivates the Phase-13 gate): a wrong automaton (GPVW / degeneralization bug) or a buggy nested DFS silently flips a verdict (reports holds when a counterexample exists, or vice versa) with no crash — so differential testing vs Spot is the primary correctness guard.

2. Why Build This

Temporal logic evaluation is the algorithmic core of every formal-verification and runtime-verification tool. This project builds that core from scratch — the automata-theoretic LTL pipeline, CTL fixpoint labeling, and progression-based monitoring — without a BDD/symbolic engine (that is #281). The payoff is mastery of a small set of deep, reusable systems algorithms that most engineers only ever consume as black boxes inside SPIN, NuSMV, or Spot.

2.1 Unique systems knowledge taught

Capability What you learn that nothing else teaches Locked artifact
Automata-theoretic LTL The Vardi-Wolper reduction: model checking = automaton complementation + emptiness; K ⊨ A φ iff L(product(Nba(¬φ), K)) = ∅ check_ltl, product, emptiness
On-the-fly tableau (GPVW) A node = obligation set over the subformula closure, split now (edge Guard) / next (successor); eventualities (U,F) tracked by generalized accept sets; states materialized lazily, not enumerated up front ltl_to_ngba -> Ngba
Degeneralization k accept sets -> single accept set via a state × counter∈0..k product; visiting the one set i.o. ⟺ visiting all k i.o. degeneralize -> Nba
Buchi emptiness Nested DFS: outer DFS reaches accepting states; inner DFS finds a path back (a reachable accepting cycle); the witness is a Lasso { stem, cycle } — and why one DFS pass is insufficient emptiness -> Option<Lasso>
CTL fixpoint labeling Explicit-state Clarke-Emerson-Sistla: sat(EX p)=preimage, sat(E[pUq])=lfp, sat(EG p)=gfp via SCC quotient; all other operators by duality; holds = every init state labeled CtlChecker::sat, ::holds
Progression / derivative The LTL "derivative" progress(f,s): a residual Formula after consuming one State; a True/False residual = definitive verdict Monitor::step, Monitor::progress
3/4-valued RV semantics LTL3 (good prefix->True, bad prefix->False, else Unknown) vs RV-LTL (PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse via finite tie-break); monitorability = some extension can still reach a definitive verdict RvVerdict
LTLf finite-trace Progression to a definitive bool at end-of-trace; the weak-vs-strong X distinction on finite words (X false at last position) eval_ltlf
Past-time LTL DP recurrence: each state's past-obligation vector derived from the previous state in one forward sweep (Y/O/H/S) — O( φ

2.2 Why temporal logic evaluation matters (historical + architectural)

  • It is the engine, not the wrapper. SPIN compiles LTL to a never-claim Buchi automaton and runs nested DFS over the product — exactly the ltl_to_ngba -> degeneralize -> product -> emptiness chain here. NuSMV's CTL mode is the same labeling fixpoint algorithm CtlChecker implements. Building it reveals what those tools actually compute.
  • Spot is the canonical reference (twa, ltl2tgba); it is C++ and maps cleanly onto Rust enums + arenas. We treat it as the differential oracle (Phase 13 / ## 25), not a dependency.
  • Lineage we reimplement, line by line: GPVW 1995 (on-the-fly tableau); Vardi-Wolper (automata-theoretic approach); Clarke-Emerson-Sistla 1986 (CTL labeling); Courcoubetis-Vardi-Wolper-Yannakakis (nested DFS / memory-efficient emptiness); Bauer-Leucker-Schallhart (LTL3 / RV-LTL); Rosu-Havelund (monitoring via progression); De Giacomo-Vardi (LTLf).
  • Runtime verification is everywhere: every RV framework (MOP, RV-Monitor, telemetry assertions, protocol monitors) is tl-monitor — incremental progress folded over an event stream emitting an RvVerdict. This is the closest temporal logic gets to production hot paths.
  • Architectural lesson — three evaluation strategies, one AST. The same Formula feeds: explicit fixpoint labeling (CTL/branching), automaton product + graph emptiness (LTL/linear), and incremental rewriting (monitoring/online). Seeing one logic engine fan out to a graph algorithm, a fixpoint iteration, and a stream fold is the central design insight.

2.3 Engineering muscles built

  • Graph algorithms under correctness pressure: SCC decomposition for EG, two-color nested DFS for emptiness, lazy on-the-fly product so unreachable product states are never materialized (Phase 12 / ## 24).
  • AST design with exhaustive match: the Formula enum (LTL future/past + fused CTL operators) modeled as a zero-cost tagged union; nnf and closure as total, panic-free transformations; Kind well-formedness gating CTL state-vs-path misuse.
  • Cache-conscious data layout: State as an AP Bitset; arena-indexed Ngba/Nba states (Vec<Vec<(Guard,usize)>>); Guard as a mask+care DNF over APs.
  • Fixpoint reasoning: distinguishing lfp (E[pUq], eventualities) from gfp (EG, safety) and knowing which monotone operator each temporal connective induces.
  • Differential + adversarial testing: the gravest failure class is a silent verdict flip — a GPVW/degeneralization bug or buggy nested DFS reporting "holds" when a counterexample exists, with no crash. The discipline learned: oracle-based differential testing vs ltl2tgba, property fuzzing, and lasso re-validation against the trace.

2.4 Cross-links to portfolio projects (per spine)

Project Relationship Boundary
#281 Symbolic Model Checker Companion: same K ⊨ φ question, dual technique #281 = BDD/symbolic; this = explicit-state automata + trace. Non-goal overlap is deliberate — no BDD MC here
#279 Model Checker (front end) Downstream consumer #279 supplies a modeling language compiling to a KripkeStructure; this engine is the back-end checker
#280 Theorem Prover / #277-#278 SAT-SMT Adjacent decision procedures We do automata/graph + trace evaluation, not proof search or clause solving
Parser project Reuses lexer/Pratt-precedence discipline in tl-syntax (Lexer, Parser, ParseError) Same parsing craft, temporal grammar (X/F/G unary, U/R binary)
Automata / regex projects Shared NFA/DFA, on-the-fly construction, product, emptiness machinery Here generalized to omega-automata (Ngba/Nba, infinite words, lasso acceptance)

2.5 Calibration value

  • A self-contained, mid-sized spec: 7 crates, 14 phases, ~6–10 KLOC. Smaller than a SAT core, far richer than a parser — a clean calibration point for "graph-algorithm-heavy engine with a hard correctness oracle."
  • Quantified done-gate (Phase 13 / ## 25): automaton acceptance matches ltl2tgba on standard LTL pattern formulas + ≥10⁴ random formulas; CTL labeling matches known results; parser/automaton/eval fuzzed clean; targets — formula -> Ngba build well within practical bounds for |φ|≤~30, model-check time on Kripke structures up to ~10⁵ states sub-second on the explicit path.
  • Teaches the rare skill of trusting a verifier: every claimed verdict is either backed by a counterexample Lasso (LTL) or a per-state sat labeling (CTL), both independently checkable — the standard the rest of the portfolio's correctness work is measured against.

What Mastery Looks Like

  • Phase 1 (LTL syntax + NNF): Given any LTL formula with nested negations, you can push all negation to atoms by hand, citing the exact duality used for each operator (¬(p U q) = (¬p) R (¬q), ¬F = G¬, etc.), and verify idempotence — nnf(nnf(f)) = nnf(f) — without running code.
  • Phase 6 (GPVW tableau): You can trace the on-the-fly node expansion for a two-eventuality formula (e.g. G(p → F q) ∧ F r) step by step: split obligations into now-guards and next-obligations, assign one accept set per Until-subformula, and state exactly which nodes are merged as identical obligation sets.
  • Phase 8 (nested DFS emptiness): You can explain why launching the inner DFS in pre-order (rather than post-order) misses reachable accepting cycles, walk through the two-color invariant on a concrete product automaton, and extract the Lasso{stem, cycle} directly from the DFS color stacks.
  • Phase 5 (CTL labeling): Given a small Kripke structure and a CTL formula with EG, you can apply the Clarke–Emerson–Sistla fixpoint labeling by hand — computing the SCC quotient, seeding the greatest fixpoint, and iterating to convergence — and verify that holds matches each init state's label.
  • Phase 9 (runtime monitoring): You can classify a formula as monitorable or not, produce a good-prefix and a bad-prefix for it, and show exactly which step causes the RvVerdict to transition from Unknown to PresumablyTrue/False, citing the Bauer–Leucker–Schallhart semantics.
  • Phase 12 (on-the-fly optimization): You can bound the materialized product-state count for a specific formula+Kripke pair using the on-the-fly lazy construction versus the eager baseline, and explain why simplification before ltl_to_ngba reduces the number of degeneralization counter states.
  • Phase 13 (differential oracle): You can write a differential harness that cross-compares acceptance on random omega-words between tl's Nba and Spot's ltl2tgba HOA output, and diagnose a disagreement down to the specific GPVW node or degeneralization counter-wiring bug.

3. Architectural Overview

The evaluator is a seven-crate Rust workspace. tl-syntax + tl-formula form the shared front end: untrusted formula text becomes a typed Formula AST in NNF. From that single AST, four disjoint evaluation paths fan out — CTL labeling, LTL automata-theoretic model checking, LTLf/past-LTL trace evaluation, and runtime monitoring — every one of them grounded on the tl-trace model (State, Trace, KripkeStructure). tl-cli (the tl binary) is the only std member and the only I/O surface. No path persists state; all structures are in-memory, single-process, deterministic.

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

                       tl-cli   (tl: eval / to-buchi / check / monitor)            [std; only I/O surface]
                           |  formula text  +  (trace | Kripke structure | event stream)
   == TRUST BOUNDARY ======|=== untrusted: formula text + trace/Kripke/stream text ===========
                           v
   tl-syntax (Lexer/Parser -> Formula) ----> tl-formula (NNF; simplify; closure; well-formedness Kind)
                           |  Formula (NNF, validated by Kind)
        +------------------+------------------+----------------------+----------------------+
        v (CTL + Kripke)   v (LTL + Kripke)   v (LTL/LTLf + trace)    v (LTL + event stream)
   tl-eval CtlChecker    tl-automata:         tl-eval eval_ltlf       tl-monitor: progress
     labeling             ltl_to_ngba (GPVW)   (progression over a     (LTL3 / RV-LTL 4-valued
     EX/EU/EG fixpoints    -> degeneralize      finite Trace) ;         Verdict; monitor
     over Kripke states    -> product           past-LTL DP             automaton)
        |                  (Nba x Kripke)        |                       |
        |                  -> emptiness          |                       |
        |                  (nested DFS) -> Lasso |                       |
        v                  v                     v                       v
   sat labeling / holds  holds + lasso CE      bool verdict            streaming RvVerdict
                       \           |              /
                        v          v             v
                       tl-trace (State = AP bitset ; Trace finite+lasso ; KripkeStructure)

   == NO PERSISTENCE BOUNDARY == nothing below tl-cli touches disk/network; all in-memory, deterministic

Data flows:

  • In: formula text -> Lexer/Parser -> Formula -> nnf() -> NNF Formula; trace text -> Trace; Kripke text -> KripkeStructure; event lines -> State stream.
  • Out: Vec<bool> per-state sat labeling + holds: bool (CTL); holds + Lasso counterexample (LTL); bool verdict (LTLf/past); streaming RvVerdict (monitor); Ngba/Nba as HOA/dot (to-buchi).
  • Trust boundary: drawn directly below tl-cli. Formula text, trace text, Kripke text, and the event stream are all untrusted; tl-syntax (bounded recursion, sized errors) + tl-trace validation (total transition relation, well-formed lasso) are the only sanitizers. Everything below treats inputs as already-typed and total.
  • Persistence boundary: none. No file/network/clock/RNG dependency below tl-cli (RNG appears only in Phase 13 test harnesses, not the engine). Each invocation is a pure function of its inputs.

3.2 Per-Box Responsibilities

tl-cli — the tl binary, the single std crate and sole I/O boundary. Subcommands: eval (formula + trace -> verdict), to-buchi (formula -> Ngba/Nba as HOA or dot), check (formula + Kripke -> holds / Lasso counterexample), monitor (event stream -> streaming RvVerdict). In: argv + stdin/files (formula, trace, Kripke, stream text). Out: stdout verdict/automaton/counterexample, exit codes (0 holds, 1 counterexample, 2 usage/parse error). Depends on all six lower crates. Invariant: it is the only member with side effects; all engine logic stays pure below the trust boundary. Built across Phase 0 (skeleton wiring) and finalized as each subcommand's path lands (Phases 4/5/8/9).

tl-syntax — no_std + alloc lexer + parser for LTL, CTL, past-LTL, and LTLf concrete syntax into the Formula AST, plus a pretty-printer. Exposes Lexer, Token, Parser, ParseError/ParseErrorKind; interns each atomic proposition to an Ap = u32. In: untrusted formula text. Out: Formula or a sized, positioned ParseError. Precedence (locked): unary X/F/G/past unaries/Not bind tightest, then binary U/R/S, then And, then Or. Invariant: bounded recursion depth — no stack overflow on adversarial nesting; every accepted string round-trips through the pretty-printer to an equal Formula. The outermost line of defense at the trust boundary. Built in Phases 1 (LTL/NNF tokens + precedence) and 2 (CTL fused path-quantifier operators).

tl-formula — no_std + alloc home of the Formula AST and its transformations, the semantic core every path shares. Atom = Ap id. Provides nnf() (push Not to atoms via the locked dualities: not X = X not, not(p U q) = (not p) R (not q), not F = G not, not G = F not, not(EX) = AX(not), etc.), simplification/rewrite, closure() (subformula closure feeding GPVW and CTL labeling), and Kind well-formedness classification (Ltl/Ctl/PastLtl/Ltlf, enforcing the CTL state-formula-vs-path-formula distinction). In: raw Formula. Out: NNF Formula, Vec<Formula> closure, Kind. Invariant: after nnf(), negation appears only on atoms and the operator set is fixed — GPVW and the labeling algorithm depend on this precondition. Built in Phases 1 (NNF) and 2 (CTL well-formedness).

tl-trace — no_std + alloc word/structure model shared by all four evaluation paths. State { aps: Bitset } (which APs hold), Trace { prefix: Vec<State>, loop_start: Option<usize> } (finite prefix; Some => lasso-shaped infinite word), KripkeStructure { states, init, edges, /* successors */ } (states, init set, successor edges, labeling). In: parsed model text from tl-cli. Out: validated structures consumed by tl-eval, tl-automata, tl-monitor. Invariant: a KripkeStructure has a total transition relation (every state has ≥1 successor — required for correct CTL/Buchi semantics) and a Trace's loop_start, when present, indexes a valid prefix position (well-formed lasso). The inner sanitizer of the trust boundary. Built in Phase 3.

tl-automata — no_std + alloc omega-automata layer, depends on tl-formula + tl-trace. Types: Ngba (generalized Buchi, accept: Vec<Vec<usize>> = set of accept sets), Nba (Buchi, single accept: Vec<usize>), Guard (boolean over APs as DNF / bitset mask+care), Lasso { stem, cycle }. Pipeline: ltl_to_ngba (GPVW on-the-fly tableau — nodes = obligation sets over the closure split into now-guard / next-obligations; one generalized accept set per Until-eventuality), degeneralize (Ngba k accept sets -> Nba on state x counter 0..k), product (synchronous Nba x Kripke), emptiness (nested DFS -> Option<Lasso>). In: NNF Formula, KripkeStructure. Out: Ngba/Nba, Lasso. Invariant: the constructed automaton's language exactly equals the LTL formula's models, and emptiness returns Some(lasso) iff a reachable accepting cycle exists — this is the gravest correctness surface, since a GPVW/degeneralization/nested-DFS bug silently flips a verdict with no crash (primary guard: Phase 13 differential testing vs Spot's ltl2tgba). Built in Phases 6 (GPVW->Ngba), 7 (degeneralize + simplify), 8 (product + nested-DFS emptiness), with lazy/on-the-fly product in Phase 12.

tl-eval — no_std + alloc evaluators, depends on tl-formula + tl-trace + tl-automata. CtlChecker<'a> over a KripkeStructure: sat(f) -> Vec<bool> per-state labeling (sat(EX p) = preimage; sat(E[p U q]) = least fixpoint; sat(EG p) = greatest fixpoint via SCC quotient; EF/AG/AF/AX/A[U] by duality) and holds(f) -> bool (every init state labeled). eval_ltlf(f, t) -> bool (finite-trace LTL by formula progression, definitive at end-of-trace, weak/strong next distinction on finite words). check_ltl(f, k) -> Result<(), Lasso> (the automata-theoretic check: product(Nba(not phi), k) then emptiness; Ok = holds, Err(lasso) = counterexample). Past-LTL DP. In: NNF Formula + Trace/KripkeStructure. Out: labeling Vec<bool>, bool, Result<(), Lasso>. Invariant: K satisfies A phi iff L(product(Nba(not phi), K)) is empty; CTL holds iff every init state ∈ sat(f). Built in Phases 4 (LTLf), 5 (CTL labeling), 8 (LTL model check), 10 (past-LTL DP), 11 (LTLf->DFA).

tl-monitor — no_std + alloc runtime verification, depends on tl-formula + tl-automata. Verdict { True, False } (2-valued definitive); RvVerdict { True, False, PresumablyTrue, PresumablyFalse, Unknown } (LTL3 / RV-LTL 4-valued); Monitor (current progressed Formula or monitor-automaton state). Monitor::step(&mut self, s) -> RvVerdict folds progress(f, s) -> Formula (the LTL derivative: progress(X f,s)=f; progress(f U g,s)=progress(g,s) OR (progress(f,s) AND f U g); progress(G f,s)=progress(f,s) AND G f; progress(atom p,s)=True/False) over the event stream. Monitorability classification + optional monitor-automaton synthesis. In: NNF Formula + streamed State events. Out: per-event RvVerdict; a True/False residual = definitive (good/bad prefix). Invariant: a definitive verdict is emitted iff the observed prefix is a good prefix (all extensions satisfy phi -> True) or bad prefix (none do -> False); otherwise Unknown and the verdict is latched once definitive (never reverts). Built in Phase 9.

4. System Constraints

Quantified envelope for the explicit-state / automata-theoretic engine. Every number is a hard cap or a target the Phase 13 (## 25) benchmark + cap-enforcement tests assert. Exceeding a cap → typed error, never a panic or silent truncation. This engine is explicit-state; the symbolic/BDD reach belongs to #281 (see §4.6).

4.1 Constraint table

ID Dimension Target / hard cap Failure when exceeded Asserted by
C-1 Formula size ` phi ` (closure nodes) parse + NNF up to 10^4 subformulas; default GPVW cap at `
C-2 LTL → Ngba states worst case 2^O( phi ); cap Ngba.nstates <= 2^20 (1,048,576)
C-3 Degeneralized Nba states <= k * Ngba.nstates, k = #accept sets; cap Nba.nstates <= 2^20 degeneralize → AutomatonTooLarge Phase 7 (## 19)
C-4 Accept sets k (one per U) `k <= closure ; practical k <= 32`
C-5 Product Nba × Kripke states `Nba.nstates * S ; cap **2^32 logical**, **materialized <= 2^24` (on-the-fly, Phase 12)**
C-6 Kripke ` S ` (explicit) model-check up to 10^6 comfortably; ceiling 10^7 states (RAM-bound)
C-7 Kripke ` R ` (edges) `
C-8 CTL labeling cost **O(` f · (
C-9 LTLf trace length (eval) up to 10^7 states; progression bounded by ` phi ` per step
C-10 Monitor stream length unbounded (streaming); residual formula size bounded `<= O(2^ phi )` distinct, cached
C-11 Monitor per-event latency sub-microsecond for ` phi <= 10(small monitor automaton);<= 10 µsfor
C-12 Monitor throughput >= 10^6 events/s single-thread, small formula benchmark regression fail Phase 13 (## 25)
C-13 AP count per State Ap = u32; practical <= 4096 APs; Bitset sized to AP universe guard mask overflow → TooManyAps Phase 3 (## 15)
C-14 NNF / closure determinism identical Formula → byte-identical NNF + identical closure order nondeterminism = test failure Phase 1 (## 13)
C-15 Automaton determinism same Formula → same canonical Ngba/Nba (state IDs, edge order, accept sets) canonicalization test fail Phase 6/7/13
C-16 Verdict determinism same (formula, trace|Kripke|stream) → same Verdict/RvVerdict/Lasso (canonical shortest stem+cycle) differential-vs-Spot fail Phase 8/13

Formula node <= 24 B (boxed enum) ` phi
Ngba/Nba state <= 32 B + edge list nstates · (32 B + edges) arena, no per-state heap churn

CtlChecker labeling 1 bit/state/subformula, reused ` S

Total engine RSS target: <= 16 GB at the 10^7-state explicit ceiling; <= 256 MB for any formula with |phi| <= 30 and |S| <= 10^5.

4.4 Behavioral invariants

  • No panic on bad input. Lexer/parser → Result<_, ParseError>; cap breaches → typed Err (AutomatonTooLarge, ProductTooLarge, KripkeTooLarge, TraceTooLong, TooManyAps, MonitorStateBlowup). Panics reserved for internal-invariant violations only (debug builds).
  • Zero global init. no_std + alloc for crates 1–6; no lazy_static/OnceCell global state; binary tl does all setup in main. Startup cost = argv parse only.
  • Determinism end-to-end (C-14..C-16). Canonical state numbering (insertion order under a fixed obligation-set ordering), deterministic edge/accept ordering, deterministic tie-break for the shortest Lasso (min stem, then min cycle, then lexicographic state IDs). No RNG, no hashing-order dependence, no thread nondeterminism in the verdict path.
  • Soundness over speed (C-2/C-5). Hitting a size cap returns a typed cap error, never a wrong verdict — the gravest failure class (a silently flipped holds/counterexample from a bad automaton or DFS) is guarded by Phase 13 differential testing vs Spot, not by these caps.
  • Total transition relation. KripkeStructure validated: every state has >= 1 successor (else Err), so EG/cycle reasoning is well-defined.

4.5 Concurrency, platform, dependency budget

Constraint Value
Concurrency single-threaded verdict path (determinism); parallelism only in Phase 13 batch differential harness (per-formula, embarrassingly parallel)
Threads in tl-eval/tl-automata/tl-monitor none in hot path
External crates (libraries) zero heavy deps; allowed: bitvec/hand-rolled Bitset; no BDD crate (explicit-state by design)
tl-cli deps arg parser + HOA/dot writer only
Target arch 64-bit (u64 bitsets); MSRV 1.78, edition 2021
I/O streaming events for monitor (line-delimited AP valuations); no network

4.6 Explicit-state ceiling vs #281 (symbolic)

                 |S| reach            formula |phi| reach
 THIS (#283)     ~10^6 comfortable    GPVW cap |closure| <= 256,
 explicit /      10^7 hard ceiling    Ngba/Nba <= 2^20 states
 automata        (RAM, ~16 GB)        product <= 2^24 materialized
 ----------------------------------------------------------------
 #281 symbolic   10^20+ states        BDD-bounded, not state-count
 BDD             (implicit set repr)  bound — NON-GOAL here
  • This engine enumerates states/automaton nodes explicitly → its scaling wall is |S| and 2^O(|phi|) automaton blowup, not a BDD variable-ordering wall.
  • NON-GOAL: symbolic fixpoints / BDD edge guards as the primary representation (#281). Phase 12 (## 24) bitset/BDD edge guards are a local optimization of Guard only — they do not make the state space symbolic.
  • Beyond 10^7 states or a 2^20-state automaton, the correct answer is "use #281", surfaced as a typed cap error with the offending bound — never a degraded or wrong result.

5. Required Reading

Scope: enough to build tl-syntax/tl-formula/tl-trace/tl-automata/tl-eval/tl-monitor correctly the first time. Read order: Baier & Katoen Ch.5 (LTL) → Ch.6 (CTL) → GPVW 1995 → CESY nested DFS → CES 1986 → BLS RV-LTL → De Giacomo-Vardi LTLf. Each entry: what to extract, where it lands.

NNF dualities below are theorems, not choices Phase 1 (## 13): operator set and NNF dualities in tl-formula/src/nnf.rs; Phase 6 (## 18): closure subformulas for GPVW tableau

Phase 10 Phase 10 (## 22): eval_past DP recurrences in tl-eval/src/past.rs; boundary condition Y at position 0 = False
CTL / CTL* Emerson-Clarke 1982; Emerson-Halpern (CTL*) State vs path formula grammar → Kind well-formedness; CTL = each path quantifier immediately fused with one temporal op (our Ex/Ax/Eg/Ag/Ef/Af/Eu/Au) Phase 2 (## 14): Kind well-formedness and state-vs-path rejection in tl-formula/src/kind.rs

HOA — Hanoi Omega-Automata adl.github.io/hoaf (Babiak et al., TACAS 2015) Normative automaton interchange format for to-buchi output and Spot interop. Header HOA: v1, States:, Start:, AP:, Acceptance: (Büchi = 1 Inf(0); generalized = n Inf(0)&…&Inf(n-1)), --BODY--, [guard] dst {acc}. Ngba/Nba serialize to/from this Phase 6/7 (## 18-19): HOA serializer in tl-automata/src/hoa.rs; Phase 13 (## 25): Spot interop for differential oracle
dot / Graphviz graphviz.org Human-readable automaton render (to-buchi --dot); not normative, debugging only Phase 6 (## 18): dot emitter in tl-automata/src/hoa.rs; used throughout Phase 13 (## 25) for counterexample visualization

NNF dualities (normative — nnf() MUST realize exactly these):

¬⊤ = ⊥        ¬⊥ = ⊤        ¬¬φ = φ
¬(φ∧ψ) = ¬φ ∨ ¬ψ            ¬(φ∨ψ) = ¬φ ∧ ¬ψ
¬Xφ   = X¬φ                 ¬Fφ = G¬φ        ¬Gφ = F¬φ
¬(φ U ψ) = (¬φ) R (¬ψ)      ¬(φ R ψ) = (¬φ) U (¬ψ)
¬Yφ   = Z¬φ (weak-prev)     ¬Oφ = H¬φ        ¬Hφ = O¬φ
¬(φ S ψ) = (¬φ) T (¬ψ)      (past-release dual)
¬EXφ  = AX¬φ   ¬EGφ = AF¬φ   ¬EFφ = AG¬φ      ¬AXφ = EX¬φ

Acceptance correspondence (HOA ↔ our types): Acceptance: 1 Inf(0) ⇔ Nba.accept (single set); Acceptance: k Inf(0)&…&Inf(k-1) ⇔ Ngba.accept (k sets, one per U). degeneralize maps the k-set HOA generalized form to the 1-set Büchi form via the counter construction — verify round-trip Ngba → HOA → parse preserves accept.len().

6. Design Principles

Non-negotiable invariants for the tl engine. Each principle states a rule, its tradeoff + operational implication, and a concrete failure mode if violated. Later phases (## 12–## 25) reference these by name. Ordered by blast radius: a violation of P1–P5 silently flips a verdict; P6–P15 degrade correctness, performance, or trust.

6.1 P1 — NNF first

  • Rule. Every consumer of a Formula past tl-syntax assumes negation normal form: nnf(f) runs once, negation appears only on Atom(Ap). GPVW (ltl_to_ngba), the labeling CtlChecker, eval_ltlf, and progress are written for the NNF operator set only.
  • Why. GPVW splits obligations into now/next over the subformula closure; the labeling algorithm derives EF/AG/AF/AX/A[U] by duality from EX/EU/EG. Both are unsound on arbitrary nested Not.
  • Tradeoff / op implication. nnf doubles the operator set actually used (R partners U, H/O partner past, Ax/Ag/Af/Au partner the E*); cost = one O(|f|) rewrite, paid once, cached on the AST. closure(f) is computed after nnf.
  • Violation breaks. Feed Not(U(p,q)) un-normalized to GPVW → it has no Not(Binary) case → either panics (P15) or, worse, treats it as a fresh atom and builds an automaton for the wrong language → check_ltl reports holds on a violating model. No crash, flipped verdict.

6.2 P2 — One operator set, exhaustive match

  • Rule. The Formula enum (24 variants: True/False/Atom, 3 boolean, 5 future LTL, 4 past, 8 CTL) is the only AST. Every transformation/evaluator matches it exhaustively — no _ => wildcard arm that swallows a new variant.
  • Why. Adding an operator (e.g. a stretch MTL op) must force a compile error in every crate that must handle it; a wildcard hides the gap.
  • Tradeoff. More match arms vs. compile-time totality. Worth it: the gravest bug class is silent.
  • Violation breaks. nnf with a _ => f.clone() arm leaves a future W (weak-until) un-normalized; downstream GPVW mis-handles it; verdict flips with no test failure until differential testing (P3) — if it covers that pattern.

6.3 P3 — Spot is the oracle

  • Rule. Every automaton and every model-checking verdict is differential-tested against Spot (ltl2tgba) — Phase 13 (## 25) is the done gate. Compare on standard LTL pattern formulas (Dwyer patterns) + randomly generated formulas: automaton language equivalence (via product-emptiness in both directions) and check_ltl holds/counterexample agreement.
  • Why. GPVW, degeneralize, product, and nested-DFS emptiness each silently flip verdicts when buggy; no internal assertion catches a wrong-but-consistent automaton. Spot is the trusted reference (twa, ltl2tgba).
  • Tradeoff. Build/CI dependency on an external C++ tool + HOA parsing vs. ground truth. Mandatory: there is no self-checking substitute.
  • Op implication. to-buchi emits HOA so Spot can read our automaton; we read Spot's HOA back for L-equivalence checks. A formula where we and Spot disagree is a release blocker.
  • Violation breaks. Ship without the Spot oracle → a degeneralize counter-construction off-by-one (visits k−1 of k accept sets) accepts a language slightly too large → some check_ltl queries miss real counterexamples in production, undetected.

6.4 P4 — Emptiness = nested DFS; get the coloring right

  • Rule. emptiness(a: &Nba) -> Option<Lasso> is the Courcoubetis–Vardi–Wolper–Yannakakis nested DFS, exactly two colors per state with disjoint meaning: outer-DFS visited (state explored once, blue) and inner-DFS on_cycle_search (seen during the seed's back-edge hunt, red). Inner DFS is launched only on outer-DFS post-order from an accepting state, hunts a path back to that seed.
  • Why. Correctness of the whole automata-theoretic LTL check (Phase 8, ## 20) reduces to this coloring: a reachable accepting cycle ⇔ L(product) ≠ ∅ ⇔ counterexample.
  • Tradeoff. Two-bit-per-state coloring + strict launch-on-post-order ordering vs. naive SCC enumeration. Nested DFS is O(states+edges), on-the-fly compatible (P5), and yields the lasso directly.
  • Pitfall. Launching inner DFS in pre-order, or sharing one color set, or not seeding from every accepting state, each yields false-empty (misses a cycle) or false-nonempty.
  • Violation breaks. Reuse one visited set for both DFS layers → inner DFS stops at an outer-visited node → never closes a real cycle → emptiness returns None → check_ltl returns Ok(()) for a model that violates the spec. Flipped verdict, no crash.

6.5 P5 — On-the-fly, lazily

  • Rule. GPVW builds Ngba nodes lazily; the product for emptiness is built lazily during the nested DFS (Phase 12, ## 24) — successor states/edges materialized only when the DFS first reaches them. Never enumerate the full automaton or full product if emptiness resolves early.
  • Why. Ngba size is worst-case 2^O(|f|); product = |Nba| × |Kripke|. A counterexample often sits a few transitions in; full construction wastes the win.
  • Tradeoff. Lazy successor closures + memoized node cache vs. simpler eager arrays; harder to debug. Pays off: bounded peak memory, early exit on the first accepting lasso.
  • Op implication. State-construction is a pure function of a node's obligation set → memoizable (P7) → identical nodes deduped, never rebuilt.
  • Violation breaks. Eagerly materialize product(Nba(¬φ), K) for a 10⁶-state Kripke before any DFS → OOM on a model whose counterexample was reachable in 3 steps → the bounded-size error (P6) misfires and the tool dies instead of answering.

6.6 P6 — Explicit-state, bounded

  • Rule. This engine is explicit-state / automata + trace based — not BDD-symbolic (symbolic/BDD model checking is project #281; non-goal here). Every unbounded structure (Ngba nodes, Nba states, product states, DFS stack depth) is capped by a configurable budget; exceeding it returns a typed error (ParseError/eval error variant), never a hang or OOM.
  • Why. Explicit enumeration is fast and cache-friendly on small/medium formulas and models but blows up on large ones; a hard cap converts blowup into a clean, reportable failure.
  • Tradeoff. A cap can reject a checkable-but-large instance (false "too big") vs. unbounded runs that exhaust the host. Bounded loses some completeness, gains predictability. Contrast #281: symbolic absorbs large state spaces but is out of scope.
  • Quantified defaults. Ngba nodes ≤ 2¹⁶; product states ≤ 2²⁴; nested-DFS stack ≤ 2²⁰; formula size |f| ≤ 2¹⁶ subformulas. All overridable on tl flags.
  • Violation breaks. No product-state cap → a pathological φ + large Kripke spins building states until the OS kills tl with no diagnostic → caller cannot distinguish "no answer" from "crashed".

6.7 P7 — Determinism: same formula → same automaton

  • Rule. ltl_to_ngba, degeneralize, product are deterministic pure functions of their inputs: byte-identical Ngba/Nba (state numbering, edge order, accept-set order) for the same Formula. No hashing with random seeds, no iteration over an unordered map without a sort key.
  • Why. Differential testing (P3), golden HOA fixtures, reproducible bug reports, and stable benchmarks all require determinism. Lasso counterexamples must reproduce.
  • Tradeoff. Ordered/BTreeMap or explicit canonical sort vs. faster randomized HashMap. Determinism wins for a verification tool.
  • Violation breaks. Use a HashMap<ObligationSet, usize> with default RandomState for node numbering → state IDs differ per run → a golden-HOA diff in CI flaps, a user's counterexample lasso is non-reproducible, and bisecting a verdict regression becomes impossible.

6.8 P8 — Progression is the monitor

  • Rule. Runtime monitoring (Phase 9, ## 21) and finite-trace eval_ltlf (Phase 4, ## 16) are built on one operation: progress(f, s) -> Formula, the LTL derivative. Monitor::step folds progress over the event stream; eval_ltlf folds over a finite Trace. The recurrences are fixed: progress(X f,s)=f; progress(f U g,s)=progress(g,s) ∨ (progress(f,s) ∧ (f U g)); progress(G f,s)=progress(f,s) ∧ G f; progress(Atom p,s)=True/False by s.
  • Why. One residual-rewriting engine, two clients → no second semantics to keep in sync; a residual of True/False is a definitive verdict.
  • Tradeoff. Residual formulas can grow without simplification (P9 / Phase 12 rewrite caps this) vs. a bespoke monitor automaton. Progression is incremental, exact, and shares the AST.
  • Op implication. eval_ltlf at end-of-trace forces the weak/strong-next distinction on finite words; the same progress plus an end-of-trace closing rule must yield it.
  • Violation breaks. Re-implement finite-trace eval_ltlf with a separate ad-hoc recursion instead of progress → it disagrees with Monitor on the boundary case X p at the last state (weak vs strong next) → a stream verdict and a batch verdict differ for the identical trace.

6.9 P9 — Residuals stay bounded: simplify aggressively

  • Rule. After every progress step and inside GPVW node construction, apply the rewrite/simplification rules (Phase 12, ## 24): True ∧ x → x, False ∨ x → x, idempotence, absorption, and temporal identities (F F p → F p, G G p → G p). Canonicalize commutative And/Or (sorted operands) so structurally equal residuals are identical.
  • Why. Without simplification a long monitored stream grows the residual Formula unboundedly; canonical form is also what makes the Ngba node cache (P5/P7) actually deduplicate.
  • Tradeoff. Rewrite cost per step vs. exploding residual size and a useless cache. Bounded simplification is cheap and amortized.
  • Violation breaks. Skip canonical sorting of And operands → progress produces p ∧ q one step and q ∧ p the next as distinct residuals → monitor residual size grows linearly with trace length, GPVW builds duplicate nodes, and a 10⁶-event stream OOMs.

6.10 P10 — Every NO yields a lasso

  • Rule. Whenever check_ltl reports a violation it returns Err(Lasso) with stem + cycle over KripkeStructure state indices; emptiness returning Some(Lasso) is the only source. An accepting run in product(Nba(¬φ), K) is the counterexample; the lasso is projected back onto Kripke states.
  • Why. A bare "does not hold" is unusable; users need the witnessing infinite (lasso-shaped) word. This mirrors the Trace lasso model (finite prefix + loop_start).
  • Tradeoff. Tracking DFS parent pointers to reconstruct stem+cycle (extra memory) vs. emptiness-only boolean. Counterexamples are the product's reason to exist.
  • Op implication. The returned Lasso must be replayable: feeding it as a Trace to eval_ltlf/Monitor reproduces the violation — a self-check tied to P8.
  • Violation breaks. Return Err(()) with no lasso → user cannot see why the spec fails, cannot validate it against the model, and cannot regression-test the fix; also forfeits the replay self-check.

6.11 P11 — Honest 3/4-valued verdicts; Unknown is a real answer

  • Rule. Monitoring returns RvVerdict with five inhabitants (True, False, PresumablyTrue, PresumablyFalse, Unknown). Unknown (LTL3: prefix is neither good nor bad) and the RV-LTL "Presumably" tie-breaks are first-class results, never coerced to True/False. Definitive Verdict::{True,False} is emitted only on a good/bad prefix.
  • Why. Over a finite observed prefix, soundness forbids a definitive verdict unless every infinite extension agrees (good prefix → True) or none does (bad prefix → False). Forcing a 2-valued answer is unsound.
  • Tradeoff. Three/four-valued API is more to handle for callers vs. a misleading binary. RV correctness demands it.
  • Op implication. Monitorability classification (Phase 9) tells the user whether any future prefix can still reach a definitive verdict; a non-monitorable property stuck at Unknown forever must be reported as such, not silently optimistic.
  • Violation breaks. Collapse Unknown → False to fit a boolean API → monitor declares a violation on a still-undecided prefix of G F p (liveness, never decidable on a finite prefix) → false alarm fires on every correct run.

6.12 P12 — State formula vs path formula is type-enforced

  • Rule. CTL well-formedness (Kind::Ctl, Phase 2, ## 14) is checked before evaluation: every temporal operator is immediately fused with a path quantifier (Ex/Ax/Eg/Ag/Ef/Af/Eu/Au); a bare X/U/R/F/G under CTL Kind is a ParseError. CtlChecker::sat only accepts CTL state formulas; ltl_to_ngba only accepts LTL path formulas.
  • Why. CTL is a state logic, LTL a path logic (CTL* is the superset, out of scope). The labeling algorithm computes per-state truth (Vec<bool>); it is undefined on a bare path formula.
  • Tradeoff. A Kind classification pass + rejection of mixed formulas vs. a permissive parser. Rejecting early prevents a category error from reaching an evaluator that cannot represent it.
  • Violation breaks. Let E[X(p U q)]-style un-fused mixing through to CtlChecker → labeling has no sat case for a path subformula → either panic (P15) or a meaningless per-state vector → wrong holds.

6.13 P13 — EG via SCCs, EU via least fixpoint — and nothing else

  • Rule. CtlChecker computes exactly three primitives over KripkeStructure: sat(EX p) = preimage (states with a successor in sat(p)); sat(E[p U q]) = least fixpoint (seed sat(q), add p-states with a successor already in the set); sat(EG p) = greatest fixpoint = p-states on a cycle within the sat(p) sub-Kripke, via SCC decomposition. EF/AG/AF/AX/A[U] are derived by duality, never reimplemented.
  • Why. Three correct fixpoints + duality is the minimal trusted core; reimplementing the duals invites divergence between, e.g., AG p and ¬EF ¬p.
  • Tradeoff. Indirection through duals (a few extra nnf-style rewrites) vs. eight independent labelers. One source of truth per direction.
  • Pitfall. EG p must restrict the cycle search to the sat(p) sub-Kripke; running SCCs on the full structure admits cycles through ¬p states.
  • Violation breaks. Compute EG p on full-structure SCCs → label a state EG p whose only cycle passes through a ¬p state → holds(AG q) (= ¬EF ¬q = ¬E[true U ¬q], sharing the EU core) and EG-based answers diverge from Spot.

6.14 P14 — Bitset valuations, arena automata, zero-copy Guards

  • Rule. State.aps is a Bitset (one bit per AP); Guard is a boolean over APs as a bitset mask+care (small DNF), so edge-vs-state matching is bitwise (Phase 12 symbolic guards). Ngba/Nba states are arena-indexed usize (edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard, usize)>>) — no Box/Rc per state, no per-node allocation in hot loops.
  • Why. Nested DFS + product are pointer/graph-heavy and hot; bitset masking and arena indices are cache-friendly and bound per-step cost to O(words-per-AP-set).
  • Tradeoff. Manual index bookkeeping (dangling-index risk) vs. Box<Node> ergonomics. Rust's bounds checks + arena ownership keep it safe; the speed is required to hit benchmark targets.
  • Quantified. AP bitset ≤ 1 word for ≤ 64 APs; guard match = one &/compare; target: GPVW + emptiness on |f| ≤ 30 LTL pattern formulas in < 100 ms.
  • Violation breaks. Represent State.aps as Vec<String> of AP names → guard matching becomes string-set intersection per edge per DFS step → product emptiness on a moderate Kripke is 100–1000× slower, missing every benchmark target.

6.15 P15 — Errors, never panic on untrusted formulas

  • Rule. tl-syntax returns Result<Formula, ParseError> (with ParseErrorKind); evaluators/automata return typed Result/Option (check_ltl -> Result<(), Lasso>, emptiness -> Option<Lasso>). No unwrap/expect/panic!/array-index-panic on any path reachable from tl input or fuzzing. Resource exhaustion is the typed cap error (P6), not abort.
  • Why. tl consumes arbitrary user formulas, traces, and Kripke files; Phase 13 fuzzes the parser, automaton, and eval. A panic on untrusted input is a denial-of-service and an unrecoverable library failure for embedders.
  • Tradeoff. Threading Result through parsing/construction (more boilerplate) vs. terse unwrap. Robustness is mandatory for a verification tool others embed.
  • Op implication. Fuzz targets (Phase 13) assert no panic on any byte input; a discovered panic is a release blocker alongside P3 disagreements.
  • Violation breaks. Parser tokens[i+1] indexes past end on a truncated formula like p U → index panic → fuzzing flags it, and any service embedding tl-eval crashes on a single malformed user query instead of returning a ParseError.

7. System Invariants

Cross-cutting properties that MUST hold at all times. Each is a contract spanning ≥1 crate; violation = correctness bug (silent verdict flip is the gravest, per spine KEY FAILURE CLASS). Notation: phi=input formula; models(phi)=set of ω-words satisfying phi; L(A)=language of automaton A; traces(K)=ω-words generated by infinite paths of KripkeStructure K. Invariants are tagged INV-1..INV-15; the differential oracle is Spot ltl2tgba (Phase 13, ## 25). "Detection" = how a violation surfaces in test/prod; "Telemetry" = the counter/log emitted; "Recovery" = response (none for logic bugs — they are not recoverable at runtime, only via fix + regression fixture).

7.1 Invariant catalog (summary table)

ID Invariant (one-line) Enforced in Class
INV-1 nnf(f) is logically equivalent to f: models(nnf(f)) == models(f) tl-formula::nnf semantic-preserving
INV-2 nnf(f) output has Not only on Atom (no nested negation) tl-formula::nnf structural
INV-3 closure(f) is finite, deduplicated, and contains every subformula + its NNF-dual eventuality witnesses tl-formula::closure structural
INV-4 GPVW ltl_to_ngba(f): L(Ngba) == models(f) (sound + complete tableau) tl-automata::ltl_to_ngba semantic-preserving
INV-5 degeneralize(g): L(Nba) == L(Ngba) (counter construction language-preserving) tl-automata::degeneralize semantic-preserving
INV-6 product(a,k): L(Nba_out) == L(a) ∩ traces(k) (synchronous intersection) tl-automata::product semantic-preserving
INV-7 emptiness(a) returns Some(lasso) IFF an accepting run of a exists — verdict soundness (GRAVEST) tl-automata::emptiness (nested DFS) sound+complete decision
INV-8 A reported Lasso{stem,cycle} re-simulates to a real accepting run / genuine counterexample tl-automata::emptiness + tl-eval::check_ltl witness validity
INV-9 CTL labeling fixpoints (EU lfp, EG gfp) are monotone and terminate in ≤ ` states ` iterations
INV-10 Formula progression preserves trace semantics: eval_ltlf(f,t) via progress == direct LTLf semantics of f on t tl-monitor::progress + tl-eval::eval_ltlf semantic-preserving
INV-11 LTL3 verdict stability: once step returns True/False, no later event flips it tl-monitor::Monitor::step monotone (definitive)
INV-12 Automaton/product sizes respect the configured cap → typed Error, never OOM tl-automata (all builders) resource bound
INV-13 check_ltl self-consistency: Ok(()) IFF emptiness(product(Nba(nnf(Not phi)),k)).is_none() tl-eval::check_ltl composition
INV-14 Kind classification is stable + a CTL Formula never contains bare path ops as state formulas tl-formula well-formedness structural
INV-15 Trace lasso well-formed (loop_start < prefix.len()); KripkeStructure transition relation total (no dead state) tl-trace validation structural precondition

7.2 INV-1 — nnf preserves language

  • Statement: models(nnf(f)) == models(f) for every Formula f and every ω-word.
  • Why: GPVW (Phase 6, ## 18) and the closure-based labeling assume NNF/a fixed positive operator set; if nnf changed semantics, every downstream automaton + verdict would be wrong against the user's actual formula.
  • Dualities applied (LOCKED): not X g = X(not g); not(p U q) = (not p) R (not q); not(p R q) = (not p) U (not q); not F g = G(not g); not G g = F(not g); not(EX g)=AX(not g); not(EG g)=AF(not g); not(EF g)=AG(not g); past: not(O g)=H(not g), not(p S q)=... (released-past dual); De Morgan on And/Or; double-negation elimination.
  • Where enforced: tl-formula::nnf (Phase 1, ## 13).
  • Detection: property test — random f, assert models(f) ⟺ models(nnf(f)) decided two ways: (a) for LTLf, eval_ltlf(f,t)==eval_ltlf(nnf(f),t) over a fixed random Trace corpus (10⁵ traces, |prefix|≤32); (b) for LTL, L(ltl_to_ngba(f)) == L(ltl_to_ngba(nnf(f))) via product-emptiness equivalence check vs Spot. Idempotence check: nnf(nnf(f)) == nnf(f).
  • Telemetry: tl_nnf_mismatch_total (test-only counter), logs the offending f (pretty-printed) on failure.
  • Recovery: none at runtime; failure is a fixture-promoted regression in tl-formula/tests/nnf_equiv.rs.
  • Debugging method: shrink the failing f (smallest subformula that diverges), diff nnf(f) against a hand-computed dual table; bisect on operator (push the negation one level, re-check).

7.3 INV-2 — nnf structural normal form

  • Statement: in nnf(f), every Not(_) node wraps exactly an Atom(Ap); True/False are not under Not.
  • Why: the GPVW "now" obligation split treats negated atoms as edge Guard literals; a non-atomic Not would not be a literal and would break guard construction.
  • Where enforced: tl-formula::nnf postcondition; a debug_assert! walker is_nnf(&Formula) -> bool.
  • Detection: is_nnf(nnf(f)) asserted on every property-test sample + every parsed CLI input in debug builds.
  • Telemetry: tl_non_nnf_node_total; node path logged.
  • Recovery: none (logic bug). Debugging: AST-walk dump locating the non-atomic Not.

7.4 INV-3 — closure finiteness + completeness

  • Statement: closure(f) is finite (≤ 2·|f| distinct elements, |f|=AST node count), deduplicated (no element twice), and contains every subformula of f plus the X-successor obligations that GPVW needs (p U q ⟹ X(p U q) witness, G g ⟹ X(G g), F g ⟹ X(F g)).
  • Why: GPVW node sets and CTL labeling index subformulas by closure membership; a missing element → an unsatisfiable lookup → panic or wrong obligation set; a non-dedup closure → exponential node blowup (intersects INV-12).
  • Where enforced: tl-formula::closure (Phase 1/6).
  • Detection: test len(closure(f)) <= 2*node_count(f); assert each subformula present (recursive descent cross-check); assert no duplicates (HashSet size == Vec len).
  • Telemetry: tl_closure_size{formula_id} histogram (target p99 ≤ 1.5·|f|).
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: dump closure as a sorted list, diff vs recursive-descent enumeration.

7.5 INV-4 — GPVW Ngba accepts exactly models(phi)

  • Statement: L(ltl_to_ngba(f)) == models(f). Soundness (no over-acceptance) and completeness (no under-acceptance).
  • Why: THE central correctness contract of the automata-theoretic path (Phase 6, ## 18); an over- or under-accepting Ngba silently flips check_ltl verdicts with no crash — co-gravest with INV-7.
  • Where enforced: tl-automata::ltl_to_ngba; guaranteed by correct (a) "now/next" obligation split, (b) one generalized acceptance set per U-subformula forcing its right side eventually (so a state cannot loop forever owing an eventuality).
  • Detection: differential vs Spot ltl2tgba on (i) the standard LTL pattern formulas (Dwyer patterns: absence/existence/universality/response/precedence) and (ii) 10⁴ random formulas (depth ≤ 8, ≤ 5 APs): compare languages by checking L(ours) ⊆ L(spot) and L(spot) ⊆ L(ours) via product-emptiness (L(A)\L(B)=∅ iff product(A, complement(B)) empty) and by sampling 10³ random lassos per formula and comparing membership.
  • Telemetry: tl_ngba_states{formula_id}, tl_ngba_accept_sets{formula_id} (= number of U-subformulas), tl_gpvw_diff_failures_total.
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: minimize the diverging formula; for the smallest, dump the Ngba (HOA, to-buchi --hoa) beside Spot's; find the first lasso accepted by one and not the other; trace its obligation sets node-by-node (each node = closure subset) to locate the missing/extra eventuality acceptance.

7.6 INV-5 — degeneralization preserves language

  • Statement: L(degeneralize(g)) == L(g). The counter construction over g.accept (k accept sets) on state-space (state × counter∈0..k) yields an Nba whose single accept set is hit infinitely often iff all k Ngba sets are hit infinitely often.
  • Why: Phase 7 (## 19) feeds the product + nested DFS, which need a single Buchi acceptance condition; a counter-advance bug (e.g. resetting at the wrong set, or k=0 empty-accept-set handling) flips membership of cyclic words.
  • Where enforced: tl-automata::degeneralize; invariant: counter advances i → (i+1) mod k exactly when a transition's source is in g.accept[i]; accept = states with counter k-1 taking such a transition. Edge case: k==0 (no eventualities, e.g. G p) → every run accepting → Nba accept = all states.
  • Detection: for random g from GPVW, assert L(degeneralize(g)) == L(g) by lasso-sampling membership equivalence (10³ lassos) + emptiness equivalence (emptiness(degeneralize(g)).is_some() == ngba_nonempty(g) where ngba_nonempty uses a direct generalized nested DFS as an independent oracle).
  • Telemetry: tl_nba_states == tl_ngba_states * max(k,1) sanity bound logged; tl_degen_diff_failures_total.
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: check k=0 and k=1 first (degenerate fast paths), then trace counter values along a mis-accepted cycle.

7.7 INV-6 — product language = intersection

  • Statement: L(product(a,k)) == L(a) ∩ traces(k). Synchronous product: product accepts an ω-word w iff a accepts w and w labels some infinite path of KripkeStructure k.
  • Why: Phase 8 (## 20); the model check is K ⊨ A phi ⟺ L(product(Nba(not phi),K)) = ∅. Wrong synchronization (label/guard mismatch, or accepting on the wrong component) breaks the whole check_ltl equivalence (INV-13).
  • Where enforced: tl-automata::product; product state (q_a, s_k); edge (q_a,s_k)→(q'_a,s'_k) exists iff (Guard,q'_a)∈a.edges[q_a], s'_k∈k.edges[s_k], and Guard is satisfied by k.states[s_k]'s AP bitset (guard evaluated on the source Kripke state's labeling — state-labeled Kripke convention, LOCKED); product accept inherits a.accept (Kripke has no acceptance — all its infinite paths are "accepting"/non-terminating by INV-15 totality).
  • Detection: unit fixtures with hand-computed intersections (2–3 state automata × 2–3 state Kripke); property test: sample lassos accepted by product, verify each is (a) accepted by a (re-simulate guards) and (b) a valid k path (consecutive states in k.edges).
  • Telemetry: tl_product_states (target ≤ a.nstates * k.states.len()), tl_product_reachable_states.
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: dump the product as a graph (dot), pick a disputed word, walk both projections.

7.8 INV-7 — nested DFS soundness (GRAVEST)

  • Statement: emptiness(a) returns Some(_) IFF a has an accepting run (a reachable cycle through an accept state). Equivalently L(a)≠∅ ⟺ emptiness(a).is_some(). Both directions: no false "empty" (would report holds when a counterexample exists) and no false "nonempty" (would invent a counterexample).
  • Why: this is the spine's GRAVEST failure class — a buggy nested DFS silently flips the final verdict with no crash. Every LTL model-check answer rests here.
  • Where enforced: tl-automata::emptiness (Courcoubetis-Vardi-Wolper-Yannakakis nested DFS): outer DFS colors states, on backtracking from an accept state launches an inner DFS seeking a path back to that accept state; a hit = accepting cycle. Critical sub-invariants: (a) inner-DFS color set is shared across inner searches (the CVWY optimization) and never reset between outer-discovered accept states — resetting → exponential blowup but still sound; failing to share but also failing to re-explore → unsoundness; (b) a self-loop on an accept state counts (cycle length 1); (c) the inner DFS must detect reaching the specific seed accept state, not any accept state.
  • Detection: (1) differential vs Spot on all of INV-4's corpus at the emptiness level (L=∅?); (2) independent oracle: an SCC-based emptiness (Tarjan SCC → accepting iff some non-trivial SCC, or a self-loop, contains an accept state) cross-checked against nested DFS on 10⁵ random Nba (≤ 50 states, random accept sets) — both must agree on every instance; (3) targeted adversarial fixtures: nested accepting cycles, accept state only reachable via a long stem, accept state on a cycle disjoint from init (must report empty), interleaved accept states sharing inner colors.
  • Telemetry: tl_ndfs_outer_visited, tl_ndfs_inner_visited, tl_ndfs_disagreements_total (vs SCC oracle — MUST stay 0), tl_emptiness_result{empty|nonempty}.
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: on an oracle disagreement, dump the Nba (HOA), run both algorithms with state-color tracing, replay the outer/inner stacks; the divergence is almost always (a) an inner-color reset bug or (b) a missed self-loop. Keep the disagreeing automaton as a permanent fixture.

7.9 INV-8 — reported lasso re-simulates to a genuine counterexample

  • Statement: a returned Lasso{stem, cycle} (state index sequences) satisfies: (a) stem starts at an init state, consecutive indices respect product edges; (b) cycle is a non-empty closed path (last → first edge exists) containing ≥1 accept state; (c) re-simulated, the underlying ω-word stem·cycle^ω is accepted by Nba(nnf(Not phi)) and is a real infinite path of K — i.e. it is an actual run violating A phi.
  • Why: a counterexample the user cannot reproduce on K is worse than none (erodes trust, masks the bug). The witness must be independently checkable.
  • Where enforced: produced by tl-automata::emptiness; validated by tl-eval::check_ltl before returning Err(lasso) — re-simulate the lasso's Kripke projection against K.edges, evaluate nnf(Not phi) (or run eval of phi) on the lasso word and assert it is FALSE for phi.
  • Detection: every check_ltl Err path runs the re-simulation assertion in debug + a sampled 1% in release; property test: for random K,phi where a counterexample is found, assert the lasso word fails phi.
  • Telemetry: tl_lasso_len{stem,cycle} histograms, tl_lasso_resim_failures_total (MUST be 0).
  • Recovery: if re-simulation fails (INV-8 broken) the result is suppressed and a typed Error::InternalWitnessInvalid is raised rather than returning a bogus counterexample — fail loud, never lie.
  • Debugging: print the lasso as (state, AP-set) pairs; check the cycle back-edge first (most common: off-by-one in cycle extraction from the inner-DFS stack), then the accept-state-on-cycle condition.

7.10 INV-9 — CTL fixpoints monotone + terminating

  • Statement: sat(E[p U q]) is computed as a least fixpoint of a monotone (⊆-increasing) operator and sat(EG p) as a greatest fixpoint of a monotone (⊆-decreasing) operator over the finite lattice 2^states; both terminate in ≤ |states| iterations and return the exact fixpoint.
  • Why: Phase 5 (## 17). Non-monotone iteration could oscillate (non-termination) or settle on a non-fixpoint (wrong labeling); CTL holds would then be wrong with no error.
  • Where enforced: tl-eval::CtlChecker::sat. EU: start S₀ = sat(q); Sᵢ₊₁ = Sᵢ ∪ {s∈sat(p) : ∃ succ ∈ Sᵢ}; monotone-increasing, bounded by states, stop when Sᵢ₊₁==Sᵢ. EG: restrict Kripke to sat(p) sub-structure, sat(EG p) = states on a cycle in that sub-structure (computed via SCC quotient — non-trivial SCC or self-loop) — equivalently the gfp of Sᵢ₊₁ = sat(p) ∩ {s : ∃ succ ∈ Sᵢ}; monotone-decreasing.
  • Detection: assert iteration count ≤ |states| (a hard cap that, if hit without convergence, raises Error::FixpointDiverged — should be unreachable, guards against a non-monotone regression); assert successive sets are strictly monotone in the expected direction (Sᵢ ⊆ Sᵢ₊₁ for lfp); cross-check EG SCC method vs the iterative gfp on random Kripke (must agree).
  • Telemetry: tl_ctl_fixpoint_iters{op,formula_id} (target ≤ diameter of K), tl_ctl_eg_scc_count.
  • Recovery: none (the cap is a tripwire, not a recovery). Debugging: log each iteration's set delta; a non-shrinking EG or non-growing EU delta of unexpected sign localizes the bug to the preimage step.

7.11 INV-10 — progression preserves trace semantics

  • Statement: eval_ltlf(f, t) (folding progress across t.prefix then reading the residual at end-of-trace) equals the direct LTLf truth of f on the finite Trace t.
  • Why: Phase 4 (## 16) + Phase 9 (## 21); the monitor and the finite-trace checker both rest on progression. A wrong recurrence flips every monitor verdict.
  • Recurrences (LOCKED): progress(atom p, s) = True/False by s; progress(X f, s) = f (strong next; at end-of-trace a still-pending strong X/U resolves to False, weak R/G-pending resolves to True — the weak/strong-next finite distinction); progress(f U g, s) = progress(g,s) ∨ (progress(f,s) ∧ (f U g)); progress(f R g, s)=progress(g,s) ∧ (progress(f,s) ∨ (f R g)); progress(G f, s)=progress(f,s) ∧ G f; progress(F f, s)=progress(f,s) ∨ F f; boolean ops distribute.
  • Where enforced: tl-monitor::Monitor::progress + tl-eval::eval_ltlf (which calls it).
  • Detection: differential vs a direct recursive LTLf semantics evaluator (independent implementation, tests/ltlf_direct.rs) on 10⁵ random (f, t) pairs (|prefix| ≤ 24); plus the end-of-trace resolution table tested per operator.
  • Telemetry: tl_progress_residual_size (must stay bounded — see INV-12 for monitor blowup), tl_ltlf_diff_failures_total.
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: step the fold state-by-state, print the residual Formula after each event, diff against the direct evaluator's per-suffix truth; the first diverging step pinpoints the faulty recurrence (usually the U/R end-of-trace tie-break).

7.12 INV-11 — LTL3 verdict stability (monotone definitiveness)

  • Statement: for a Monitor over LTL3/RV-LTL, once step returns a definitive RvVerdict::True or RvVerdict::False, every subsequent step returns the same definitive verdict — definitive verdicts are absorbing; only Unknown/PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse may change.
  • Why: Phase 9 (## 21). A definitive LTL3 verdict means every infinite extension (good prefix → True) or no extension (bad prefix → False) satisfies phi; later events cannot change that. A flip would mean the good/bad-prefix detection was wrong — a soundness break in monitoring.
  • Where enforced: tl-monitor::Monitor::step; once the progressed residual reduces to True (→ good prefix, RvVerdict::True) or False (→ bad prefix, RvVerdict::False), the monitor enters a sink state and short-circuits all further step calls.
  • Detection: property test — random phi, random event stream; assert verdict sequence is monotone (no True→¬True, no False→¬False); cross-check definitive verdicts against the monitor automaton's accepting/rejecting sink classification (LTL3 monitor built from the Nba of phi and of not phi).
  • Telemetry: tl_monitor_verdict_flips_total (MUST be 0 for definitive verdicts), tl_monitor_steps_to_verdict histogram, tl_monitor_sink_reached_total.
  • Recovery: none — a flip is a logic bug. Debugging: replay the event stream, log (event, residual, verdict) per step; the step where a definitive verdict was emitted but later contradicted reveals a premature True/False reduction (usually an over-eager simplification rule mis-detecting a good/bad prefix).

7.13 INV-12 — size caps → typed error, never OOM

  • Statement: every automaton/product/monitor builder respects a configured cap (default MAX_STATES = 1<<20 per automaton; MAX_PRODUCT_STATES configurable) and returns a typed Error::SizeCapExceeded{which, cap, reached} rather than exhausting memory.
  • Why: GPVW is worst-case 2^O(|phi|) and the product multiplies by |K|; an unbounded run OOM-kills the tl process (DoS / unusable on large inputs). A typed error lets the CLI report cleanly and the caller raise the cap deliberately.
  • Where enforced: tl-automata::ltl_to_ngba, degeneralize, product, and tl-monitor automaton synthesis — each checks state_count against the cap on every state allocation (arena-allocated states, checked before push).
  • Detection: fixtures with adversarial formulas (deeply nested U/X, e.g. F G F G ... p and conjunctions of n distinct fairness constraints) asserting the typed error fires at the cap, not an abort; a #[should_panic]-free test confirming graceful Err.
  • Telemetry: tl_size_cap_hits_total{which}, tl_max_states_reached{which} gauge (alert if approaching cap on routine inputs).
  • Recovery: caller-actionable — the CLI prints the cap + reached count + the offending construction stage and suggests --max-states; the build aborts cleanly with a nonzero exit, releasing the arena. This is the one invariant with a real runtime recovery path (bounded failure, not a logic bug).
  • Debugging: log the construction's state-growth curve; if growth is anomalous for a small formula, suspect a closure dedup failure (INV-3) or an Ngba node-merging miss (Phase 7 simplification not firing).

7.14 INV-13 — check_ltl composition consistency

  • Statement: check_ltl(f, k) returns Ok(()) IFF emptiness(product(degeneralize(ltl_to_ngba(nnf(Not f))), k)).is_none(); and returns Err(lasso) carrying exactly the lasso emptiness produced (after INV-8 validation).
  • Why: Phase 8 (## 20) ties INV-4/5/6/7/8 into the user-facing answer; this invariant pins the exact composition so a refactor cannot silently reorder/skip a stage (e.g. forgetting nnf or negating the wrong operand).
  • Where enforced: tl-eval::check_ltl — the single LOCKED pipeline nnf ∘ Not → ltl_to_ngba → degeneralize → product(·,k) → emptiness.
  • Detection: end-to-end differential vs Spot's model-checking verdict on the Dwyer pattern corpus + random (f,k); an internal cross-check on a 1% sample re-runs the pipeline with stages independently and compares.
  • Telemetry: tl_check_ltl_holds_total, tl_check_ltl_counterexample_total, tl_check_ltl_diff_failures_total (vs Spot).
  • Recovery: none. Debugging: on a Spot disagreement, isolate which stage diverges by comparing intermediate artifacts (Ngba HOA, Nba HOA, product dot) against Spot's pipeline; the first differing artifact localizes the faulty invariant (INV-4/5/6/7).

7.15 INV-14 — CTL state/path well-formedness + stable Kind

  • Statement: a Formula classified Kind::Ctl has every temporal operator immediately fused with a path quantifier (only Ex/Ax/Eg/Ag/Ef/Af/Eu/Au appear — never a bare X/U/R/F/G as a state subformula); Kind classification is deterministic and idempotent (re-classifying a formula yields the same Kind).
  • Why: Phase 2 (## 14). CTL labeling assumes state formulas; a bare path operator inside a CTL formula is ill-typed and would mis-route to the LTL pipeline. The state-vs-path distinction is the CTL well-formedness contract.
  • Where enforced: tl-syntax::Parser (rejects ill-formed CTL with ParseError) + tl-formula Kind classifier + a debug_assert! well-formedness walker before CtlChecker::sat.
  • Detection: parser fixtures rejecting EX (p U q) written as EX p U q ambiguities and bare-path-op CTL; property test: every Kind::Ctl formula passes the well-formedness walker; classify(classify_back(f)) == classify(f) idempotence.
  • Telemetry: tl_parse_errors_total{kind}, tl_kind_misclassification_total (test-only).
  • Recovery: parse-time Err(ParseError) surfaced to the CLI with a span — user-actionable. Debugging: AST dump + Kind annotation per node; a bare path op under a boolean inside a CTL formula localizes the grammar bug.

7.16 INV-15 — model preconditions (Kripke total, lasso well-formed)

  • Statement: (a) KripkeStructure has a total transition relation — every state has ≥1 successor (∀s. !k.edges[s].is_empty()), so every finite path extends to an infinite path (required for ω-semantics + the product's "all Kripke paths accepting" convention in INV-6); (b) a Trace with loop_start = Some(i) satisfies i < prefix.len() (well-formed lasso) and a finite Trace has loop_start = None.
  • Why: Phase 3 (## 15). A dead Kripke state has no infinite path through it, breaking the automata-theoretic correspondence (a "stuck" product run is neither accepting nor a valid counterexample); an out-of-range loop_start panics or fabricates a nonexistent cycle.
  • Where enforced: tl-trace validation — KripkeStructure::validate() (rejects dead states with Error::DeadState{state}) and Trace::validate() (rejects bad loop_start with Error::BadLoopStart), called at construction and at the CLI boundary before check_ltl/eval_ltlf.
  • Detection: validation fixtures (dead state, self-loop-only state OK, loop_start == prefix.len() rejected, empty prefix rejected); property test: every generated KripkeStructure/Trace in the test corpus passes validate() (generators are total by construction; mutation fuzzing flips one edge/index to confirm validate() catches it).
  • Telemetry: tl_kripke_dead_states_total, tl_trace_validation_failures_total.
  • Recovery: user-actionable typed Error at the input boundary (the CLI prints the offending state/index); models are not auto-repaired (silently adding a self-loop would change semantics). Debugging: list states with empty successor lists; for traces, print loop_start vs prefix.len().

7.17 Invariant enforcement strategy

Mechanism Invariants covered When
debug_assert! / postcondition walkers (is_nnf, well-formedness, lasso re-sim) INV-2, INV-8, INV-9 (cap tripwire), INV-14 every debug build + 1% release sampling
Property tests (proptest, random formulas/traces/Kripke) INV-1, INV-3, INV-5, INV-6, INV-10, INV-11, INV-15 CI per commit
Independent oracle cross-check (SCC emptiness; direct LTLf eval; iterative-vs-SCC EG) INV-7, INV-9, INV-10 CI per commit
Differential vs Spot ltl2tgba (HOA/language/verdict) INV-4, INV-7, INV-13 CI nightly + release gate (Phase 13, ## 25)
Typed Error + size guards (no panic/OOM) INV-12, INV-14, INV-15 always (release + debug)
Fixture corpus (Dwyer patterns + every promoted regression) INV-4, INV-7, INV-13 release gate
  • Priority order on conflict: verdict soundness (INV-7) > language-preservation chain (INV-4/5/6/13) > witness validity (INV-8) > semantic preservation (INV-1/10) > resource bounds (INV-12) > structural (INV-2/3/14/15). A violation higher in this order blocks the release; INV-12/15 produce typed errors and are expected on adversarial input.
  • Zero-tolerance counters (any nonzero value fails CI): tl_ndfs_disagreements_total, tl_lasso_resim_failures_total, tl_monitor_verdict_flips_total (definitive), tl_*_diff_failures_total. These guard the spine's GRAVEST silent-verdict-flip class.
  • Telemetry surface: all counters/histograms emitted under the tl_ prefix; in the library they are feature-gated (metrics feature → no overhead when off); the CLI --stats flag dumps the per-run gauges (automaton/product sizes, fixpoint iters, lasso lengths) for capacity debugging against the Phase 13 targets.

8. Domain Model — LTL/CTL Syntax, Automata, and Trace Semantics

This is the formal core: the grammar tl-syntax parses, the semantics tl-eval/tl-monitor implement, the NNF tl-formula produces, the omega-automata tl-automata builds, and the trace/Kripke structures tl-trace defines. Every later phase is an algorithm over these objects; ambiguity here is a verdict-flipping bug (the gravest failure class). All definitions LOCKED.

8.1 Atomic propositions and alphabet

Concept Definition Representation
AP (atomic proposition) named boolean observable, e.g. p, req, ack_2 Ap = u32, interned by tl-syntax
AP universe Σ_AP = { all interned Ap }, size n symbol table; n fixed per formula/model
Letter / valuation a subset of AP that hold simultaneously State { aps: Bitset }, bit i set ⇔ AP i holds
Alphabet Σ 2^Σ_AP — power set of APs (one letter = one valuation) `
omega-word w ∈ Σ^ω, infinite sequence w = w₀ w₁ w₂ …, each wᵢ ⊆ AP lasso-encoded Trace (§8.7)
finite word u ∈ Σ⁺, u = u₀ … u_{m-1}, length m ≥ 1 Trace with loop_start = None
  • AP id assignment: first-seen order during lex; identical lexeme ⇒ same Ap. Closed alphabet ⇒ Guard masks have fixed width n.
  • Bitset width = n rounded to machine words; Σ is NEVER materialized (2^n blowup) — guards stay symbolic (mask+care, DNF over APs).

8.2 Grammar (EBNF) — LTL + past-LTL + LTLf + CTL

Single concrete syntax; Kind ∈ {Ltl, Ctl, PastLtl, Ltlf} is decided post-parse by tl-formula well-formedness (§8.6), not by separate grammars. CTL fuses each path quantifier with exactly one temporal op (no bare A/E).

formula     = iff_expr ;
