(* boolean layer — lowest precedence first, all left-assoc except -> right-assoc *)
iff_expr    = imp_expr , { "<->" , imp_expr } ;          (* <-> : equivalence  *)
imp_expr    = or_expr  , [ "->" , imp_expr ] ;           (* -> : right-assoc, lowest binary *)
or_expr     = and_expr , { "|"  , and_expr } ;           (* | : disjunction *)
and_expr    = bin_temp , { "&"  , bin_temp } ;           (* & : conjunction *)

(* binary temporal — bind tighter than boolean, right-assoc *)
bin_temp    = un_expr , { ("U" | "R" | "W" | "M" | "S") , un_expr } ;
                (* U until, R release, W weak-until, M strong-release (LTL);
                   S since (past-LTL). W,M are sugar -> U,R in NNF *)

(* unary prefix — highest precedence, right-assoc, stackable: e.g. X X G p *)
un_expr     = { un_op } , primary ;
un_op       = "!" | "X" | "F" | "G"                       (* future LTL: ! not, X next, F eventually, G globally *)
            | "Y" | "O" | "H"                             (* past-LTL: Y previous, O once, H historically *)
            | ctl_un ;                                    (* CTL fused quantifier+op *)
ctl_un      = "EX" | "AX" | "EF" | "AF" | "EG" | "AG" ;

primary     = atom
            | "true" | "false"
            | "(" , formula , ")"
            | ctl_bin_until ;                             (* fused CTL until needs explicit bracket form *)

ctl_bin_until = ("E" | "A") , "[" , formula , "U" , formula , "]" ;  (* E[φ U ψ], A[φ U ψ] *)

atom        = lower_id ;                                  (* AP: [a-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*  e.g. p, req, ack_2 *)
lower_id    = ? [a-z][A-Za-z0-9_]* ? ;

Precedence summary (tightest → loosest): unary prefix (!,X,F,G,Y,O,H,EX…AG) > binary temporal (U,R,W,M,S) > & > | > -> > <->. Examples disambiguated:

Input Parse
G p -> F q (G p) -> (F q) — unary binds before ->
a U b U c a U (b U c) — U right-assoc
! a U b (! a) U b — ! binds before U
a & b U c a & (b U c) — U tighter than &
EF p & q (EF p) & q — EF unary, tighter than &
A[p U q] -> r (A[p U q]) -> (r)

Sugar expansion (parse-time, before NNF): p<->q ⇒ (p->q)&(q->p); p->q ⇒ !p|q; p W q ⇒ (p U q)|G p; p M q ⇒ (p R q)&F p. Formula AST has no W/M/->/<-> nodes — only LOCKED variants.

8.3 LTL semantics over omega-words

w ⊨ φ ("word w models φ"); wⁱ = wᵢ wᵢ₊₁ … is the suffix from position i; w ⊨ φ ≜ w⁰ ⊨ φ. Future fragment (positions i,j ∈ ℕ):

Operator wⁱ ⊨ … iff
Atom(p) p ∈ wᵢ
True / False always / never
Not φ not wⁱ ⊨ φ
And φ ψ / Or φ ψ both / either
X φ (next) wⁱ⁺¹ ⊨ φ
F φ (eventually) ∃ j ≥ i. wʲ ⊨ φ
G φ (globally) ∀ j ≥ i. wʲ ⊨ φ
U φ ψ (until) ∃ j ≥ i. (wʲ ⊨ ψ) ∧ ∀ i ≤ k < j. wᵏ ⊨ φ
R φ ψ (release) ∀ j ≥ i. (wʲ ⊨ ψ) ∨ ∃ i ≤ k < j. wᵏ ⊨ φ

Identities (used by simplification + NNF): F φ ≡ true U φ; G φ ≡ false R φ ≡ !F!φ; φ R ψ ≡ !(!φ U !ψ); X(φ∧ψ) ≡ Xφ ∧ Xψ; X is self-dual: !Xφ ≡ X!φ. Strong-until U requires ψ to actually occur; weak-until W (sugar) does not.

8.4 CTL semantics over Kripke states

CTL is interpreted at a state s of a Kripke structure K = (S, S₀, →, L) where L(s) ⊆ AP. A path π = s₀ s₁ … satisfies s_{k} → s_{k+1} for all k; π[i] is its i-th state. K, s ⊨ φ:

Operator K, s ⊨ … iff Quantifier reading
Atom(p) p ∈ L(s) —
Not/And/Or boolean, pointwise —
Ex φ (EX) ∃ s'. s → s' ∧ K,s' ⊨ φ exists-next
Ax φ (AX) ∀ s'. s → s' ⇒ K,s' ⊨ φ all-next = !EX!φ
Ef φ (EF) ∃ path π from s. ∃ i. K,π[i] ⊨ φ exists-eventually = E[true U φ]
Af φ (AF) ∀ path π from s. ∃ i. K,π[i] ⊨ φ all-eventually = !EG!φ
Eg φ (EG) ∃ path π from s. ∀ i. K,π[i] ⊨ φ exists-globally
Ag φ (AG) ∀ path π from s. ∀ i. K,π[i] ⊨ φ all-globally = !EF!φ
Eu φ ψ (E[φUψ]) ∃ path π. ∃ j. K,π[j] ⊨ ψ ∧ ∀ k<j. K,π[k] ⊨ φ exists-until
Au φ ψ (A[φUψ]) ∀ path π. ∃ j. K,π[j] ⊨ ψ ∧ ∀ k<j. K,π[k] ⊨ φ all-until

CTL adequate basis: {EX, EG, EU} + boolean. Derivations (used by CtlChecker): EF φ = E[true U φ]; AX φ = !EX !φ; AG φ = !EF !φ = !E[true U !φ]; AF φ = !EG !φ; A[φ U ψ] = !( E[!ψ U (!φ∧!ψ)] ) ∧ !EG !ψ. State-vs-path: every CTL operator pairs a quantifier {A,E} with a temporal op {X,F,G,U} — a temporal op WITHOUT a quantifier (e.g. EX(p U q)) is a path formula in a state context ⇒ rejected (§8.6). CTL* (full nesting) is a NON-GOAL.

8.5 Finite-trace LTLf and past-LTL semantics

LTLf (De Giacomo–Vardi): same syntax as LTL, interpreted over a finite word u = u₀…u_{m-1}, m = |u| ≥ 1. Suffix uⁱ, last index m-1. The end-of-trace boundary forces a weak/strong-next split:

Operator uⁱ ⊨ … (LTLf), 0 ≤ i < m iff
X φ (strong next) i+1 < m ∧ u^{i+1} ⊨ φ — false at last index (no successor)
weak-next N φ i+1 = m ∨ u^{i+1} ⊨ φ — true at last index; N φ ≜ !X!φ on finite words
F φ ∃ i ≤ j < m. uʲ ⊨ φ
G φ ∀ i ≤ j < m. uʲ ⊨ φ (vacuously true past the end)
U φ ψ ∃ i ≤ j < m. uʲ ⊨ ψ ∧ ∀ i ≤ k < j. uᵏ ⊨ φ
R φ ψ ∀ i ≤ j < m. uʲ ⊨ ψ ∨ ∃ i ≤ k < j. uᵏ ⊨ φ (release weak at end)
  • eval_ltlf(f, t) returns a definitive bool (no Unknown) — the whole trace is observed. Computed by progression (§8.8, Phase 4): X progresses to its arg; at the final state the residual is evaluated with X φ ⇒ false, G φ ⇒ true, etc. — the weak/strong-next distinction lives entirely at this last step.
  • LTLf ≠ LTL on the same prefix: G p is true on any finite all-p trace but false on an infinite word that later drops p.

Past-LTL: future + past operators over a word, position i, looking backward (Phase 10, evaluated by DP):

Operator wⁱ ⊨ … iff
Y φ (previous, strong) i > 0 ∧ w^{i-1} ⊨ φ — false at i = 0
O φ (once) ∃ 0 ≤ j ≤ i. wʲ ⊨ φ — past-F
H φ (historically) ∀ 0 ≤ j ≤ i. wʲ ⊨ φ — past-G = !O!φ
S φ ψ (since) ∃ 0 ≤ j ≤ i. wʲ ⊨ ψ ∧ ∀ j < k ≤ i. wᵏ ⊨ φ — past-U

Duals: O φ = true S φ; H φ = !O !φ; weak-previous Ỹ φ = !Y!φ (true at i=0). Past-LTL is evaluable left-to-right: each state's past-obligation vector derives from the previous state's (DP, §10), O(m·|cl|) time.

8.6 Well-formedness — Kind classification

tl-formula::Kind ∈ {Ltl, Ctl, PastLtl, Ltlf} assigned by structural rules. Mixing fragments is rejected with the listed error (ParseError/well-formedness check):

Rule Holds Violation → error
CTL state-formula closure every temporal op carries a quantifier (Ex/Ax/Ef/Af/Eg/Ag/Eu/Au); the immediate arg of a quantifier is itself a state formula bare X/F/G/U/R under a state context ⇒ PathFormulaWhereStateRequired
LTL purity no Ex…Au nodes; future ops only CTL op in LTL ⇒ MixedFragment
Past purity (PastLtl) Y/O/H/S present; combined with future LTL allowed; NO CTL CTL op ⇒ MixedFragment
LTLf same node set as LTL; flagged by CLI/context (--finite), not by distinct nodes —
Quantifier well-nesting Eu/Au written E[..U..]/A[..U..]; both args state formulas A p U q (no brackets) ⇒ ParseError::ExpectedBracket

Kind selects the evaluator: Ltl+Kripke → automata path (check_ltl); Ctl+Kripke → CtlChecker; Ltl/Ltlf+Trace → eval_ltlf; PastLtl+Trace → past-LTL DP; Ltl+stream → tl-monitor.

8.7 NNF + duality rules

nnf(f: &Formula) -> Formula pushes Not inward until it sits only on Atom. Why: GPVW tableau (§8.10) and CTL labeling (§8.12) assume negation only on atoms (so the subformula closure is positive and obligation sets are monotone). LOCKED dualities:

Negated form Rewrites to
!!φ φ
!True / !False False / True
!(φ ∧ ψ) !φ ∨ !ψ
!(φ ∨ ψ) !φ ∧ !ψ
!X φ X !φ (X self-dual)
!F φ G !φ
!G φ F !φ
!(φ U ψ) !φ R !ψ
!(φ R ψ) !φ U !ψ
!Y φ Ỹ !φ (weak-previous; = !Y!φ, true at i=0)
!O φ H !φ
!H φ O !φ
!(φ S ψ) weak-since of duals (!φ S̃ !ψ)
!EX φ AX !φ
!AX φ EX !φ
!EF φ AG !φ
!AG φ EF !φ
!EG φ AF !φ
!AF φ EG !φ
!E[φ U ψ] A[!φ R !ψ] (= AG/AF-derived; uses release form)
!A[φ U ψ] E[!φ R !ψ]

NNF preserves semantics + Kind; size grows ≤ ×2 (one Not per negated leaf). After NNF, Not appears only as Not(Atom(_)); an assert in debug builds checks this invariant before GPVW/labeling.

8.8 Formula progression (the LTL derivative)

progress(f, s) -> Formula rewrites f given current letter s, returning the obligation for the next step; folding it over a stream is the monitor + LTLf evaluator core. Recurrences (over NNF):

progress(true, s)        = true
progress(false, s)       = false
progress(p, s)           = if p ∈ s.aps then true else false       // Atom
progress(!p, s)          = !progress(p, s)                          // p atomic post-NNF
progress(φ ∧ ψ, s)       = progress(φ,s) ∧ progress(ψ,s)
progress(φ ∨ ψ, s)       = progress(φ,s) ∨ progress(ψ,s)
progress(X φ, s)         = φ                                        // discharge one step
progress(F φ, s)         = progress(φ,s) ∨ F φ
progress(G φ, s)         = progress(φ,s) ∧ G φ
progress(φ U ψ, s)       = progress(ψ,s) ∨ (progress(φ,s) ∧ (φ U ψ))
progress(φ R ψ, s)       = progress(ψ,s) ∧ (progress(φ,s) ∨ (φ R ψ))
  • Residual simplified after each step (constant folding) → bounded growth; residual true/false = definitive verdict.
  • LTLf end-of-trace: after the last letter, evaluate the residual with X φ ⇒ false (strong next has no successor), G φ ⇒ true, F φ ⇒ false, φ U ψ ⇒ false, φ R ψ ⇒ true.

8.9 omega-automata model — Nba / Ngba

Concept Ngba (generalized Buchi) Nba (Buchi)
States 0..nstates 0..nstates
Init init: Vec<usize> init: Vec<usize>
Transitions edges[q] = Vec<(Guard, q')> — guard = boolean over APs same
Acceptance accept: Vec<Vec<usize>> — set of accept sets F₁..F_k accept: Vec<usize> — one set F
Run on w∈Σ^ω ρ = q₀ q₁ …, q₀∈init, ∃(g,q_{i+1})∈edges[qᵢ] with wᵢ ⊨ g same
Accepting run ∀ j. inf(ρ) ∩ Fⱼ ≠ ∅ (each Fⱼ hit infinitely often) inf(ρ) ∩ F ≠ ∅ (F hit infinitely often)
Language `L(A) = { w A has an accepting run on w }`
inf(ρ) set of states visited infinitely often same
  • Guard(mask+care / small DNF over APs): a letter wᵢ satisfies g iff wᵢ & care == mask & care. One edge ⇒ a set of letters; Σ never enumerated.
  • A lasso Lasso { stem, cycle } encodes an ultimately-periodic run/word stem·cycle^ω. By the pumping/Ramsey argument: L(A) ≠ ∅ ⇔ A accepts some lasso ⇔ there is a reachable cycle through an accept state (Nba) / through all accept sets (Ngba). emptiness returns Some(Lasso) iff non-empty.

8.10 Worked GPVW example — G(p -> F q) → Ngba

Formula in NNF: G(!p ∨ F q). Subformula closure closure(φ) (positive parts driving obligations): { G(!p∨Fq), !p∨Fq, !p, F q, q }. A GPVW node = an obligation set = subformulas required to hold now, split into a now-part (atomic/boolean ⇒ the edge Guard) and a next-part (carried to the successor). Eventualities (F/U right side) tracked by one generalized accept set each.

Obligation expansion: G ψ ⇒ require ψ now AND G ψ next. F q ⇒ either q now, OR F q next (deferred — must eventually discharge ⇒ accept-set membership). Resulting 2 reachable nodes:

Node Obligation (now / next) Edge guard Successor
n₀ (q pending free) now: !p∨Fq, G-recur; eventuality Fq not yet due true (when p false: !p satisfies; when p true: need Fq) self-loop variants → n₀, n₁
n₁ (owes q) committed Fq deferred: must reach q true n₁ (while !q) / n₀ (when q seen) 
            !p | q                  p & !q
   ┌────────────────────┐      ┌──────────────┐
   │                    v      │              v
 ( n0 )  ───────────────────►  ( n1 )  ◄───────┘   self-loop while !q
   ▲   p & !q (owe q)            │
   └──────── q ──────────────────┘   (q seen: eventuality discharged)
 Ngba accept sets: F1 = { n0 }     (states NOT owing an unfulfilled Fq)
   accepting run must hit F1 infinitely often  ⇒  q recurs whenever p holds
  • One accept set F₁ = {n₀} (one F-eventuality). An infinite run is accepting iff it leaves n₁ (discharges q) infinitely often ⇒ exactly G(p→Fq) semantics: every p is eventually followed by q.
  • Built on-the-fly: nodes generated lazily as obligation sets are explored; only reachable nodes materialized (Phase 6 + Phase 12 lazy product).

Degeneralize Ngba → Nba (§8.11, Phase 7): k=1 accept set ⇒ degeneralization is trivial — the counter has range 0..1, Nba.accept = F₁ = {n₀}, structure unchanged. (For k>1: states become (q, c), c∈0..k, c advances when current accept set F_c is visited; single Nba accept set = {(q,0) : q∈F_k}.)

Accepting lasso readoff: word ({p,q})^ω — at each step p holds and q holds, run n₀ →(p&q? handled as q seen) n₀ … stays in/returns to n₀ ⇒ visits F₁ infinitely ⇒ accepted. Word ({p})^ω (p forever, q never): run forced into n₁ and stuck (!q self-loop), never revisits F₁ ⇒ rejected ⇒ correct (p never followed by q). Lasso for the rejected case in a model check = counterexample stem=[], cycle=[{p}].

8.11 Degeneralization correctness (counter construction)

degeneralize(g: &Ngba) -> Nba: states = {0..g.nstates} × {0..k} where k = g.accept.len(); init = {(q,0) : q∈g.init}; from (q,c) on edge (guard,q'): go to (q', c') where c' = (c+1) mod k if q ∈ accept[c] else c; single accept set F = {(q, 0) : q ∈ accept[k-1]} (chosen convention: accept when the counter wraps past the last set). State blowup ≤ × k. Invariant: an Nba run hits F infinitely often ⇔ the projected Ngba run hits every accept[i] infinitely often. k=0 (no accept sets, e.g. a safety formula) ⇒ all states accepting (every infinite run accepted).

8.12 Worked CTL labeling example — EF q and AG p over a Kripke structure

CtlChecker::sat(f) -> Vec<bool> returns per-state truth (index = state id). holds ⇔ all init states labeled true. Example K: 4 states.

   states: s0{p}   s1{p,q}   s2{p}   s3{}
   init = { s0 }
   edges:  s0 -> s1, s2 ;  s1 -> s1 ;  s2 -> s3 ;  s3 -> s3
   L(s0)={p}  L(s1)={p,q}  L(s2)={p}  L(s3)={}

sat(EF q) = E[true U q] — least fixpoint, seed = states with q, then add predecessors:

Iter Rule Set (state ids true)
0 sat(q) (seed) {s1}
1 add s : ∃ s→s' ∈ set {s1, s0} (s0→s1)
2 fixpoint (no new preds of s1 besides s0) {s1, s0} — stable

sat(EF q) = [s0:T, s1:T, s2:F, s3:F]. holds(EF q): init {s0} true ⇒ holds (some path from s0 reaches q via s0→s1).

sat(AG p) = !EF !p: first sat(!p) = {s3} (only s3 lacks p). sat(EF !p) lfp: seed {s3}; preds: s2→s3 ⇒ add s2; preds of s2: s0→s2 ⇒ add s0; preds of s3: s3(self) already in; preds of s0: none ⇒ {s3,s2,s0}. sat(AG p) = ¬{s3,s2,s0} = {s1} ⇒ [s0:F,s1:T,s2:F,s3:F]. holds(AG p): init s0 false ⇒ fails (path s0→s2→s3 reaches s3 where !p). Counterexample path: s0 s2 s3.

sat(EG p) (gfp via SCCs): restrict to p-states {s0,s1,s2}; an EG p state lies on an infinite p-path = a state that can reach a nontrivial SCC (or self-loop) within the p-subgraph. s1 self-loops (in p-subgraph) ⇒ s1∈sat(EG p); s0→s1 reaches it ⇒ s0; s2→s3 exits p ⇒ s2∉. sat(EG p)=[s0:T,s1:T,s2:F,s3:F].

Labeling complexity: O(|f| · (|S| + |→|)) (each subformula one fixpoint/SCC pass over the graph) — Clarke-Emerson-Sistla.

8.13 Worked runtime-monitoring example — Monitor::step over a stream for G(req -> F ack)

NNF G(!req ∨ F ack). Monitor::step(s) folds progress then maps the residual to RvVerdict (LTL3/RV-LTL). Stream s₀={req}, s₁={}, s₂={ack}, …:

Event progress residual (simplified) LTL3 verdict Why
s₀={req} F ack ∧ G(!req∨F ack) (req fires ⇒ owes ack) Unknown both an extension satisfying and one violating exist
s₁={} (no ack) F ack ∧ G(…) (still owes ack) Unknown ack could still come
s₂={ack} G(!req∨F ack) (ack discharged; back to base obligation) Unknown safety part can still be violated forever-after
(hypothetical) req then ack never, run ends-ish residual keeps F ack undischarged on every extension that drops ack tends False only if a bad prefix is reached G(req→F ack) is non-monitorable for True (no good prefix)
  • LTL3 maps residual: residual ≡ true ⇒ True; residual ≡ false ⇒ False; else Unknown. True/False are definitive (every / no infinite extension satisfies φ — good/bad prefix).
  • RV-LTL (4-valued via RvVerdict): when LTL3 says Unknown, refine using the finite (LTLf) semantics of the residual to PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse; reserves True/False for definitive. Verdict (2-valued) used only where a definitive answer is guaranteed.
  • Monitorability: φ is monitorable from prefix u iff some extension of u reaches a definitive verdict. G(req→F ack) has no good prefix (cannot ever conclude True) but has bad prefixes only in the LTLf reading ⇒ classified by tl-monitor monitorability check.

8.14 Malformed inputs and their errors

Lexer/Parser (ParseError{kind: ParseErrorKind, span}) and tl-formula well-formedness reject these; each is a conformance fixture (Phase 13):

Malformed input Error Cause
G (p -> ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedEof unbalanced ( / missing operand
p & & q ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken binary & with no left/right operand
EX(p U q) (as CTL) well-formedness PathFormulaWhereStateRequired p U q is a path formula under quantifier scope; CTL needs E[p U q]
A p U q ParseErrorKind::ExpectedBracket fused CTL until must be A[p U q]
G EX p (mixing) MixedFragment LTL G over CTL EX — not CTL* (NON-GOAL)
U p q ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken U is infix, not prefix
0bad / Req ParseErrorKind::BadAtom AP must match [a-z][A-Za-z0-9_]* (lowercase-initial)
empty Trace (prefix=[]) for eval_ltlf EvalError::EmptyTrace LTLf requires m ≥ 1 (semantics undefined at m=0)
Trace{loop_start:Some(3), prefix.len()=2} TraceError::BadLoopStart lasso loop_start must index into prefix
Kripke with s having no successor KripkeError::NotTotal transition relation must be total (every state has ≥1 successor) for CTL/Buchi semantics

8.15 Domain invariants (carried into every phase)

# Invariant Enforced where
D1 post-nnf, Not wraps only Atom debug assert before GPVW/labeling
D2 Guard width = AP-universe size n; Σ never enumerated tl-automata
D3 Kripke transition relation total KripkeStructure validation
D4 Trace lasso well-formed: loop_start < prefix.len() if Some; prefix non-empty tl-trace validation
D5 Ngba ⇒ Nba degeneralization preserves language (each accept set hit i.o. ⇔ single set i.o.) Phase 7 + differential test vs Spot
D6 K ⊨ Aφ ⇔ L(product(Nba(!φ), K)) = ∅; counterexample = accepting lasso Phase 8
D7 LTL3 True/False are definitive (good/bad prefix); Unknown never downgrades a definitive verdict Phase 9
D8 Kind correctly routes formula to its evaluator; cross-fragment formula rejected pre-eval Phase 2 + 5/8
D9 progression residual ≡ true/false ⇔ definitive monitor verdict; LTLf end-of-trace uses strong-X⇒false Phase 4 + 9

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Architecture Decision Records. Each row is LOCKED for the lifetime of the blueprint; revisiting requires a new ADR superseding the old. Status = Accepted unless noted. "#281" = the symbolic/BDD model checker project; "#279/#280/#277/#278" = the modeling-language frontend / theorem prover / SAT / SMT projects (all NON-GOALS here).

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 C/C++; Python; Java; Haskell; OCaml; Go Automata construction + graph emptiness + trace eval are pointer/graph-heavy + hot. Rust enums + exhaustive match model Formula/Nba/Ngba/Verdict as zero-cost tagged unions; bitset AP valuations + arena automaton states give cache-friendly perf with memory safety. C/C++ = memory-unsafe on-the-fly automaton graphs; Python/Java = nested DFS + product too hot for GC + boxing; Haskell/OCaml = strict mutable DFS-coloring + on-the-fly build fight laziness/GC. C would be chosen if the engine must embed inside an existing C-based verifier toolchain with strict ABI stability requirements (e.g., a legacy SPIN plugin). Python wins for rapid prototype phases where verification is a script that calls an external solver and correctness is asserted via Spot.
ADR-02 Project decomposition 7-crate Cargo workspace: tl-syntax, tl-formula, tl-trace, tl-automata, tl-eval, tl-monitor, tl-cli Single monolithic crate; 2 crates (lib+bin); 12+ micro-crates One concern per crate -> independent test surfaces + clean dep DAG (syntax->formula->trace->automata->eval->monitor->cli). Monolith = no enforced layering, slow incremental builds. Micro-crates = version churn + dep-graph noise with no benefit at this scale. DAG is acyclic by construction; cli is the only std member. A monolith wins when the team is a solo developer shipping quickly without needing tl-monitor as an independent embedded library. 12+ micro-crates win when the formula AST and the automata layer are independently versioned and distributed to separate teams consuming only one crate each.
ADR-03 Crate-level no_std policy tl-formula, tl-trace, tl-automata, tl-eval, tl-monitor, tl-syntax are no_std + alloc; only tl-cli is std All crates std; whole workspace no_std (incl. cli) Core engine embeddable in RV/embedded monitors (tl-monitor on-device). alloc suffices for Vec/Box AST + automaton arenas. std-everywhere blocks embedding; no_std cli is pointless (needs files/argv/stdout for HOA/dot). I/O + filesystem live solely in tl-cli. All-std wins when the engine will never target embedded or no_std environments and the cost of maintaining no_std compatibility (no std::collections, manual alloc error handling) outweighs the embedding benefit.
ADR-04 Formula AST representation Single unified Formula enum covering LTL + CTL + past-LTL + LTLf operators; Kind enum classifies well-formedness post-parse Separate LtlFormula/CtlFormula/PastFormula types; trait-object AST; untyped s-expression tree Shared nnf(), closure(), simplify, pretty-printer over ONE type; CTL fuses path-quantifier+temporal-op into single variants (Ex,Au,…) so the state-vs-path distinction is enforced by Kind + well-formedness, not by separate types. Separate types = 3x duplicated traversals + lossy conversions. Trait objects = no exhaustive match, heap-indirection on hot eval paths. Separate typed formulas win when the logics diverge significantly in future (e.g., adding MTL/TCTL with time-bounds that require a fundamentally different AST node type), making a unified enum unwieldy. Trait objects win when the logic set is open-ended and plugins must add operators at runtime without recompiling the core.
ADR-05 NNF as algorithmic precondition nnf(&Formula)->Formula pushes negation onto atoms only; GPVW + CTL labeling + progression all assume NNF Carry arbitrary Not; on-the-fly negation handling in each algorithm Dualities (not X = X not; not(p U q)=(not p) R (not q); not F=G not; not G=F not; not(EX)=AX(not)) applied once. Fixed positive operator set shrinks GPVW node alphabet + labeling cases. Arbitrary Not = combinatorial special-cases in every evaluator + duplicated closure entries. NNF is idempotent + size-linear. In-place negation wins when the formula is only evaluated once and the one-time NNF pass costs more than the occasional in-evaluator Not-dispatch (e.g., a single-shot web-service that runs progress exactly once per request with a small formula).
ADR-06 LTL->automaton algorithm GPVW on-the-fly tableau: ltl_to_ngba(&Formula)->Ngba LTL2BA (Gastin-Oddoux, very-weak alternating automata); Safra/Safraless determinization; tableau-free (Couvreur); direct Spot port GPVW (Gerth-Peled-Vardi-Wolper 1995) builds nodes lazily from current/next obligation sets over the subformula closure -> matches the explicit-state philosophy + is the canonical teaching/reference construction. Builds only reachable nodes (on-the-fly). LTL2BA/Safra add alternating/determinization machinery out of scope; Safra needed only for determinization (not required for emptiness). LTL2BA wins when minimizing automaton state count is critical (its VWAA route often yields smaller automata than GPVW on safety/liveness patterns). Safra determinization wins when the application requires a deterministic Büchi automaton (e.g., quantitative or probabilistic model checking where nondeterminism is disallowed).
ADR-07 Buchi flavor + reduction Build generalized Buchi Ngba (one accept set per Until-eventuality) then degeneralize(&Ngba)->Nba (counter construction) Construct a deterministic-acceptance Buchi directly; transition-based generalized Buchi (TGBA) as the terminal form; keep Ngba and check generalized emptiness directly GPVW naturally yields generalized acceptance (one set per U); degeneralization on (state x counter 0..k) advancing through accept sets is textbook + lets the downstream product/emptiness assume a single accept set (Nba.accept: Vec<usize>). Direct Buchi = re-derive acceptance bookkeeping. TGBA terminal form would complicate product + nested DFS contract. TGBA terminal form wins when targeting Spot's twa_graph directly for interop, since Spot operates natively on TGBA and conversion to NBA bloats state count by the acceptance-set count factor. Generalized-emptiness-directly wins in a research context where the degeneralization state blowup is measured as unacceptably large.
ADR-08 Emptiness / counterexample algorithm Nested DFS (Courcoubetis-Vardi-Wolper-Yannakakis): emptiness(&Nba)->Option<Lasso> SCC-based (Tarjan) emptiness; one-pass Couvreur SCC; CEGAR Nested DFS = memory-efficient (2-bit coloring/state), finds a reachable accepting cycle, and directly extracts a Lasso{stem,cycle} counterexample for check_ltl. Outer DFS finds accepting states; inner DFS seeks a path back -> the cycle. SCC/Tarjan returns components, not a ready lasso witness, and needs extra reconstruction. Nested DFS is the canonical RV/model-check emptiness check. SCC-based (Tarjan) emptiness wins in parallel model checkers where SCC decomposition can be distributed (nested DFS is inherently sequential due to its DFS-coloring invariant). Couvreur one-pass wins when the acceptance condition is generalized (TGBA) and you want to avoid degeneralization before the emptiness check.
ADR-09 LTL model-check engine class Explicit-state / automata-theoretic: product(Nba(not phi), Kripke) then nested-DFS emptiness; `K = A phi iff L(product) = empty` Symbolic / BDD fixpoint model checking; bounded model checking via SAT; partial-order reduction THIS engine is explicit-state + automata/trace based by charter. Symbolic/BDD is project #281 (explicit NON-GOAL here) — keep the two engines disjoint for differential cross-checking. BMC needs the SAT core (#277). Explicit state keeps counterexamples concrete (lasso = real word) and code auditable against Spot.
ADR-10 AP valuation + edge guards State{aps: Bitset}; automaton edges carry Guard = boolean over APs (DNF / bitset mask+care) HashSet<Ap> per state; per-AP Vec<bool>; explicit power-set edge alphabet (2^ AP labels)
ADR-11 Automaton state storage Arena-allocated automaton states (index-addressed Vecs: edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard,usize)>>, accept), usize ids Box/Rc<Node> graph with pointer edges; HashMap<StateId,Node> Arena + usize indices = cache-friendly contiguous storage, trivial visited-bitsets for nested DFS, no per-node alloc, no reference-cycle leaks. Pointer graphs = scattered allocs + unsafe cycles + awkward coloring. HashMap = hashing overhead + non-contiguous iteration during DFS. Product reuses the same index scheme ((auto_state, kripke_state) pair -> id). Pointer-based graphs win in incremental automaton construction scenarios where states must be inserted and deleted arbitrarily (e.g., an online symbolic fixpoint that adds states one at a time and prunes unreachable ones mid-construction). HashMap wins when state IDs are sparse and non-sequential (e.g., content-addressed nodes hashed by obligation set in a persistent tableau).
ADR-12 CTL evaluation algorithm Explicit-state iterative labeling (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla 1986): CtlChecker::sat(&Formula)->Vec<bool>; EX=preimage, E[pUq]=lfp, EG p=gfp via SCC quotient; derive EF/AG/AF/AX/A[U] Game-based (parity/model-checking games); tableau CTL; symbolic CTL Labeling is the canonical explicit CTL procedure: per-state truth vectors, fixpoints over the Kripke graph, EG via SCCs of the sat(p) sub-Kripke. holds = every init state labeled. Game-based adds parity-game machinery with no payoff for CTL (no fairness/CTL* here). Symbolic CTL belongs to #281. Game-based wins when the logic is extended to CTL* or µ-calculus (parity games subsume both), and the invested machinery pays off across the richer formula class. Symbolic/BDD CTL wins (project #281) once the Kripke structure exceeds the explicit-state wall (~10⁷ states).
ADR-13 Finite-trace (LTLf) evaluation Progression: eval_ltlf(&Formula,&Trace)->bool via progress() recurrences; weak/strong next at end-of-trace Translate every LTLf to a DFA first (Phase 11 only); brute-force suffix enumeration Progression is incremental, allocation-light, and shared with the runtime monitor (Monitor::progress). Recurrences: progress(X f,s)=f; progress(f U g,s)=progress(g,s) OR (progress(f,s) AND f U g); progress(G f,s)=progress(f,s) AND G f. DFA construction (LTLf->DFA) is reserved for Phase 11 bounded/repeated checks where amortization pays; per-trace it is overkill. DFA-first wins when the same LTLf formula is checked against thousands of traces (amortized DFA construction over many checks); DFA step is O(1) per event vs O(
ADR-14 Runtime monitoring strategy Incremental formula progression (Monitor::step/progress) as the default; monitor-automaton synthesis optional Always pre-compile a monitor automaton (Bauer-Leucker-Schallhart 3-valued automaton); rewriting engine (Rosu-Havelund) as a separate subsystem Progression = zero precompute, derivative folds over the event stream, residual True/False = definitive verdict; ideal for ad-hoc/short streams + embedded no_std. Pre-compiled automaton wins only for long high-throughput streams over a fixed formula -> kept as an OPTIONAL synthesis path (tl-monitor). Rewriting (Rosu-Havelund) is effectively progression -> one mechanism, not two. Pre-compiled monitor automaton wins for continuous high-rate streams (>10⁶ events/s) where per-event O(1) state transition is required and the one-time synthesis cost is amortized over millions of events — the monitor-automaton synthesis path in tl-monitor handles exactly this.
ADR-15 Monitor verdict domains Verdict{True,False} (2-valued definitive) + RvVerdict{True,False,PresumablyTrue,PresumablyFalse,Unknown} (LTL3 3-valued ∪ RV-LTL 4-valued) 2-valued only; raw boolean; custom per-call enum LTL3 needs Unknown (no good/bad prefix yet); RV-LTL adds PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse via finite-semantics tie-break. One RvVerdict covers both (LTL3 = drop the Presumably-* into Unknown). 2-valued cannot express "verdict not yet decided" -> would force premature commitment + break monitorability. 2-valued verdict wins in safety-critical embedded systems where the controller only needs "alarm" or "no alarm" and the richer Unknown/Presumably* states cannot be mapped to a control output — and the monitoring engineer guarantees monitorability of all deployed formulas at design time.
ADR-16 Past-time LTL evaluation Dynamic programming over the trace: per-state past-obligation vector from the previous state; operators Y (previous), O (once), H (historically), S (since) Reverse-time automaton; reuse future-LTL progression on a reversed trace Past operators are inherently left-to-right DP: each state's vector depends only on its predecessor's -> O( trace
ADR-17 LTLf decision via DFA (Phase 11) Progression-to-DFA / alternating-automaton construction -> minimized DFA for bounded + repeated finite-model checks BDD-symbolic LTLf; SAT-based bounded check; skip DFA entirely De Giacomo-Vardi LTLf->DFA gives a reusable deterministic decision procedure (minimize once, decide many traces) for bounded/finite model checks. Symbolic LTLf = #281 territory; SAT path = #277 dependency. Per-trace evaluation stays on progression (ADR-13); DFA is the amortized path only. SAT-based bounded checking wins when the LTLf formula has a very long horizon (many X nestings) that would cause DFA state explosion, but a bounded SAT encoding can express reachability within the horizon without building the full DFA.
ADR-18 Product construction timing On-the-fly (lazy) product during nested DFS (Phase 12 optimization); eager product() as the baseline/reference Always eager full product; always lazy (no eager reference impl) Eager product(&Nba,&KripkeStructure)->Nba is the simple, testable baseline (differential oracle). Phase 12 adds lazy product so unreachable product states are never materialized -> bounded memory on large Kripke structures. Keeping BOTH lets the lazy path be validated against the eager one. Always-lazy loses the simple reference; always-eager OOMs on big models. Eager full product wins when the same product is queried multiple times (e.g., checking a family of related LTL properties against one Kripke model) — build once, search many — amortizing the construction cost over multiple emptiness checks.
ADR-19 Automaton/formula output formats HOA (Hanoi Omega-Automata) + Graphviz dot, emitted by tl-cli to-buchi Custom binary format; Spot-only native dump; JSON HOA = the lingua franca interchange consumed by Spot + autfilt -> enables direct differential testing of the produced Nba/Ngba; dot = human inspection/debugging. Custom/JSON formats need bespoke parsers on the oracle side. HOA round-trips through the ecosystem; dot is universal for visualization. A custom binary format wins when automata must be serialized and loaded at high throughput (e.g., millions of small automata cached on disk) where HOA text parsing overhead is unacceptable. JSON wins when the consumer is a web frontend or a language without an HOA parser but with first-class JSON support.
ADR-20 Error handling discipline Result<_, E> everywhere on fallible paths; ParseError/ParseErrorKind in tl-syntax; NO panic!/unwrap in library crates on user input panic! on malformed input; Option for parse errors (loses cause); Box<dyn Error> in libs Libraries return typed errors; only tl-cli may convert to process exit codes. unwrap/panic on user-supplied formulas/traces = DoS + uncatchable failure for embedders. Option drops the diagnostic (location/kind). panic permitted ONLY for internal invariant violations (documented, unreachable). Fuzzers (ADR-22) assert no panic on arbitrary bytes. Box<dyn Error> wins in a CLI-only codebase where error types never need to be matched by library consumers — all errors bubble to main as display strings. Option wins for extremely simple internal helpers where the only possible error is "input was absent" and callers handle it by always checking is_none.
ADR-21 Differential correctness oracle Spot (ltl2tgba, autfilt) as the primary differential oracle for LTL automata + model-check verdicts (Phase 13) Hand-written expected-output golden files only; no external oracle; NuSMV-only GRAVEST failure = a GPVW/degeneralize bug or buggy nested DFS silently FLIPS a verdict (no crash). Cross-check Nba acceptance + emptiness/model-check verdicts against Spot on standard LTL pattern formulas + random formulas. Goldens alone can't catch the formulas nobody thought to write. CTL labeling cross-checked vs known results (no direct Spot CTL). Golden files only wins in an air-gapped environment where Spot cannot be installed (e.g., a certified avionics build environment) — the golden set must then be curated to cover all known failure modes of GPVW and nested DFS. NuSMV-only wins if the primary concern is CTL correctness and LTL automata correctness is deferred.
ADR-22 Fuzzing infrastructure cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets: parser (arbitrary bytes -> no panic), ltl_to_ngba+degeneralize (well-formed automaton invariants), eval/emptiness (no UB, terminates) AFL++ only; proptest only; no fuzzing cargo-fuzz = coverage-guided byte fuzzing of the parser + structured fuzzing of automaton/eval (proptest-style Formula generators feeding the same harness). Catches panics, infinite loops, and (via the Spot oracle) verdict flips on inputs no human wrote. proptest complements for shrinkable structured cases; not a substitute for coverage-guided. AFL++ wins if the CI environment uses a Linux-only container where cargo-fuzz/libFuzzer is unavailable or if the team has existing AFL++ infrastructure and corpus management already configured. Proptest-only wins for pure property-based testing of the nnf/closure algebraic laws where coverage-guided byte mutation adds no value over structured formula generation.
ADR-23 Benchmarking harness criterion (statistical micro/macro benches): formula->automaton size+time, model-check time, monitor throughput, against Phase-13 targets std::time::Instant ad-hoc timing; #[bench] (nightly only) criterion = stable-toolchain statistical benchmarking with regression detection -> guards the quantified targets (automaton state counts, model-check time, memory) that gate Phase 13 "done". Ad-hoc Instant is noisy + non-comparable; #[bench] requires nightly, violating the MSRV/stable policy (ADR-25). #[bench] wins if the project drops the stable-only policy and the team prefers the built-in harness without a third-party dep. Custom Instant-based timing wins for profiling scripts that need to output data in a specific format for an external performance dashboard that does not consume Criterion's JSON output.
ADR-24 CI pipeline GitHub Actions matrix: cargo build/test (stable + MSRV 1.78), clippy -D warnings, rustfmt --check, cargo deny (licenses/advisories), no_std build of core crates, nightly cargo-fuzz smoke + criterion baseline Local-only testing; single-toolchain CI; no lint gate CI enforces every invariant on every PR: compiles on MSRV + stable, lint/format clean, deps audited, no_std crates actually build no_std, fuzz harness still runs, bench baseline tracked. Differential Spot tests run where Spot is installable (gated job). Single-toolchain CI silently breaks MSRV; local-only = drift. Local-only testing wins for a solo developer in a rapid prototype phase where the overhead of maintaining a CI matrix is greater than the occasional manual check. Single-toolchain CI wins if the project is deployed exclusively to a fixed-version environment where MSRV compatibility is not a requirement.
ADR-25 MSRV + toolchain policy MSRV 1.78 pinned + CI-verified; stable channel for build/test/bench; nightly used ONLY for cargo-fuzz Track latest stable (no MSRV floor); nightly-required features; edition 2024 MSRV 1.78 = exhaustive match, let-else, needed const/iterator APIs without bleeding-edge features; predictable for embedders. Nightly confined to fuzz target build (not the shipped engine). Edition stays 2021 (ADR-01). No nightly-gated language features in any crate -> the engine compiles on any >=1.78 stable. Tracking latest stable wins when the engine is never embedded in a third-party crate with its own MSRV constraint and the team wants access to the newest Rust ergonomics without backport friction. Nightly-required wins if the engine depends on a performance-critical intrinsic or compiler feature only available on nightly.
ADR-26 Dependency policy + licensing Near-zero external deps in core crates (alloc only; optional tiny bitset helper behind a feature); dev/test/cli deps (clap, criterion, proptest, libfuzzer-sys) isolated; dual-license MIT OR Apache-2.0 Pull a parser-combinator (nom) / BDD crate / automata crate; GPL; single license Core engine is from-scratch (the point of the project) -> hand-written Lexer/Parser, no nom; no external automata/BDD crate (BDD = #281). Heavy deps confined to tl-cli + dev-deps so no_std core stays lean + auditable (ADR-22 oracle integrity). cargo deny (ADR-24) bans copyleft. MIT OR Apache-2.0 = Rust-ecosystem standard, permissive, patent-grant via Apache-2.0. nom wins if rapid parser iteration is more important than a from-scratch implementation (e.g., the grammar changes weekly during a research prototype). An existing automata crate wins if the project's primary goal shifts from learning-by-building to producing a production tool quickly — but this contradicts the stated pedagogical purpose of the project.

9.1 Cross-ADR invariants

  • Layering invariant (ADR-02/03): dependency DAG is acyclic and exactly tl-syntax -> tl-formula; tl-formula -> {}; tl-trace -> {}; tl-automata -> {tl-formula, tl-trace}; tl-eval -> {tl-formula, tl-trace, tl-automata}; tl-monitor -> {tl-formula, tl-automata}; tl-cli -> all. CI fails on any back-edge.
  • NNF invariant (ADR-05): ltl_to_ngba, CtlChecker::sat, progress, and past-LTL DP receive only NNF input; callers run nnf() first; violating it is an internal-invariant panic (ADR-20), never a user-facing path.
  • Single-accept-set invariant (ADR-07/08): everything downstream of degeneralize (i.e. product, emptiness) consumes an Nba with exactly one accept set; Ngba never reaches the nested DFS.
  • Verdict-soundness invariant (ADR-09/21): check_ltl returns Ok(()) iff the (lazy or eager) product is empty; any Err(Lasso) carries a stem+cycle that is a genuine accepting run of product(Nba(not phi), K); the Spot differential oracle is the standing guard against silent flips.
  • No-panic invariant (ADR-20/22): no library entry point panics on arbitrary user bytes/formulas/traces; the cargo-fuzz parser + eval targets assert this continuously.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace, seven tl-* members. One repo, no submodules. Directory-to-phase alignment is explicit (right column of §10.2). Layout is stable from Phase 0: shells exist day one, bodies fill in per phase — no file is renamed/moved later (churn breaks git blame, ADR links, conformance paths).

10.1 Top-level invariants

Invariant Rule Rationale / failure if violated
One workspace Cargo.toml Root Cargo.toml = [workspace] only, no [package] Virtual manifest; unifies lockfile + feature resolver v2
Single Cargo.lock Committed at root; crates do not carry own lock Reproducible CI / differential runs vs Spot
no_std + alloc for libs 1–6 Only tl-cli is std Embeddable monitor (tl-monitor); enforced by #![no_std] + CI --no-default-features build
Module file == spine name Files match LOCKED type/fn homes exactly No drift; reviewer locates gpvw.rs ⇒ ltl_to_ngba, emptiness.rs ⇒ nested DFS
Tests co-located + integration split Unit #[cfg(test)] inline; cross-crate in crates/*/tests/ Fast unit loop; integration exercises public API only
Conformance is data, not code conformance/ = TOML/HOA fixtures + golden outputs Regenerable; diffable; Spot oracle stored, not recomputed in CI hot path
Fuzz isolated fuzz/ own nightly crate (libFuzzer), excluded from workspace default-members Keeps stable build clean; cargo +nightly fuzz only

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative API surface. Every phase (## 12..## 25) builds against these exact signatures. Grouped by crate. Signatures + doc comments only; no bodies > 2 lines (lprompt CRITICAL RULE 10). Names are LOCKED — no synonyms, no renames.

11.1 Cross-cutting invariants (apply to all types below)

Concern Rule Rationale
Mutation Formula, Trace, KripkeStructure, Ngba, Nba, Guard, Lasso are immutable after construction (built once, then read-only). Checking is a pure function of inputs; sharing without locks.
Mutable state Only a Monitor (progresses across events) and a nested-DFS run (color arrays, stacks) mutate. Streaming + graph traversal are inherently stateful.
Concurrency Immutable types are Send + Sync -> shareable across threads by &T / Arc. CtlChecker<'a> borrows &KripkeStructure read-only -> many checkers over one model in parallel. Monitor is Send but not shared (single-stream owner). Zero interior mutability in shared types; no RefCell/Cell on the AST path.
Allocation tl-formula/tl-trace/tl-automata are no_std + alloc. Box<Formula> for AST children; Vec for adjacency/labels. Arena/index-based automaton states (usize ids), no Rc cycles. Cache-friendly, leak-free graphs; safe on-the-fly construction.
Serialization Automaton -> HOA (Hanoi Omega-Automata) and dot; Formula -> pretty-printer (round-trips through the parser). No serde derive required on hot types; emitters are explicit functions in tl-cli/tl-automata. Differential testing vs Spot (ltl2tgba) consumes/produces HOA.
Determinism Closure, NNF, automaton construction, and labeling are deterministic for a given input (stable iteration order over indices) -> reproducible automaton sizes + counterexamples for diff testing. Phase 13 gate requires bit-stable outputs.
Equality / hashing Formula derives PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Clone -> usable as HashMap/HashSet keys (GPVW node sets, closure dedup, progression memo). Structural identity drives caching.

11.2 tl-formula — AST, classification, transforms

/// Atomic-proposition id, interned by tl-syntax (a dense u32, 0..n_aps).
/// Identity only: the string spelling lives in the parser's symbol table.
pub type Ap = u32;

/// The Formula AST: one enum spanning LTL (future), past-LTL, and CTL.
/// Owns its children via Box (tree, never a DAG; structurally shared only via Clone).
/// Immutable after construction. Derives Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Debug.
/// LTL = boolean + future ops; PastLtl adds Y/O/H/S; CTL = fused-quantifier ops.
pub enum Formula {
    True, False, Atom(Ap),
    Not(Box<Formula>), And(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>), Or(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>),
    // future-time LTL:
    X(Box<Formula>),                              // next
    U(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>),                // until
    R(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>),                // release (dual of U)
    F(Box<Formula>),                              // eventually  (= True U f)
    G(Box<Formula>),                              // globally    (= False R f)
    // past-time LTL:
    Y(Box<Formula>),                              // previous (yesterday)
    O(Box<Formula>),                              // once
    H(Box<Formula>),                              // historically
    S(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>),                // since
    // CTL (path quantifier fused with one temporal op; arg is a STATE formula):
    Ex(Box<Formula>), Ax(Box<Formula>),           // EX / AX
    Eg(Box<Formula>), Ag(Box<Formula>),           // EG / AG
    Ef(Box<Formula>), Af(Box<Formula>),           // EF / AF
    Eu(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>),               // E[a U b]
    Au(Box<Formula>, Box<Formula>),               // A[a U b]
}

/// Negation normal form: negation pushed to atoms only, fixed dual operator set.
/// Pure; returns a fresh tree (input untouched). Idempotent: nnf(nnf(f)) == nnf(f).
/// GPVW (## 18) + the labeling algorithm (## 17) ASSUME their input is in NNF.
pub fn nnf(f: &Formula) -> Formula;

/// Subformula closure: f and all its subformulas (and the duals GPVW needs),
/// deduplicated, in a deterministic order. Drives GPVW node sets + CTL labeling.
/// Size |closure(f)| is linear in the size of f (each node contributes O(1) entries).
pub fn closure(f: &Formula) -> Vec<Formula>;

/// Well-formedness classification of a parsed formula.
/// Ltl = boolean+future only; Ctl = every temporal op carries a path quantifier
/// and every quantified arg is itself a state formula; PastLtl = future+past;
/// Ltlf = an Ltl formula intended for finite-trace semantics.
pub enum Kind { Ltl, Ctl, PastLtl, Ltlf }

NNF dualities (LOCKED rewrite set; nnf applies these until negation sits only on Atom):

Input Rewrite
Not(Not f) f
Not(And a b) Or(Not a, Not b)
Not(Or a b) And(Not a, Not b)
Not(X f) X(Not f)
Not(F f) G(Not f)
Not(G f) F(Not f)
Not(U a b) R(Not a, Not b)
Not(R a b) U(Not a, Not b)
Not(Ex f) Ax(Not f)
Not(Af f) Eg(Not f)
Not(Eg f) Af(Not f)
Not(Y f) Y(Not f) (weak/strong handled at eval, ## 22)
  • Ownership: parent owns children through Box; dropping a Formula recursively frees the tree (no cycles -> no leaks). Clone deep-copies (cheap relative to checking cost).
  • Mutation: none — transforms (nnf, simplify) return fresh trees.
  • Concurrency: Formula: Send + Sync; share by &Formula / Arc<Formula> across the CTL, automata, eval, and monitor paths simultaneously.
  • Serialization: tl-syntax pretty-printer renders Formula back to source text honoring precedence (X/F/G/Y/O/H unary; U/R/S binary; boolean); printed text re-parses to a structurally equal Formula.

11.3 tl-trace — words and structures

/// A single observed state: which atomic propositions hold here.
/// Bitset indexed by Ap; bit i set <=> AP i is true. Fixed-width per run.
pub struct State { pub aps: Bitset }              // Bitset = u64 words; |Ap| up to a few thousand

/// A trace = a finite prefix of States, optionally a lasso (infinite word).
/// loop_start = Some(i) => the suffix prefix[i..] repeats forever (omega-word);
/// loop_start = None     => a genuinely finite word (LTLf / monitoring prefix).
/// Immutable after construction.
pub struct Trace { pub prefix: Vec<State>, pub loop_start: Option<usize> }

/// An explicit-state Kripke structure: the model for CTL + automata-theoretic LTL.
/// states[i].aps = labeling of state i; init = initial state indices;
/// edges[i] = successors of state i (the transition relation).
/// Immutable after construction; validated for a TOTAL transition relation.
pub struct KripkeStructure {
    pub states: Vec<State>,
    pub init: Vec<usize>,
    pub edges: Vec<Vec<usize>>,                   // successors; edges[i] ⊆ 0..states.len()
}
Type Ownership Validation (## 15) Notes
State owns Bitset (inline words) bit indices < n_aps Copy-cheap if small; Clone otherwise
Trace owns Vec<State> loop_start < prefix.len() when Some; prefix non-empty lasso = Some; finite = None
KripkeStructure owns all Vecs every init,edges[i][j] in range; every state has ≥1 successor (total) self-loops allowed; multi-init allowed
  • Mutation: none after build; checkers/evaluators read by &.
  • Concurrency: all three Send + Sync; one &KripkeStructure feeds many parallel CtlChecker / product builds.
  • Serialization: Trace/KripkeStructure parse from the CLI's model format; states emit as AP lists. No omega-word infinite expansion in memory — the lasso is the finite representation.

11.4 tl-automata — omega-automata, construction, emptiness

/// A boolean condition over APs guarding an automaton edge.
/// Stored as a small DNF / bitset (mask = APs that matter, care = required polarity);
/// a State satisfies a Guard iff (state.aps & mask) == (care & mask). Immutable.
pub struct Guard(/* mask+care bitset, or small DNF of literals */);

/// Generalized Buchi automaton (GPVW output, ## 18): a SET of accepting sets.
/// nstates indices 0..nstates; edges[s] = labeled transitions; init = start states;
/// accept = one Vec<usize> per generalized acceptance condition (one per U-subformula).
/// Acceptance: a run is accepting iff it visits EACH accept[k] infinitely often.
/// Immutable after construction.
pub struct Ngba {
    pub nstates: usize,
    pub init: Vec<usize>,
    pub edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard, usize)>>,
    pub accept: Vec<Vec<usize>>,                  // accept.len() = number of acceptance sets
}

/// Buchi automaton (degeneralized, ## 19): ONE accepting set.
/// Acceptance: a run is accepting iff it visits accept infinitely often.
/// Immutable after construction.
pub struct Nba {
    pub nstates: usize,
    pub init: Vec<usize>,
    pub edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard, usize)>>,
    pub accept: Vec<usize>,
}

Construction, product, and search over the two automaton types above:

/// GPVW on-the-fly tableau: LTL Formula (in NNF) -> generalized Buchi automaton.
/// Nodes = obligation sets over closure(f), split now/next; one accept set per Until.
/// Built lazily; deterministic node identity -> stable nstates.
pub fn ltl_to_ngba(f: &Formula) -> Ngba;

/// Degeneralization (counter construction): Ngba with k accept sets ->
/// Nba on (state x counter in 0..k). |Nba.nstates| ≤ k * |Ngba.nstates|.
pub fn degeneralize(g: &Ngba) -> Nba;

/// Synchronous product Nba x KripkeStructure -> Nba over (auto-state x kripke-state).
/// An edge exists iff the automaton Guard holds in the Kripke state's labeling and
/// the Kripke edge is present. Accept = product states whose automaton component accepts.
pub fn product(a: &Nba, k: &KripkeStructure) -> Nba;

/// Emptiness via NESTED DFS: Some(lasso) = a reachable accepting cycle exists
/// (an accepting run / counterexample); None = language is empty.
/// The DFS run is the only MUTABLE artifact here (two color arrays + two stacks).
pub fn emptiness(a: &Nba) -> Option<Lasso>;

/// A counterexample run: stem leads into the cycle; cycle repeats forever.
/// Indices refer to the automaton `a` passed to emptiness (product states for a model check).
pub struct Lasso { pub stem: Vec<usize>, pub cycle: Vec<usize> }

Size / cost targets (Phase 13 budgets, validated vs Spot):

Quantity Bound / target
Ngba.nstates for f ≤ 2^O(
Nba.nstates after degeneralize ≤ k ·
product states ≤
emptiness time O(
Lasso length stem + cycle ≤
  • Ownership: index-based graphs (usize ids into Vecs); no pointers, no Rc, no cycles in the owning structure -> trivially Send + Sync and leak-free.
  • Mutation: Ngba/Nba/Guard/Lasso immutable; emptiness allocates transient DFS state internally and returns an owned Lasso.
  • Serialization: Ngba/Nba -> HOA (header: states, init, AP list, Inf/generalized acceptance; body: edges with Guard as a boolean over AP indices) and dot (for tl to-buchi). HOA round-trips against Spot for differential acceptance comparison.

11.5 tl-eval — CTL labeling, LTLf, automata-theoretic LTL

/// Explicit-state CTL model checker. Borrows the model read-only for its lifetime.
/// Construct once per KripkeStructure; reuse across many formulas.
pub struct CtlChecker<'a> { /* &'a KripkeStructure */ }

impl CtlChecker<'_> {
    /// Per-state truth labeling: result[i] = does state i satisfy f.
    /// EX = preimage; E[U] = least fixpoint; EG = greatest fixpoint via SCC quotient;
    /// AX/AG/AF/EF/A[U] derived by duality. Bottom-up over subformulas. Read-only.
    pub fn sat(&self, f: &Formula) -> Vec<bool>;

    /// holds iff EVERY init state is labeled by sat(f).
    pub fn holds(&self, f: &Formula) -> bool;
}

/// Finite-trace LTL (LTLf) by formula progression over a finite Trace.
/// Folds progress() across prefix; applies the end-of-trace finite semantics
/// (weak/strong next) to yield a DEFINITIVE bool. Pure.
pub fn eval_ltlf(f: &Formula, t: &Trace) -> bool;

/// Automata-theoretic LTL model check over a KripkeStructure.
/// Ok(())   = K satisfies (A f)  [product(Nba(nnf(Not f)), K) is empty];
/// Err(cex) = a counterexample lasso (an infinite run of K violating f).
/// Internally: nnf -> ltl_to_ngba(Not f) -> degeneralize -> product -> emptiness.
pub fn check_ltl(f: &Formula, k: &KripkeStructure) -> Result<(), Lasso>;

CTL labeling correspondence (## 17), one row per primitive:

Call Semantics
sat(Ex p) states with ≥1 successor in sat(p) (preimage)
sat(Eu a b) least fixpoint: sat(b) ∪ {a-states with a successor already in set}
sat(Eg p) greatest fixpoint: p-states on a cycle within the sat(p) sub-Kripke (via SCCs)
Ax/Ag/Af/Ef/Au derived from Ex/Eu/Eg by duality
  • Ownership: CtlChecker<'a> holds &'a KripkeStructure — borrow-checked, no copy of the model; multiple checkers (and parallel threads) may borrow the same model.
  • Mutation: none externally visible; sat allocates per-subformula label vectors transiently.
  • Concurrency: sat/holds/eval_ltlf/check_ltl are pure read-only -> safe to call concurrently over shared &Formula + &KripkeStructure.
  • Serialization: results are Vec<bool> / bool / Result<(), Lasso>; the Lasso renders to a human-readable trace + (via tl-trace) a Trace witness for replay.

11.6 tl-monitor — runtime verification verdicts + incremental progression

/// 2-valued definitive verdict (a settled monitor outcome).
pub enum Verdict { True, False }

/// RV-LTL / LTL3 verdict over a finite observed prefix.
/// LTL3 uses {True, False, Unknown}; RV-LTL refines Unknown into
/// PresumablyTrue / PresumablyFalse via a finite-semantics tie-break.
pub enum RvVerdict { True, False, PresumablyTrue, PresumablyFalse, Unknown }

/// A streaming monitor: the only MUTABLE core type.
/// State = the current progressed Formula (the "residual") OR a monitor-automaton
/// state. Owns its evolving state; single-stream owner (Send, not shared).
pub struct Monitor { /* current progressed Formula or monitor-automaton state */ }

impl Monitor {
    /// Consume one event (State); advance internal state; return the current verdict.
    /// A residual of True/False => definitive (True/False); else Presumably*/Unknown.
    pub fn step(&mut self, s: &State) -> RvVerdict;

    /// The LTL "derivative": one progression step of f under observation s.
    /// progress(X f, s)=f; progress(f U g, s)=progress(g,s) ∨ (progress(f,s) ∧ (f U g));
    /// progress(G f, s)=progress(f,s) ∧ G f; progress(Atom p, s)=True/False by s. Pure.
    pub fn progress(f: &Formula, s: &State) -> Formula;
}

Verdict lattice (LOCKED meaning; ## 21):

RvVerdict Condition over observed prefix Settled?
True every infinite extension satisfies f (good prefix) yes (= Verdict::True)
False no infinite extension satisfies f (bad prefix) yes (= Verdict::False)
PresumablyTrue undecided; finite-semantics favors true no (RV-LTL only)
PresumablyFalse undecided; finite-semantics favors false no (RV-LTL only)
Unknown undecided; LTL3 (no tie-break) no
  • Ownership: Monitor owns its residual Formula / automaton-state index; step replaces it in place (&mut self). progress is an associated pure function (no self) used to build/advance the residual.
  • Mutation: step mutates; this and the nested-DFS run in emptiness are the only mutable artifacts in the system — everything else is immutable-after-construction.
  • Concurrency: Monitor: Send (move to a stream-owning thread) but not designed for &mut sharing; one monitor per event stream. The underlying monitor automaton (if synthesized) is immutable + Sync and may back many monitors.
  • Serialization: a synthesized monitor automaton emits to HOA/dot like any Nba; the live residual Formula pretty-prints for debugging a stuck/Unknown monitor.

11.7 Type dependency / data-flow summary

tl-formula:  Ap, Formula, nnf, closure, Kind          (immutable AST; Send+Sync)
   |  &Formula
   +-> tl-automata: Guard, Ngba, Nba, Lasso,
   |                ltl_to_ngba -> degeneralize -> product -> emptiness   (immutable graphs)
   +-> tl-eval:     CtlChecker(&KripkeStructure).sat/holds,
   |                eval_ltlf(&Trace), check_ltl -> Result<(), Lasso>     (read-only)
   +-> tl-monitor:  Verdict, RvVerdict, Monitor.step(&mut), progress      (Monitor mutates)
tl-trace:    State(Bitset), Trace(prefix+loop_start), KripkeStructure     (immutable; Send+Sync)

Normative contract: phases ## 12–## 25 MUST implement against these exact names and signatures; any divergence is a spec violation, not an implementation choice.

0.6 Pitfalls

  • no_std leak via dev-deps: a [dev-dependencies] pulling std masks a real std leak in the lib. Root cause: dev-deps compile with default features. Debug: the dedicated --no-default-features CI build (A2) on the lib target only.
  • Recursive enum needs Box: Formula::Not(Formula) without Box is infinitely sized → E0072. Every recursive child is Box<Formula> per spine; verify by compiling, not reading.
  • todo!() reached by tests: a stub on the smoke path aborts at runtime, not compile. Debug: smoke test exercises only G+atom+eval_ltlf; keep all other paths out of it.
  • MSRV drift: an edition-2021 lib using a 1.79+ API still builds on dev's stable but breaks A6. Debug: pin rust-version=1.78; run CI matrix on 1.78 explicitly.
  • Spot version/format skew: older ltl2tgba lacks -H (HOA). Root cause: Spot <2.x. Mitigation: oracle probes ltl2tgba --version; gate behind feature; never block CI on Spot.
  • AP interning identity: parsing "p" twice must yield the same Ap. Root cause: per-parse symbol table not shared. For Phase 0 single-formula scope this is trivial; flagged so Phase 1 reuses one interner.

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • Phase 0 has no perf targets; only build hygiene. Cold cargo build --workspace ≤ 30 s on CI; incremental ≤ 3 s.
  • Bitset shell: choose u64-word SmallVec/Box<[u64]> representation now (defer impl) so State stays Copy-cheap for ≤64 APs later — fixed-word inline path for the common case.
  • Avoid #[derive(Debug)] recursion blowups later: fine now; note for large Formula/automata in Phase 6+.

0.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty trace Trace{prefix:[], loop_start:None} → eval_ltlf must not index [0]; Phase 0 documents it returns by LTLf end-of-trace rule (decided in Phase 4) — smoke test uses non-empty only.
  • Atom id 0 valid (not a sentinel); Ap=u32::MAX reserved as "no AP" if ever needed — Phase 0 does not use it.
  • Whitespace-only / empty input "" → Err(UnexpectedEof), no panic.
  • Single-state lasso loop_start:Some(0) over a finite trace: valid (a 1-state cycle); used by the smoke fixture.

0.9 Failure Analysis

failure symptom containment
std leaked into core A2 build error E0463/E0432 per-crate --no-default-features gate
smoke verdict wrong A3 assert fail minimal path is the only non-stub code; bisect lexer→parser→eval
clippy/fmt regression A4/A5 nonzero exit -D warnings makes it blocking, not advisory
Dockerfile diverges from CI "works on CI, not in image" image runs the same make ci target
silent verdict flip (future) none in Phase 0 oracle scaffold landed now so Phase 13 differential test is ready

0.10 Operational Notes

  • Makefile targets: make build, make test, make lint (=fmt --check + clippy -D warnings), make ci (= build+lint+test, the single source of truth invoked by both GitHub Actions and Docker).
  • Dockerfile: FROM rust:1.78-slim; optionally apt-get install spot to enable --features spot-oracle; default image builds without Spot so CI is hermetic.
  • CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml): matrix {stable, 1.78} × {default, --no-default-features}; steps = checkout → toolchain → make ci. Cache ~/.cargo + target/.
  • rust-toolchain.toml pins 1.78 with components rustfmt,clippy for reproducible local runs.
  • Binary name is exactly tl; cargo run -p tl-cli -- eval --formula "G p" --trace <file> is the canonical manual smoke invocation.

13. Phase 1 — LTL syntax, Formula AST, parser, and NNF

End-to-end LTL front end: tl-syntax (Lexer/Parser/ParseError) + tl-formula (Formula AST, Ap interning, nnf()). Out of scope here: CTL operators (Phase 2, ## 14), past operators Y/O/H/S (deferred to AST variants but no surface syntax yet), models/eval. Reference: standard LTL grammar à la Spot's ltlparse; NNF dualities per Vardi-Wolper.

1.1 Objectives

  • Lex LTL source -> Vec<Token> with byte spans; intern AP identifiers to Ap = u32 via an AtomTable; Pratt/precedence-parse text -> Formula (LTL fragment only: True False Atom Not And Or X U R F G); operator precedence + associativity LOCKED (table in 1.2); imp/iff/xor desugared to core Not/And/Or at parse time.
  • nnf(&Formula) -> Formula: push Not to atoms via dualities; idempotent (nnf(nnf(f)) == nnf(f)).
  • ParseError { kind: ParseErrorKind, span: Span } with caret diagnostics; no panics on any input (incl. empty, binary garbage); no_std + alloc for both crates; pretty-printer round-trips (parse(print(f)) ≡ f modulo redundant parens).

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-04 (unified Formula enum), ADR-05 (NNF precondition), P1/P3 (GPVW/nested-DFS assume NNF input), B1§5.1 (LTL operator set), B3§3.2 (NNF dualities) -->

Files: tl-syntax/src/{lib,token,lexer,parser,error,pretty}.rs; tl-formula/src/{lib,ast,nnf,closure}.rs (closure stub here, filled Phase 5/6).

enum Token {
  True, False, Ident(Ap),                 // 'true'/'1', 'false'/'0', interned AP
  Not, And, Or, Imp, Iff, Xor,            // ! & | -> <-> ^
  X, U, R, F, G,                          // next until release eventually globally
  LParen, RParen, Eof,
}
struct Span { lo: u32, hi: u32 }          // byte offsets into source
  • Lexer: longest-match; keywords case-sensitive (X U R F G reserved -> NOT valid AP names). AP ident regex [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]* minus reserved; interned into AtomTable: { map: BTreeMap<String,Ap>, names: Vec<String> }. ASCII + Unicode aliases: □ G, ◊ F, ○ X, ¬ !, ∧ &, ∨ |, → ->, ↔ <->; multi-char punctuation (->, <->) maximal-munch before single. Parser (parser.rs): precedence-climbing; unary (Not X F G) right-assoc, bind tightest; binary by table below.
Operator Surface Prec Assoc Class
Not X F G ! X F G 7 (tightest) right (prefix) unary temporal/bool
U R U R 5 right binary temporal
And & 4 left boolean
Or | 3 left boolean
Imp -> 2 right desugared
Iff <-> 1 (loosest) left desugared
  • Desugar at AST build: a -> b ⇒ Or(Not a, b); a <-> b ⇒ And(Or(Not a,b), Or(Not b,a)); a ^ b ⇒ Or(And(a,Not b), And(Not a,b)); core AST never stores Imp/Iff/Xor. Formula built via smart ctors mk_and/mk_or/mk_not that Box children; no constant folding here (Phase 12 owns rewrite).
  • nnf.rs recurrence (dualities, single bottom-up pass, returns owned Formula):
nnf(Not True)=False; nnf(Not False)=True; nnf(Not(Atom p))=Not(Atom p)   // sink
nnf(Not(Not f))=nnf(f); nnf(_)=recurse-into-children                     // positive cases fall through
nnf(Not(And a b))=Or(nnf(Not a), nnf(Not b))     // De Morgan; Or is the dual case
nnf(Not(X f))=X(nnf(Not f))                      // !X = X!
nnf(Not(U a b))=R(nnf(Not a), nnf(Not b)); nnf(Not(R a b))=U(nnf(Not a), nnf(Not b))
nnf(Not(F f))=G(nnf(Not f)); nnf(Not(G f))=F(nnf(Not f))
  • API surface: Parser::parse(src:&str, tab:&mut AtomTable) -> Result<Formula, ParseError>; tl_syntax::parse_with_table; tl_formula::nnf. Pretty-printer emits minimal parens by precedence.

1.3 Deliverable

  • tl-syntax + tl-formula compile no_std + alloc, zero warnings (-D warnings); parse("G (p -> X q) U !r", &mut tab) -> Formula; nnf of it pushes all Not to atoms.
  • Pretty-printer round-trips on the conformance corpus; tl eval/to-buchi not wired yet but parse callable from tl-cli smoke path (extends Phase 0 smoke: parse "G p" succeeds); cargo test -p tl-syntax -p tl-formula green; doctest on nnf signature.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Target
A1 All 100+ corpus formulas parse 0 errors
A2 Round-trip parse(print(f))≡f (AST equality mod parens) 100%
A3 nnf idempotent on corpus nnf(nnf f)==nnf(f) ∀
A4 nnf output has Not only on Atom structural check passes ∀
A5 Every malformed fixture -> correct ParseErrorKind + span byte-exact span
A6 No panic on 1M random byte strings + empty input 0 panics (fuzz)
A7 Precedence: !a U b & c parses as (!a) U (b & c) matches golden AST

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Dir tl-syntax/tests/fixtures/phase1/. Each <name>.ltl + <name>.ast (S-expr golden) + <name>.nnf.

Fixture Input Expected
prec_until_and !a U b & c U(Not(Atom a), And(Atom b, Atom c))
imp_desugar p -> X q Or(Not(Atom p), X(Atom q))
nnf_until !(p U q) nnf -> R(Not(Atom p), Not(Atom q))
nnf_double_neg !!p nnf -> Atom p
unicode □◊p G(F(Atom p))
right_assoc_U a U b U c U(Atom a, U(Atom b, Atom c))

Malformed (*.err, expect ParseErrorKind + span): unbalanced (p & q -> UnclosedParen@5; dangling_binop p U -> UnexpectedEof@3; reserved_as_ap X alone -> ExpectedOperand@0..1; empty "" -> EmptyInput@0; bad_char p $ q -> UnexpectedChar@2; double_op p & & q -> ExpectedOperand@4.

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Detect / debug
U/R wrong associativity flips automaton later left vs right assoc ambiguity golden-AST test right_assoc_U; print AST S-expr
Reserved F/G/X lexed as AP keyword check after ident scan, not before unit test parse("F") must be F(operand) not Atom("F")
NNF non-termination / stack blow on deep !!!!… Not(Not f) peels one layer but recurses on f not nnf(f) property test: depth-10k negation chain, bounded recursion or explicit worklist
Span off-by-one after Unicode alias multi-byte □ counted as 1 char not 3 bytes spans are byte offsets; assert against UTF-8 byte index in unicode.err
imp/iff desugar duplicates subtree -> blowup <-> expands operand twice via Box::clone acceptable in Phase 1; flag for Phase 12 sharing; cap test formula <-> nesting
nnf forgets F/G duals -> Not(G p) survives incomplete match arm A4 structural scan rejects any Not over non-Atom

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Parse O(n) in token count, lexer single pass no backtracking, target 10k-token formula < 2 ms; nnf O(|f|) nodes single bottom-up pass, no memoization needed at Phase-1 sizes (DAG sharing deferred to Phase 12); AtomTable lookup BTreeMap O(log k), k (distinct APs) typically < 64 -> negligible, switch to FxHashMap only if profiling shows a hotspot (keep no_std constraint -> custom hasher); pretty-printer allocates one String, reserve capacity ≈ 2*|f| to avoid realloc churn; Box<Formula> per node = heap alloc per subformula, tolerable now — arena/Rc interning is a Phase 12 optimization, not Phase 1.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty input "" -> EmptyInput, not panic; constants only: true, false, !true -> nnf = False/True; maximal nesting: 50k-deep X(X(...)) -> parser must not stack-overflow (iterative descent or explicit operand stack).
  • Redundant parens (((p))) -> Atom p, pretty-printer drops them; adjacent unary !!Xp, GF p (no parens) -> right-assoc chain.
  • AP name collision with Unicode-alias spelling (e.g. AP literally box) -> still valid ident, only glyphs □◊○ are operators; whitespace/\t/\n between tokens ignored, no statement separator (single formula per parse).

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong precedence/assoc, or an incomplete nnf dual, = SILENT semantic corruption -> wrong Formula/automaton/verdict downstream (the GRAVEST class per spine); guard: golden-AST corpus + Phase 13 differential vs Spot; A4's structural scan is the nnf-side firewall.
  • Lexer accepting a reserved word as AP -> formula means something else entirely; caught by reserved-word unit tests + round-trip. Span errors degrade diagnostics only (not correctness) but erode user trust; byte-exact span fixtures gate this.
  • Recovery policy: parser is fail-fast (first error, no error-recovery resync) — return single ParseError; multi-error recovery is out of scope.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tl eval / tl to-buchi accept --formula <str>; parse errors print error[Kind]: msg + source line + caret at span (rustc-style), exit code 2; feature flag unicode-aliases (default on) toggles □◊○¬∧∨ lexing for ASCII-only environments.
  • RUST_LOG=tl_syntax=debug (via a log facade behind a std-only tl-cli feature) dumps token stream + AST S-expr; debug helper Formula::to_sexpr() (behind cfg(test)/debug feature) emits the golden S-expr format used by fixtures — single canonical serializer shared by tests to avoid drift.
  • AST/NNF are the contract for Phases 2 (CTL adds variants), 4 (progress), 6 (GPVW consumes NNF over closure); any change to Formula variants or NNF dualities is an ADR-level change.

14. Phase 2 — CTL syntax, AST, and well-formedness

2.1 Objectives

  • Extend tl-syntax + tl-formula to CTL: fused path-quantifier ops Ex/Ax/Eg/Ag/Ef/Af/Eu/Au (no bare E/A).
  • Parse E[p U q] / A[p U q] (bracketed binary CTL until) into Formula::Eu/Au; parse EX/AX/EG/AG/EF/AF prefix unaries.
  • Enforce state-vs-path well-formedness: in CTL every temporal op (X/U/R/F/G) must sit directly under a path quantifier; no nesting two temporals without an intervening quantifier; no bare path quantifier.
  • Classify any parsed Formula -> Kind { Ltl, Ctl, PastLtl, Ltlf } by which operator families appear (mutually exclusive checks).
  • NNF for CTL: dualities not Ex = Ag not, not Ef = Ag not (= not Eu(true,·)), pushing negation to atoms preserving the fixed op set.
  • Reject ill-formed CTL with a precise ParseError/WfError (offset + kind + offending op). No silent acceptance — a wrong Kind mislabels downstream (CtlChecker vs automata path).

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-04 (Kind well-formedness), ADR-05 (CTL NNF dualities), B3§3.6 (state-vs-path formula grammar), P4/CES-86 (CTL operator set Ex/Ax/Eg/Ag/Ef/Af/Eu/Au) -->
Crate / file Change
tl-syntax/src/token.rs Add Token::Ex Ax Eg Ag Ef Af E A LBracket RBracket; lexer recognizes 2-char ops EX EG EF AX AG AF greedily before single E/A.
tl-syntax/src/parser.rs Pratt: CTL prefix unaries (EX..AF) bind like LTL X (prec 50); E[/A[ open bracketed-until parse: E LBracket f U g RBracket -> Eu(f,g). parse_ctl_until reads inner U only (R/W rejected inside brackets).
tl-syntax/src/lib.rs parse(src) -> Result<Formula, ParseError> unchanged signature; CTL/LTL share one entry, distinguished post-parse by Kind.
tl-formula/src/wf.rs (new) pub fn classify(f:&Formula)->Result<Kind,WfError>; fn check_ctl(f:&Formula)->Result<(),WfError> walks AST tracking under_quantifier:bool.
tl-formula/src/kind.rs (new) bitflag scan: has_ltl_temporal, has_ctl, has_past; map combos -> Kind; conflicting families -> WfError::Mixed.
tl-formula/src/nnf.rs extend nnf() match arms with CTL dualities (table below).
tl-formula/src/error.rs (new) pub enum WfError { PathOpOutsideQuantifier{op,span}, BareQuantifier{span}, MixedKinds{a,b}, NestedTemporalNoQuantifier{span} }.

Walk invariant (check_ctl): descending into a quantifier child sets under_quantifier=true for exactly one temporal level; descending through boolean ops resets to false. A X/U/R/F/G node seen with under_quantifier==false -> PathOpOutsideQuantifier.

CTL NNF dualities (push negation inward):

not Ex f  = Ax (not f)        not Ax f  = Ex (not f)
not Eg f  = Af (not f)        not Ag f  = Ef (not f)
not Ef f  = Ag (not f)        not Af f  = Eg (not f)
not Eu(f,g) = Aw(not f, not g)   // weak-until via released form; canonicalize to Au/Ax per Clarke-Emerson
not Au(f,g) = Eu(not g, not(f or g)) or Eg(not g)   // standard CTL negation identity

2.3 Deliverable

  • tl parse "AG(req -> AF ack)" -> typed Formula + Kind::Ctl, pretty-printed back identically (round-trip).
  • tl parse "AG(F p)" (path op under no quantifier between G and F) -> WfError::NestedTemporalNoQuantifier with span on F.
  • classify returns the correct Kind for LTL / CTL / past-LTL / LTLf-flagged inputs; mixed -> typed error.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

ID Criterion Measure
A1 All 8 CTL ops parse + round-trip 24 fixtures, byte-identical re-print after nnf-free pretty
A2 Well-formed CTL accepted, ill-formed rejected precision/recall = 1.0 on fixture corpus (40 ok / 30 bad)
A3 Kind classification correct 100% on 50 mixed-family fixtures
A4 NNF idempotent + preserves Kind nnf(nnf(f))==nnf(f); classify(nnf f)==classify(f) for all CTL fixtures
A5 Error carries exact byte span of offending op span matches hand-annotated offset, all 30 bad cases

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
ctl_ok_until.tl E[p U q] Eu(Atom,Atom), Kind::Ctl
ctl_ok_response.tl AG(p -> AF q) accept, Kind::Ctl
ctl_ok_nested_q.tl EX EG p (quantifier each level) accept
ctl_bad_bare_e.tl E p WfError::BareQuantifier{span@0}
ctl_bad_nested_temporal.tl AG(F p) NestedTemporalNoQuantifier{span on F}
ctl_bad_path_top.tl F EX p PathOpOutsideQuantifier{op:F,span@0}
ctl_bad_until_release.tl E[p R q] ParseError (R illegal in brackets)
kind_mixed.tl AG p U q WfError::MixedKinds{Ctl,Ltl}
kind_past_ctl.tl AG(O p) MixedKinds{Ctl,PastLtl}

Malformed: unbalanced E[p U q (no ]), A[p U q U r] (two U), empty bracket E[], EXX p (double op no operand between).

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Greedy 2-char lex: EXp vs EX p vs E X p — EX is one token; an identifier Exit must NOT lex as Ex+it. Root cause: keyword/identifier overlap. Debug: lexer must require word boundary after EX/EG/...; dump token stream.
  • Eu/Au are NOT Ex(U(..)): CTL until is a single fused node; building Ex(Box::new(U(..))) silently passes check_ctl but is wrong semantics for CtlChecker. Root cause: reusing LTL U arm. Debug: assert no Formula::U survives in a Kind::Ctl tree.
  • NNF not Au/not Eu identity errors: the negation of A[f U g] is subtle (involves Eg(not g) disjunct); a naive Eu(not f, not g) flips verdicts with no crash — gravest class. Debug: differential-check NNF vs un-normalized labeling on small Kripkes.
  • under_quantifier reset bug: forgetting to reset through boolean ops accepts EX(p & G q) (the G has no quantifier). Root cause: passing flag down through And/Or. Debug: unit-test each boolean op resets to false.
  • Kind over-eager: a formula with only booleans+atoms is Ltl (vacuously) and also valid CTL — pick Ltl as the canonical neutral kind; document, else classify is nondeterministic.
  • CTL*-shaped input (E(p U (X q)), path formula under one E): out of scope (non-goal), must reject as NestedTemporalNoQuantifier, not parse as CTL*.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • classify + check_ctl: single O(n) AST walk, n = node count; no allocation (flag passed by value). Target < 1 µs for formulas ≤ 200 nodes.
  • NNF: O(n) one pass, may grow size ≤ 2× via not Au expansion; cap expansion, warn if NNF > 4× input (pathological nested negation).
  • Parser: O(tokens); bracketed-until adds no backtracking (LL(1) on E[/A[). Token-stream buffer reused across parse calls in CLI.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • EX true, AG false, E[true U p] (≡ EF p) — accept; nnf may simplify E[true U p]->Ef p? No — keep structural in this phase, simplification is Phase 12.
  • Deeply nested alternation AX EX AX EX p — accept, each temporal has its own quantifier.
  • AF AG p (CTL stabilization pattern) — accept.
  • Negated quantifier at top !EF bad — NNF -> Ag(not bad), Kind::Ctl preserved.
  • Atom named like a keyword token suffix (force, expand) — must not partially lex as Ef/Ex.
  • Empty formula / lone atom -> Kind::Ltl (neutral), no WfError.

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius
Wrong Kind (CTL labeled Ltl) A3 fixtures; CLI routes to wrong engine sends CTL to ltl_to_ngba -> garbage automaton / panic
Wrong NNF not Au identity A4 + differential vs labeling silent verdict flip in Phase 5 CtlChecker
check_ctl false-accept (nested temporal) A2 recall malformed AST reaches sat(), unreachable! panic or wrong labeling
Lex collision (EX/identifier) A1 round-trip mismatch parse error or wrong AST on valid input
Span off-by-one in WfError A5 misleading user diagnostics (non-fatal)

2.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI surfacing: tl prints WfError with caret under the offending span (reuse Phase 1 ParseError rendering); exit code 2 for well-formedness vs 1 for syntax.
  • Logging: RUST_LOG=tl_formula::wf=debug traces the under_quantifier walk per node.
  • tl parse --kind <f> debug subcommand prints just the classified Kind for fixture triage.
  • Property test (proptest): generate random CTL trees with quantifier-balanced temporals -> classify must yield Ctl and check_ctl Ok; inject one orphan temporal -> must error. Seeds checked into tl-formula/tests/.
  • Invariant assertions are debug_assert! only (zero release cost); CI runs the debug build for fixtures.

15. Phase 3 — Trace and Kripke models

tl-trace: the word/structure model every evaluator consumes. State (AP bitset), Trace (finite prefix + optional lasso loop_start, encoding omega-words), KripkeStructure (states/init/edges/labeling). Validation enforces a total transition relation and well-formed lassos. No logic, no automata — pure model layer + invariant checks. Depends only on core bitset + tl-formula::Ap.

3.1 Objectives

  • State { aps: Bitset } — AP valuation; holds(ap) -> bool, len/popcount, Eq/Hash/Ord for state dedup + product hashing.
  • Trace { prefix: Vec<State>, loop_start: Option<usize> } — None = finite word (LTLf); Some(i) = lasso, suffix prefix[i..] repeats forever → omega-word prefix[0..i]·(prefix[i..])^ω; Trace::at(t) unrolls the lasso for any t: usize.
  • KripkeStructure { states, init, edges, labeling } — explicit-state graph; edges[s] = successors, labeling via per-state State; successors(s)/predecessors() (for CTL preimage, Phase 5). validate() on both types → reject malformed inputs before evaluation; the gravest silent-verdict-flip bug class starts at a malformed model accepted as valid.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-10 (Bitset AP valuation), ADR-11 (arena storage, usize ids), INV-4 (total transition relation), INV-5 (well-formed lasso loop_start) -->
