Files (crate tl-trace, no_std + alloc, per section 10 layout): lib.rs re-exports State/Trace/KripkeStructure/TraceError/KripkeError (#![no_std], extern crate alloc); bitset.rs — Bitset (dense u64-word vector or inline u128), set/clear/get, popcount, iter_ones, Eq/Hash/Ord; state.rs — State, ctors from_aps(&[Ap])/from_mask, holds(Ap), Display (pretty {p,q}); trace.rs — Trace, loop_start, is_finite/is_lasso, at(usize), len_prefix, loop_len, validate; kripke.rs — KripkeStructure, predecessors() builder, validate, reachable() (BFS), DOT export to_dot(); error.rs — TraceError, KripkeError (+ Kind enums). Core signatures follow (illustrative bodies ≤2 lines):

pub struct State { pub aps: Bitset }
impl State {
    pub fn from_aps(aps: &[Ap]) -> Self;      // sets each bit
    pub fn holds(&self, ap: Ap) -> bool;      // self.aps.get(ap as usize)
}
pub struct Trace { pub prefix: Vec<State>, pub loop_start: Option<usize> }
impl Trace {
    pub fn is_lasso(&self) -> bool;           // loop_start.is_some()
    pub fn loop_len(&self) -> Option<usize>;  // prefix.len() - i
    pub fn at(&self, t: usize) -> &State;     // unroll: t<n -> prefix[t]; else lasso index
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), TraceError>;
}
pub struct KripkeStructure {
    pub states: Vec<State>, pub init: Vec<usize>, pub edges: Vec<Vec<usize>>,
}
impl KripkeStructure {
    pub fn nstates(&self) -> usize;           // states.len()
    pub fn successors(&self, s: usize) -> &[usize];
    pub fn predecessors(&self) -> Vec<Vec<usize>>;   // inverse edges, cached by caller
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), KripkeError>;
}

Trace::at(t) unroll for lasso Some(i), prefix length n: if t < n { prefix[t] } else { prefix[i + (t - n) % (n - i)] } (finite trace + out-of-range t → panic/debug-assert, caller must bound by len_prefix). State labeling is embedded in states[s] (each Kripke state is a State) — no separate label map; the canonical type has states: Vec<State>, so KripkeStructure::labeling(s) == states[s].

3.3 Deliverable

tl-trace crate compiling on stable + MSRV 1.78, no_std: construct a Trace (finite or lasso) and a KripkeStructure programmatically, call validate(), and feed them to downstream evaluators (Phase 4 eval_ltlf, Phase 5 CtlChecker, Phase 8 product). to_dot() renders a Kripke structure. Phase-0 smoke test still green: 1-state trace where p holds.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
validate() total-relation check every s has edges[s].nonempty() → else KripkeError::DeadState(s)
Lasso well-formed loop_start = Some(i) ⇒ i < prefix.len() & prefix.nonempty() → else TraceError
at(t) correctness at(n + loop_len*k + j) == prefix[i+j] ∀ k, 0≤j<loop_len (proptest 10⁴ cases)
State dedup identical AP sets ⇒ equal hash/eq; product (Phase 8) relies on it
Construction throughput build + validate a 10⁶-state Kripke (avg out-degree 4) in < 200 ms, < 80 MB
Coverage ≥ 95 % line coverage on tl-trace; all error variants exercised

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

tl-trace/tests/fixtures/, each .kripke/.trace text + .expected (validate result + at() samples / DOT):

Fixture Input Expected
trace_finite.trace prefix [{p},{},{q}], no loop valid; is_finite; at(0)={p}
trace_lasso.trace prefix [{p},{q}], loop_start=1 valid; loop_len=1; at(5)={q}
trace_lasso_self.trace prefix [{p}], loop_start=0 valid; stutters {p} forever
kripke_2state.kripke s0→s1, s1→s0, init s0 valid; total relation
kripke_dot.expected DOT of kripke_2state matches golden DOT byte-for-byte
bad_lasso_oob.trace loop_start=3, prefix len 2 TraceError::LoopStartOutOfRange{i:3,len:2}
bad_empty_lasso.trace prefix [], loop_start=0 TraceError::EmptyLassoPrefix
bad_dead_state.kripke s1 has no successors KripkeError::DeadState(1)
bad_init_oob.kripke init=[5], 3 states KripkeError::InitOutOfRange(5)
bad_edge_oob.kripke edge s0→7, 3 states KripkeError::EdgeTarget{from:0,to:7}

3.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Detection / debug
Empty-prefix lasso silently "valid" loop_start checked vs len but len==0 slips when i==0 guard prefix.nonempty() first; fixture bad_empty_lasso
at(t) off-by-one in modular index wrong loop_len = n instead of n-i proptest at(n+loop_len)==at(n); assert period exactly
Dead state accepted → CTL EG/nested-DFS hang or wrong fixpoint missing total-relation check; LTL automata theory assumes total Kripke validate rejects; if skipped, infinite words don't exist → spurious "holds"
State Eq ignores trailing zero words Bitset of different word-length compares unequal for same APs canonicalize: trim trailing zero words on construction
Self-loop confused with finite end finite trace and lasso loop_start=last both end at same state is_finite vs is_lasso keyed on loop_start.is_some(), never on shape
Bitset capacity < max Ap id AP interned beyond preallocated width → silent false for high APs size Bitset to max_ap+1 from tl-syntax interner; debug-assert in holds

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • State = inline u128 when nAP ≤ 128 (common), else heap word-vec → branch-free holds, cache-resident product states; KripkeStructure::edges = CSR-style Vec<Vec<usize>>, for ≥10⁶ states prefer flat (offsets, targets) to cut allocations (Phase 12 opt; expose same successors API).
  • predecessors() is O(V+E), built once and cached by CtlChecker (Phase 5 preimage) — never recompute per sat call; validate() is O(V+E) single pass, lasso validate O(1) — never quadratic.
  • at(t) is O(1) (modular arithmetic) — used in tight LTLf progression loops (Phase 4) and counterexample replay.

3.8 Edge Cases

Case Required behavior
Empty AP set state {} valid State (no APs hold); not an error
Single-state self-loop Kripke total relation satisfied; EG true holds
Lasso loop_start = 0 whole word is a pure cycle, no stem
Lasso loop_start = len-1 stem of len-1, period-1 cycle on last state
Multiple init states holds (Phase 5) requires all init satisfy; validate each ∈ range
Unreachable Kripke states allowed (still valid); reachable() reports them; don't reject
Duplicate edge s→t twice allowed; successors may contain dups (dedup is Phase 12 opt, not a validity error)

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Malformed model → silent verdict flip (gravest, per spine): dead state ⇒ no infinite continuation ⇒ LTL check vacuously "holds"; missing init-range check ⇒ panic deep in evaluator; lasso period bug ⇒ wrong omega-word ⇒ eval_ltlf/automata acceptance diverges from Spot (caught Phase 13 differential). Mitigation: validate() is mandatory at every tl-cli entry and asserted at evaluator boundaries.
  • Bitset width underflow ⇒ high-id APs read false ⇒ subtle false negatives; debug-assert + width derived from interner max. DOT export mismatch ⇒ user mis-debugs counterexamples; golden-file test pins format.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI surface (Phase deps): tl check, tl eval, tl monitor parse trace/Kripke text → validate() → human-readable error with offending index, never a panic in release.
  • Trace/Kripke text format: line-oriented; STATES n, INIT i j, s -> t u, LABEL s p q, LOOP i — documented in Appendix; fixtures double as format spec.
  • no_std + alloc: panics route through core panic_handler; validate() returns Result, never panics on user input (only debug-asserts on internal invariants); memory budget logged by tl check --stats (state count, edge count, bytes/state) surfaces oversized models before evaluation.

16. Phase 4 — Finite-trace LTL (LTLf) by formula progression

Scope: implement eval_ltlf(f, t) -> bool in tl-eval via the progression recurrences over a finite Trace; resolve every residual obligation at end-of-trace under the weak/strong-next distinction; produce a single definitive boolean verdict. LTLf semantics per De Giacomo–Vardi: a formula is evaluated over a finite word w = s_0 … s_{n-1}, n = prefix.len(), with NO loop (loop_start ignored — that is the infinite/lasso path of Phase 8). Reuses progress recurrences shared structurally with the tl-monitor::Monitor::progress of Phase 9; this phase is the offline batch evaluator.

4.1 Objectives

  • tl-eval::ltlf::eval_ltlf(f: &Formula, t: &Trace) -> bool: total, panic-free, definitive verdict on any finite, NNF-or-raw LTL/LTLf Formula.
  • Progression recurrence engine: progress(f, s) -> Formula folding one State -> residual; eval_ltlf = fold over prefix then end-of-trace collapse.
  • Weak/strong next on finite words: X f is strong (false past end), weak-X f / dual R,G are satisfied vacuously at end; collapse residual via end_value.
  • Linear-in-trace, bounded residual size; reject CTL/path-quantifier Formula variants with an Err-free precondition (debug_assert Kind == Ltl|Ltlf); match canonical LTLf truth tables on a fixture suite with zero verdict drift vs hand-computed semantics.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-13 (progression as LTLf evaluation), P6 (Roşu-Havelund progress recurrences), P7/De-Giacomo-Vardi-13 (LTLf finite semantics, strong/weak X), ADR-05 (NNF precondition → progress assumes NNF) -->

Files (new) under tl-eval: src/ltlf.rs (public eval_ltlf, progress), src/ltlf/end.rs (end_value), tests/ltlf.rs. Re-export in tl-eval/src/lib.rs: pub use ltlf::{eval_ltlf, progress};. No new crate deps (uses tl-formula::Formula, tl-trace::{Trace, State}).

Core signatures:

pub fn eval_ltlf(f: &Formula, t: &Trace) -> bool;        // §4.1 entry; ignores loop_start
pub fn progress(f: &Formula, s: &State) -> Formula;      // one-step LTL derivative
fn end_value(f: &Formula) -> bool;                        // collapse residual at end-of-trace
fn holds_atom(p: Ap, s: &State) -> bool;                  // s.aps.contains(p)

Algorithm (eval_ltlf): let mut r = f.clone(); for s in &t.prefix { r = simplify(progress(&r, s)); } then end_value(&r). simplify = the Phase 1 constant-folding (And(True,x)=x, Or(False,x)=x, And(False,_)=False, Or(True,_)=True) applied bottom-up to keep residual bounded; without it residual grows ~2× per U step.

progress(f, s) recurrences (telegraphic; p1=progress(a,s), p2=progress(b,s)):

f progress(f, s)
True/False itself
Atom(p) True if holds_atom(p,s) else False
Not(Atom(p)) (NNF expected) False/True by holds_atom; on raw input Not(g) -> simplify(Not(progress(g,s)))
And(a,b) And(p1,p2)
Or(a,b) Or(p1,p2)
X(a) a.clone() — strong next: obligation deferred whole to successor
U(a,b) Or(p2, And(p1, U(a,b)))
R(a,b) And(p2, Or(p1, R(a,b)))
F(a) Or(p1, F(a)) (= U(True,a))
G(a) And(p1, G(a)) (= R(False,a))

end_value(f) (residual collapse at end-of-trace, finite semantics): closed Formula over the temporal-residual constructors only (no Atom survives — all atoms already resolved by progress). Recurrence:

residual f end_value(f) rationale
True true definitive
False false definitive
And(a,b) ev(a) && ev(b)
Or(a,b) `ev(a)
X(a) false strong next has no successor -> unsatisfied
U(a,b) false eventuality b never discharged
F(a) false unfulfilled eventuality
R(a,b) true release vacuously holds (no future to violate)
G(a) true universal over empty future -> vacuously true
Not(a) !ev(a) only on raw non-NNF residual

Normalization: callers SHOULD pass nnf(f) (Phase 1). eval_ltlf runs let f = nnf(f_in) internally first so progress/end_value see only Not(Atom) negations; this makes the Not(g) arms dead in practice and lets end_value assume the dual pairs above. weak-X is expressed as Not(X(Not a)) post-NNF -> R/dual handling; there is no distinct WeakX AST node (LOCKED Formula has none).

4.3 Deliverable

tl-eval::eval_ltlf compiling under no_std + alloc, cargo test -p tl-eval ltlf green. tl-cli eval subcommand wired: tl eval --finite -f "<formula>" --trace <file> -> prints true|false (exit 0). Smoke (extends Phase 0): tl eval --finite -f "F q" --trace t_q_at_2.json -> true; tl eval --finite -f "G p" --trace t_p_then_not.json -> false.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Every fixture in tests/data/ltlf/*.json yields the recorded expected verdict; 0 mismatches.
  • eval_ltlf is total: never panics on any finite Trace incl. empty prefix (n=0) and 10⁶-state prefix.
  • Residual size bound: after simplify, residual node-count ≤ c · |closure(f)| (constant c ≤ 4); asserted in a stress test for (a U b) U (c U d) over 10⁴ states (no superlinear blowup).
  • Throughput ≥ 5 M progress-steps/s single-thread on G(p -> F q) (release, modern x86-64); linear in |prefix| · |closure(f)|.
  • Empty-trace law: eval_ltlf(G x, &empty) == true, eval_ltlf(X x, &empty) == false, eval_ltlf(F x, &empty) == false, eval_ltlf(True, &empty) == true.

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Dir tl-eval/tests/data/ltlf/. Each *.json = { "formula": "...", "trace": {prefix:[[ap,…],…], loop_start:null}, "expected": bool }. Golden table tests/data/ltlf/expected.tsv (name<TAB>verdict).

fixture formula trace (APs per state) expected checks
x_strong_end.json X p [{p}] (n=1) false strong-X past end
x_strong_ok.json X p [{}, {p}] true X discharged at s₁
g_vacuous_empty.json G p [] (n=0) true end_value(G)=true
f_unfulfilled.json F q [{}, {}, {}] false eventuality fails
f_last_state.json F q [{}, {q}] true discharged at last s
until_release_dual.json p U q [{p},{p},{q}] true U discharge
until_no_q.json p U q [{p},{p}] false U end -> false
release_vacuous.json p R q [{q}] true R end -> true
weakx_via_nnf.json Not(X(Not p)) [{}] (n=1) true weak-X at end
nested_blowup.json (a U b) U (c U d) 10⁴ states no d false residual bound §4.4

Malformed/edge cases (must debug_assert-fail or be rejected pre-eval, never silent-wrong): ctl_in_ltlf.json (EX p -> precondition Kind != Ctl violated); lasso_ignored.json (loop_start: Some(0) present — eval_ltlf MUST ignore it, treat as finite, doc-noted); bad_ap_id.json (AP id beyond State bitset width -> holds_atom returns false, not panic).

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Weak vs strong next confusion: treating X as weak at end-of-trace flips X p on [{p}] from false->true. Root: copying infinite-LTL intuition. Debug: unit x_strong_end; assert end_value(X _)==false.
  • Residual blowup (no simplify): progress(U,…)=Or(_,And(_,U)) doubles the live U term each step -> O(2ⁿ) residual on p U q with long no-q prefix. Root: missing constant-fold of And(False,_)/Or(True,_). Debug: log residual node-count per step; it must plateau, not grow.
  • loop_start leakage: accidentally honoring the lasso loop makes finite eval diverge / loop forever. Root: shared Trace type with Phase 8 infinite path. Guard: eval_ltlf reads prefix only; never touches loop_start; lasso_ignored fixture pins this.
  • Non-NNF input + end_value: end_value assumes the dual collapse (G->true, F->false); a stray Not(G a) residual without NNF collapses wrong. Root: caller skips nnf. Guard: eval_ltlf forces internal nnf first.
  • Empty-trace UB: for over empty prefix correctly returns end_value(nnf(f)); bug is special-casing n==0 separately and forgetting NNF (debug: g_vacuous_empty + x_strong_end(n=0)). Atom resolved to wrong polarity: Not(Atom p) arm forgotten post-NNF -> negated atoms always False (debug: fixture with G(!p)).

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Cost = |prefix| × per-step(progress + simplify); per-step O(|residual|) ≤ O(|closure(f)|) after fold, target linear, no per-step heap thrash — reuse an arena/Box where the residual shape is stable (most U/G residuals are fixpoints r == progress(r,s)); fixpoint short-circuit (once simplify(progress(r,s))==r structurally and loop-free) is not exploited this phase (correctness-first) but flagged for Phase 12 caching.
  • Avoid Formula::clone() per step on large subtrees: X(a) arm clones a (acceptable, shallow), but U/R arms re-embed the original U(a,b) — share via Rc is a Phase 12 optimization, not now. Memory: residual is the only live allocation beyond the (borrowed) Trace; ≤ c·|closure(f)| nodes, no growth with |prefix|.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • n = 0 (empty prefix): verdict = end_value(nnf(f)) (e.g. G x->true, F x->false, True->true); single state n = 1: X _->false, F p/p->holds_atom, G p->holds_atom(p,s₀).
  • All-True/all-False constant formula: short-circuits at first progress; verdict independent of trace.
  • loop_start = Some(k): explicitly IGNORED, finite semantics over prefix only; AP id ≥ bitset width: holds_atom -> false (sparse-false), no panic, flagged in bad_ap_id.
  • Deeply right-nested U (a U (b U (c U …)), depth 10³): residual depth bounded by simplify; no stack overflow — progress/end_value use bounded recursion ≤ formula depth, validated against RUST_MIN_STACK default.

4.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest (silent verdict flip): a wrong end_value dual or a dropped simplify flips true<->false with no crash. Mitigation: every fixture pins the exact verdict; differential check vs the Phase 9 monitor's progression on the same prefix (must agree at end-of-trace); Phase 13 differential vs Spot's LTLf where available.
  • Divergence: honoring loop_start -> infinite fold; caught by lasso_ignored + a 100 ms test timeout. Stack overflow on pathological nesting: bounded recursion; deep-nest fixture asserts no overflow.
  • Panic on malformed AP / CTL node: holds_atom saturates to false; CTL nodes hit debug_assert!(matches!(kind, Ltl|Ltlf)) in debug, and in release progress has no arm for Ex/Au/… — guarded by a fallthrough unreachable!("CTL operator in LTLf eval") documented as a precondition violation, never a wrong bool.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tl eval --finite -f <FORMULA> --trace <FILE>; --finite selects LTLf (eval_ltlf) vs the infinite/lasso path; exit 0 always (verdict on stdout true|false), exit 2 on parse/precondition error.
  • Determinism: pure function of (f, t.prefix); reproducible, no RNG, no clock.
  • Diagnostics: TL_LTLF_TRACE=1 env prints per-step (state_idx, residual_node_count, residual_pretty) to stderr for debugging blowup/flip; off by default.
  • Interplay: progress is the shared kernel reused by tl-monitor (Phase 9, streaming, 3-/4-valued); eval_ltlf is its batch/definitive specialization — keep the recurrence table single-sourced to prevent drift. Observability hook: residual-size plateau is the canary for the simplify-blowup pitfall; log max residual node-count per run in benchmark mode.

17. Phase 5 — CTL explicit-state model checking (labeling)

CtlChecker over a KripkeStructure: per-state truth labeling by the Clarke-Emerson-Sistla 1986 iterative algorithm. Three primitive labelings — EX (preimage), E[U] (least fixpoint), EG (greatest fixpoint via SCC quotient) — span the existential basis; all other CTL operators derive by Boolean+temporal duality over NNF. holds iff every init state carries the label. No automata, no traces: pure backward state-set iteration on edges.

5.1 Objectives

  • CtlChecker<'a>::sat(&self, f: &Formula) -> Vec<bool>: length-nstates bitvector, true at state i iff i ⊨ f. Recurse on NNF subformulas; memoize by closure(f).
  • Three primitives: sat(Ex φ)=preimage(sat φ); sat(Eu(φ,ψ))=lfp; sat(Eg φ)=gfp over the sat(φ) sub-Kripke via SCCs.
  • Derive Ef/Ag/Af/Ax/Au by duality — NO independent fixpoints; reuse the three primitives + And/Or/Not.
  • holds(&self, f) -> bool = init.iter().all(|&i| labeled[i]). Each operator linear or near-linear in |S|+|E|.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-12 (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla labeling), P4/CES-86 (EX=preimage, EU=lfp, EG=gfp via SCC), P11/Tarjan-72 (SCC for EG), INV-1 (correctness: holds iff every init labeled), ADR-05 (NNF precondition) -->
  • File tl-eval/src/ctl.rs: CtlChecker<'a>{ k:&'a KripkeStructure, pred:Vec<Vec<usize>> }; ctor CtlChecker::new(k) precomputes the reverse edge index pred once (transpose of edges), O(|S|+|E|).
  • tl-eval/src/ctl/labeling.rs: fn sat_rec(&self, f, memo:&mut HashMap<*const Formula,Vec<bool>>); bottom-up over subformula closure so each node labeled once.
  • Atoms/Bool: Atom(a) → k.states[i].aps.test(a); True/False → const vec; Not/And/Or → bitwise over child vecs.
  • preimage(target:&[bool]) -> Vec<bool>: out[i]=true iff ∃ j∈edges[i] with target[j] — iterate via pred for cache locality.
  • lfp (Eu): init out=sat(ψ); worklist of frontier states; pop j, for each p∈pred[j] with sat(φ)[p] && !out[p] set out[p]=true, push p. Terminates ≤|E| relaxations.
  • gfp (Eg): restrict to Sφ=sat(φ); Tarjan SCCs on the induced subgraph; seed out with states in a nontrivial SCC (size≥2, or self-loop); backward-propagate within Sφ (states in Sφ with a successor already in out).
  • Derivations table (see 5.8 ref): Ax φ=¬Ex¬φ; Ef φ=Eu(True,φ); Af φ=¬Eg¬φ; Ag φ=¬Ef¬φ=¬Eu(True,¬φ); Au(φ,ψ)=¬( Eu(¬ψ,¬φ∧¬ψ) ∨ Eg ¬ψ ). Apply on NNF input; assert no past/X-only-LTL operators (Kind::Ctl gate).
  • Wire tl-cli check (Phase 8 shares): when Kind::Ctl, dispatch to CtlChecker::holds; counterexample for CTL is the witness-state set, not a Lasso.

5.3 Deliverable

cargo test -p tl-eval ctl green. CtlChecker::sat returns correct per-state labels and holds correct verdicts for the 8 CTL operators + nesting, on hand-built Kripke structures. tl check accepts a CTL formula + a Kripke file and prints HOLDS / FAILS with the failing init-state ids.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
sat correctness matches reference labels on all fixtures, every state
Eg SCC method agrees with naive O(
Complexity sat(f) ≤ O(|f|·(|S|+|E|)); Eg single Tarjan pass per Eg node
holds true iff all init labeled; vacuous (init=∅) → true
Throughput |S|=10⁵, |E|=10⁶, |f|=20 → holds < 200 ms

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

  • tl-eval/tests/ctl/mutex.kripke + mutex.expect: AG(¬(c0∧c1)) HOLDS; AG EF idle HOLDS; EF(c0∧c1) FAILS.
  • tl-eval/tests/ctl/eg_cycle.kripke: EG p true only on the p-cycle states; eg_cycle.expect lists the exact bitvector.
  • tl-eval/tests/ctl/eu_reach.kripke: E[p U q] lfp result vs golden; AF q vs ¬EG¬q.
  • tl-eval/tests/ctl/nested.kripke: AG(req → AF ack) (response pattern) and E[p U (E[q U r])].
  • Malformed: path_formula.txt (bare X p with no path quantifier → Kind::Ltl, reject in check CTL path); ltl_in_ctl.txt (p U q unquantified) → error NotCtl; dangling_state.kripke (edge to id ≥ nstates) → KripkeError::OutOfRange.

5.6 Pitfalls

  • Eg via lfp instead of gfp → wrong: EG is greatest fixpoint; computing it as repeated-AND from True or as an lfp drops the infinite-path requirement, mislabeling cycle-free p-states as satisfying. Debug: any state with no p-successor must be false in EG p.
  • Self-loop SCCs dropped: Tarjan returns size-1 SCCs for self-loops; if the seed only takes size≥2 components, a single state with a p-self-loop is wrongly excluded from EG p. Root cause: trivial-SCC filter must still admit self-loop states.
  • Au mis-derivation: forgetting the ∨ Eg ¬ψ disjunct makes A[φ U ψ] accept paths where ψ never holds. Cross-check Af ψ = Au(True,ψ) against ¬Eg¬ψ.
  • NNF not applied / Not over derived ops: deriving Ax,Ag injects fresh Not nodes; if sat_rec assumes NNF it must push them onto atoms first, else Not(Ex …) recurses with no case. Normalize each derived subformula.
  • Preimage direction flipped: using edges[i] forward when pred (backward) is required computes the image, not preimage — labels propagate the wrong way. Symptom: EX true on sinks.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Precompute pred once per CtlChecker; reuse across every sat call — avoid O(|E|) transpose per operator.
  • Bitvectors as Vec<u64> words for And/Or/Not; SIMD-friendly, 64× fewer ops than Vec<bool>.
  • Eu worklist (not round-robin) → each edge relaxed ≤1×; Eg one Tarjan pass amortizes all cycle detection.
  • Memoize shared subformulas via closure so AG(p → AF q)-style reuse of q-labels is computed once.

5.8 Edge Cases

Case Required behavior
init = ∅ holds = true (vacuous ∀)
state with no successors (sink) total relation required (Phase 3); if allowed, EX φ=false, EG φ=false at sink
EG True every state on/leading-to a cycle; all states if relation total
E[φ U ψ] with ψ everywhere = True vec immediately
disconnected components per-component labels independent; SCC pass handles each
single state + self-loop EG p = [p₀], AG p = [p₀]

5.9 Failure Analysis

  • Silent verdict flip (gravest, per spine): an Eg/Au derivation bug reports HOLDS when a counterexample state exists, no crash. Guard: differential check vs naive O(|S|²) fixpoint labeler in tests; Phase 13 cross-validates CTL labels vs known results.
  • Stack overflow: deep sat_rec on a 10⁴-deep formula — use explicit closure ordering (iterative bottom-up), not native recursion, for large |f|.
  • Wrong pred after Kripke mutation: CtlChecker caches pred; reusing it after editing k.edges yields stale labels. Treat CtlChecker as immutable-borrow; rebuild on change.

5.10 Operational Notes

  • tl check --logic ctl formula.tl model.kripke → HOLDS or FAILS: init states {…} violate φ; --labels dumps the full sat bitvector per top operator for debugging.
  • RUST_LOG=tl_eval::ctl=debug traces fixpoint iteration counts + SCC sizes per Eg.
  • CtlChecker is no_std + alloc compatible (only Vec/HashMap via alloc); CLI wraps it under std.
  • Determinism: SCC order from Tarjan is fixed by edge order → identical bitvectors across runs, safe for golden-file diffing.

18. Phase 6 — LTL to generalized Buchi (GPVW on-the-fly tableau)

GPVW (Gerth-Peled-Vardi-Wolper 1995) on-the-fly tableau: ltl_to_ngba(f: &Formula) -> Ngba. Input must be NNF LTL (Phase 1); output Ngba whose accepted ω-language L(Ngba) = { w : w ⊨ f } exactly — models of f, no over/under-approx. One generalized accept set per U-subformula enforces eventuality fulfillment. Lazy node expansion → only reachable tableau nodes materialized. This is the front half of the automata-theoretic pipeline (Phase 8 consumes Nba(¬f)); correctness here gates verdict soundness, so it is differentially checked vs Spot ltl2tgba.

6.1 Objectives

  • ltl_to_ngba(f) -> Ngba: NNF LTL → generalized Buchi, L(Ngba) = models of f exactly.
  • GPVW node = obligation set over subformula closure(f), split current (now) vs next; expand boolean/temporal until only literals + X-deferrals remain.
  • Edge Guard = conjunction of the node's atomic literals (positive AP, negated AP); next field → successor node's incoming obligations.
  • Accept sets: one per U-subformula α U β; a node is in that accept set iff β ∈ now OR (α U β) ∉ now → prevents stalling on the eventuality.
  • Lazy worklist: expand only generates successors of reached nodes; dedup by canonical now-set key → no full-2^closure blowup.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-06 (GPVW on-the-fly as locked LTL→automaton algorithm), ADR-07 (Ngba with k accept sets), P1/GPVW-95 (now/next split, accept sets per U), ADR-05 (NNF precondition), ADR-10 (Guard bitset mask+care), ADR-11 (arena NodeId indexing) -->
  • Crate tl-automata, new module src/gpvw.rs (pub ltl_to_ngba); src/ngba.rs holds Ngba; src/guard.rs holds Guard. lib.rs re-exports.
  • Closure (from tl-formula::closure(f)): intern each subformula → SubId(u32); obligation set = Bitset over SubId. closure includes f and all subformulas (NNF, so no naked Not except on Atom).
  • Tableau node struct (internal): struct Node { now: Bitset, next: Bitset, incoming: Vec<NodeId> }; GPVW "Graph" node split per the paper (Old/New/Next collapses to now after full expansion + next).
  • expand(new_obligations) -> Vec<Node> recurrence over the head subformula μ of New:
    • True/Atom/¬Atom → move to now (literals → guard); False → discard branch (unsatisfiable node).
    • α ∧ β → add both to New; α ∨ β → split into two branches.
    • X α → add α to next; α U β → split: {β} ∪ rest, OR {α} ∪ Next:=Next∪{αUβ}; α R β → split {α,β} OR {β} ∪ Next:=Next∪{αRβ}; F β = ⊤ U β, G α = ⊥ R α.
  • Guard (src/guard.rs): Guard { care: Bitset, val: Bitset } (mask+care over Ap) — literal p→set care&val bit, ¬p→set care, clear val. Conflicting literals (p ∧ ¬p) → unsatisfiable node, drop. pub fn matches(&self, s: &State) -> bool.
  • Worklist loop: HashMap<NodeKey, NodeId> where NodeKey = (now, next) canonical bitsets → dedup; pop unexpanded, expand, link edges[pred].push((guard, succ)).
  • Init: synthetic source; init = succ nodes of expand({f}).
  • Accept sets: for each U-subformula with id u = (α U β), accept[k] = { node : β∈now(node) ∨ u∉now(node) }. Ngba.accept: Vec<Vec<usize>>, accept.len() = #Until-subformulas (0 ⇒ all-accepting single set, every state accepting).
  • Self-loop check on next: a node's successor is determined by its next set becoming the successor's New; terminal node with empty next → loops on guard-satisfying True-node.

6.3 Deliverable

ltl_to_ngba in tl-automata returning a populated Ngba (nstates, init, edges with Guards, accept = one set per Until). tl to-buchi --gba <formula> prints the Ngba as HOA / dot. Lazy construction: a --max-states N guard aborts pathological inputs. Round-trips through Phase 7 degeneralize.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • L(ltl_to_ngba(f)) = models of f for all Phase-13 pattern formulas (verified via product-emptiness vs hand traces + Spot).
  • State count within constant factor of Spot ltl2tgba on the pattern set; |accept| == count_until_subformulas(f).
  • ltl_to_ngba(F p) ≤ 2 reachable states, 1 accept set; ltl_to_ngba(G p) ≤ 1 state, 0 accept sets (all-accepting); ltl_to_ngba(p U q) ≤ 2 states, 1 accept set.
  • Worst-case ≤ 2^|closure(f)| states (theoretical bound); typical pattern formulas ≪ that. Construction of any pattern formula < 50 ms.
  • Every reachable node is dedup-canonical: no two Ngba states share an identical (now, next) key.

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
gpvw/Fp.hoa F p 2 states, init→accept on p, 1 acc set
gpvw/Gp.hoa G p 1 state, self-loop guard p, 0 acc sets (all-acc)
gpvw/pUq.hoa p U q 2 states, acc = {q∈now ∨ pUq∉now}
gpvw/GFp.hoa G F p self-loop + F p eventuality, 1 acc set
gpvw/conflict.txt node with p ∧ ¬p node dropped, not in output
gpvw/two_until.hoa (a U b) ∧ (c U d) 2 accept sets, distinct fulfillment
gpvw/false.txt p ∧ ¬p (top) empty Ngba (no init), L = ∅

Malformed: non-NNF input (e.g. raw Not(U..)) → caller-side debug_assert / Err(NotNnf); past-LTL operator (Y/O/H/S) in formula → Err(UnsupportedOperator).

6.6 Pitfalls

  • Until eventuality lost → accept set computed as β∈now only (dropping u∉now): nodes that never assert αUβ get wrongly excluded → G F formulas accept stuttering non-models. Root cause: misreading GPVW acceptance. Debug: dump accept-set membership per node, check G(¬accept) reachable cycle.
  • Release vs Until duality → R expanded with U's split → silent language flip. Root cause: copy-paste recurrence. Debug: differential ltl2tgba on p R q vs ¬(¬p U ¬q).
  • Guard conflict not pruned → p ∧ ¬p node kept with unsatisfiable Guard → dead but inflates nstates, may create phantom accepting cycle if guard erroneously treated as True. Debug: assert care & val ⊆ care and conflict-free at node seal.
  • next not folded into successor New → successor expands wrong obligations → off-by-one-step language (accepts X-shifted words). Root cause: forgetting New := next on transition. Debug: trace Fp — should accept p at step 0.
  • Non-canonical dedup → ordering of bitset construction differs → duplicate nodes, exponential blowup on G(a∨b∨c…). Debug: log dedup hits/misses ratio; expect high hit rate.
  • NNF assumption violated → input has Not above temporal op → recurrence has no arm → panic or wrong split. Root cause: skipping nnf() precondition.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Node set = Bitset over |closure(f)| (≤ formula size); now/next are O(|closure|/64) words → cheap union/test.
  • Dedup via HashMap<(Bitset,Bitset), NodeId>; canonical bitset hash O(words). Target: pattern formulas → 10³ states < 50 ms.
  • Lazy: never materialize the 2^closure powerset; only reachable nodes (typically polynomial for patterns). --max-states guard caps adversarial inputs.
  • Guard as mask+care (two Bitset) → O(1) matches; avoid DNF-of-clauses unless disjunctive guard needed (defer to Phase 12 symbolic edges).
  • Expansion recursion depth ≤ formula nesting; iterative worklist (no deep call stack) for the outer loop.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • True / False constants: True → single all-accepting state (universal L = (2^AP)^ω); False → empty Ngba, init = [], L = ∅.
  • No Until subformula (G p, X p, pure boolean) → accept = [] → degeneralize treats all states accepting.
  • Nested U: a U (b U c) → 2 accept sets, inner fulfillment independent.
  • F G p / G F p: distinct automata; G F self-loops with recurring eventuality (1 acc set), F G defers to a persistent component.
  • Tautology after simplify (p ∨ ¬p) → collapses to True node if Phase-1 simplifier ran; else valid 1-state all-accepting.
  • Formula with unused APs → Guard.care simply omits them (don't-care bits).

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest: wrong Ngba language silently flips Phase-8 verdict (reports holds when counterexample exists). No crash. Guard: Phase-13 differential vs Spot ltl2tgba on every pattern + 10⁴ random NNF formulas, comparing acceptance on shared random lassos.
  • Blowup DoS: adversarial formula → exponential nodes → OOM. Guard: --max-states + memory ceiling → Err(StateLimitExceeded), not abort.
  • Eventuality bug class: accept-set miscomputation → liveness properties unsound. Detection: targeted fixtures GFp, FGp, pUq with known-good HOA reference.
  • Determinism: HashMap iteration order must not affect emitted state ids → use insertion-ordered Vec<NodeId> for stable HOA output (else fixtures flake).

6.10 Operational Notes

  • tl to-buchi --gba --format hoa|dot <formula> for inspection; HOA acc-name: generalized-Buchi <k>.
  • Tracing: RUST_LOG=tl_automata::gpvw=debug logs each node expansion (now/next/branch) + dedup hit/miss + accept-set assignment.
  • HOA output consumable by Spot tooling → enables external cross-check (autfilt --equivalent-to).
  • --stats prints nstates, nedges, |accept|, dedup-hit-rate, build-time-ms.
  • Invariant assertions (debug builds): NNF precondition, conflict-free guards, |accept| == #Until, every node canonical. Disabled in release for speed.

19. Phase 7 — Degeneralization and automaton simplification

7.1 Objectives

  • degeneralize(g: &Ngba) -> Nba: counter construction over the k = g.accept.len() acceptance sets → single-acceptance Nba, language-equivalent to g.
  • Invariant: L(Nba) == L(Ngba) — accepting Nba run visits accept infinitely often iff Ngba run visits all k sets infinitely often.
  • Simplification on the resulting Nba: drop unreachable states; drop dead (non-co-accepting) states; merge bisimilar/equivalent states. Target: ≤ size from Phase 6, never larger language.
  • Quantified target: degeneralized nstates ≤ k * g.nstates; after simplification, typical shrink 20–60% on LTL pattern formulas vs Spot's ltl2tgba.
  • Special cases: k == 0 (no acceptance set = all runs accepting) → every state accepting; k == 1 → degeneralization is identity copy (no counter widening).

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-07 (degeneralize Ngba→Nba counter construction), ADR-08 (downstream emptiness assumes single accept set), P9/Gastin-Oddoux-01 (simplification ideas: unreachable + dead-state pruning), B1§4.3 (generalized-Büchi counter degeneralization), INV-7 (L(Nba)==L(Ngba)) -->

Crate tl-automata. Files: src/degen.rs (counter construction), src/simplify.rs (reachability/dead/merge), wired into src/lib.rs.

pub fn degeneralize(g: &Ngba) -> Nba             // src/degen.rs
fn product_state(s: usize, c: usize, k: usize) -> usize   // s*k + c, k>=1
fn next_counter(c: usize, k: usize, src: usize, accept: &[Vec<usize>]) -> usize
pub fn simplify(a: &mut Nba)                     // src/simplify.rs: orchestrates the three passes
fn prune_unreachable(a: &mut Nba) -> Vec<usize>  // returns old->new id remap
fn prune_dead(a: &mut Nba)                       // remove states that cannot reach accept-cycle
fn merge_equiv(a: &mut Nba)                      // signature-based partition refinement
  • Counter construction: product state (s, c), c ∈ 0..k. On edge s --Guard--> t: if s ∈ accept[c] advance c -> (c+1) mod k, else keep c. Acceptance set = states with c == 0 and s ∈ accept[k-1] (equivalently the wrap point). Locked encoding: flat id s*k + c; init = { i*k + 0 : i in g.init }.
  • Dead-state prune: build reverse graph, BFS back from accept; a state survives only if (reachable from init) ∧ (can reach an accepting SCC). Reuse tl-trace SCC helper (Tarjan) to keep only states on/feeding accepting cycles.
  • Merge: partition refinement keyed by signature (is_accepting, sorted set of (Guard, target-class)); iterate to fixpoint, then collapse classes. Guards compared by canonical Guard form (mask+care).
  • Order is fixed: degeneralize → prune_unreachable → prune_dead → merge_equiv (merge last, on smallest graph).

7.3 Deliverable

  • degeneralize + simplify callable; ltl_to_ngba(f) |> degeneralize |> simplify yields a single-acceptance Nba for any NNF LTL f.
  • CLI hook: tl to-buchi --degen FORMULA emits the simplified Nba as HOA/dot (acceptance condition Inf(0)), consumed by Phase 8 product/emptiness.
  • Round-trippable: HOA output re-parsable; state count + acceptance reported.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
degeneralize correctness L(Nba) == L(Ngba) on every fixture (membership over sampled lassos)
State blowup bound nstates_pre_simplify ≤ k * g.nstates exactly
k == 0 handling all states accepting, language = L(Ngba)
k == 1 handling counter pass is identity (nstates unchanged pre-simplify)
Simplification soundness language unchanged before/after each pass (differential)
Size vs Spot within 1.5× of ltl2tgba -B state count on the 12 pattern formulas

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

  • fixtures/degen/fairness_GFp.hoa — G F p → Ngba k=1 → Nba unchanged count; expected 1 accept state.
  • fixtures/degen/two_until.hoa — (F a) & (F b) → k=2 → counter widens; expected nstates_pre = 2*g.nstates, post-merge collapses to ≤ k+1.
  • fixtures/degen/k0_safety_Gp.hoa — G p Ngba with empty accept (k=0) → all-accepting Nba.
  • fixtures/simplify/dead_branch.json — Nba with a non-co-accepting branch; expect pruned, language identical.
  • fixtures/simplify/equiv_pair.json — two bisimilar states; expect merged to one.
  • Malformed: bad_accept_index.json (accept set references state ≥ nstates) → degeneralize asserts/debug_assert! panic in debug, documented Err-free contract (caller guarantees valid Ngba); empty_init.json (no init) → Nba with empty init, emptiness returns None.

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Counter never wraps → no accepting set ever satisfied → false "empty"/verdict flip. Root cause: advancing on s ∈ accept[c] but defining acceptance on wrong index. Debug: assert every counter value 0..k reachable; trace a known G F p lasso.
  • k=0 forgotten → mod 0 panic / zero-size product. Guard early: if k == 0 { return all_accepting_copy(g) }.
  • Acceptance on the wrong state (mark c==0 source vs the transition into wrap) → off-by-one cycle detection. Fix: mark state where c just wrapped to 0; cross-check with Spot acceptance.
  • Merge collapses non-equivalent states because Guard not canonicalized → over-merge widens language. Root cause: structurally-different DNFs compared raw. Debug: canonicalize Guard before signature; differential vs unmerged.
  • Dead-prune deletes accepting SCC when reverse-reachability seeded from wrong set → loses counterexamples. Seed strictly from a.accept.
  • Remap stale edges after prune_unreachable: forgetting to rewrite edges/init/accept through old→new map → dangling indices, OOB later in nested DFS.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Construction O(k * |g.edges|); allocate nstates = k * g.nstates arena up front (no per-state realloc).
  • Many Ngba states never paired with all counters → lazy materialization: only create (s,c) reachable from init (combine with prune_unreachable in one BFS) to avoid the k× worst case.
  • Merge: partition refinement O(n log n · edge-degree); cap iterations, bail if no class splits.
  • Bitset Guard comparison is hot in merge signatures — sort edges once, dedup, hash the signature.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • g.nstates == 0 → empty Nba (no init, no states), emptiness == None.
  • g.init empty → empty language; degeneralization preserves (empty init).
  • Self-loop on accepting state crossing all counter values (single state, k>1) → expands to k states forming a counter cycle; merge must NOT collapse them (different counters distinct).
  • Disconnected accept set member (unreachable) → pruned; ensure language unchanged.
  • Guard true (universal) and false (dead edge): false-guarded edges dropped pre-degeneralize.

7.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest: degeneralization bug silently flips emptiness → wrong "holds"/counterexample (Phase 8/20). No crash. Primary guard: differential vs Spot ltl2tgba -B (Phase 13) on accepting-lasso membership.
  • Over-merge → language grows → false "empty" → missed counterexample. Detect via random-lasso membership both directions.
  • Under-prune → bloated automaton → Phase 8 slow but still correct (perf, not correctness).
  • Mitigation: post-condition check (debug builds) sampling N lassos: accepts(nba, w) == accepts(ngba, w).

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics logged: ngba_states, k, nba_states_pre, nba_states_post, prune/merge counts, wall-time.
  • RUST_LOG=tl_automata::degen=debug dumps counter transitions; --emit-hoa snapshots intermediate Nba for offline autfilt --equivalent-to cross-check against Spot.
  • Determinism: state ordering fixed by s*k+c then remap, so HOA output is byte-stable for snapshot tests.
  • Feature flag degen-lazy toggles eager vs reachable-only construction for A/B perf comparison.

20. Phase 8 — Product and emptiness (automata-theoretic LTL model checking)

Closes the LTL pipeline: K ⊨ Aφ ⟺ L(product(Nba(¬φ), K)) = ∅. Build the synchronous product of the Buchi automaton for the negated formula with the Kripke structure, decide emptiness by nested DFS (reachable accepting cycle), and on non-emptiness emit a Lasso (stem + cycle) counterexample that re-simulates against φ. check_ltl returns Ok(()) (holds) or Err(Lasso) (counterexample).

Module: tl-automata/src/product.rs, tl-automata/src/emptiness.rs; orchestration in tl-eval/src/ltl_check.rs. Inputs are Phase 6/7 artifacts (ltl_to_ngba → degeneralize → Nba).

8.1 Objectives

  • product(a: &Nba, k: &KripkeStructure) -> Nba — synchronous product; states (q_aut, q_kripke); edge iff Kripke s→s' AND aut q→q' under a guard satisfied by label(s') (next-state guard convention, fixed once and asserted).
  • emptiness(a: &Nba) -> Option<Lasso> — nested (two-color) DFS (CVWY); Some(lasso) ⟺ reachable accepting cycle ⟺ non-empty ω-language.
  • check_ltl(f, k) -> Result<(), Lasso> — nnf(Not(f)) → ltl_to_ngba → degeneralize → product → emptiness; map product Lasso indices back to Kripke states; verify the witness re-simulates ¬φ (and violates φ).
  • Decision is over all init states: holds ⟺ no init Kripke state begins an accepting product run. Product init = init_aut × init_kripke.
  • Counterexample is a concrete trace: stem States reach the cycle, cycle States repeat, together a lasso ω-word violating φ.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-08 (nested DFS as emptiness algorithm), ADR-09 (explicit-state automata-theoretic model checking), P2/Vardi-Wolper-86 (K⊨Aφ iff L(product(Nba(¬φ),K))=∅), P3/CVWY-92 (two-color nested DFS, lasso extraction), P4 (derives from INV-1 correctness), ADR-18 (eager product baseline, lazy in Phase 12) -->
File Item Signature / shape
product.rs product fn product(a:&Nba,k:&KripkeStructure)->Nba — on-by-default eager build (lazy variant deferred to Phase 12)
product.rs ProdId struct ProdId{aut:u32,kr:u32}; flat index id = aut*k.states.len()+kr; reverse via div/rem
product.rs guard_holds fn guard_holds(g:&Guard,s:&State)->bool — mask+care match of Guard DNF vs s.aps bitset
emptiness.rs emptiness fn emptiness(a:&Nba)->Option<Lasso>
emptiness.rs Color enum Color{White,Cyan,Blue,Red} (cyan=on outer stack, blue=outer done, red=inner done)
ltl_check.rs check_ltl fn check_ltl(f:&Formula,k:&KripkeStructure)->Result<(),Lasso>
ltl_check.rs lasso_to_trace fn lasso_to_trace(l:&Lasso,k:&KripkeStructure)->Trace — project ProdId → Kripke → State; loop_start=Some(stem.len())
  • Product construction: only Nba edges (single accept set) are productized — degeneralize first. Accept set: (aut,kr) accepting ⟺ aut ∈ a.accept (Kripke contributes liveness only via paths, not acceptance, since K is a "generator", every state accepting). Edge: for Kripke kr→kr' and aut (g,aut'), add iff guard_holds(g,&k.states[kr']). BFS/DFS from init to materialize reachable product states only; intern ProdId → dense usize via HashMap/Vec.
  • Nested DFS (CVWY): outer DFS post-order pushes each accepting state into inner DFS seeking a path back to that seed; inner uses a separate Red coloring; report cycle on edge into the seed (or any cyan-on-inner-stack node). Single shared color array, two passes interleaved — O(|S|+|E|), each state outer-visited once, inner-visited once.
  • Lasso extraction: stem = outer DFS stack at seed discovery; cycle = inner DFS path from seed back to seed. Store Lasso{stem:Vec<usize>, cycle:Vec<usize>} in product indices; check_ltl decodes kr component for the trace.
  • check_ltl flow: let g = nnf(&Not(Box::new(f.clone()))); → let ngba = ltl_to_ngba(&g); → let nba = degeneralize(&ngba); → let prod = product(&nba,k); → match emptiness(&prod){None=>Ok(()), Some(l)=>Err(l_decoded)}.
  • Witness check (debug-assert): re-run eval_ltlf-style progression of ¬φ over the lasso word (cycle unrolled ≥ closure-size times) → must stay satisfiable; assert φ violated on same word.

8.3 Deliverable

End-to-end LTL model checking: tl check '<φ>' --kripke <file> prints holds or a counterexample lasso (stem states / cycle states, marked loop_start). check_ltl public in tl-eval. Product + nested-DFS emptiness in tl-automata, exercised by tl-cli check. Counterexample re-simulates and provably violates φ.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Target
A1 K ⊨ Gp where p holds in all reachable states check_ltl → Ok(())
A2 K ⊭ Gp with one ¬p reachable state Err(Lasso); decoded trace reaches a ¬p state
A3 Lasso re-simulation progressed ¬φ stays SAT over the lasso word; φ violated (debug-assert passes)
A4 Emptiness complexity states each visited ≤1× outer + ≤1× inner; product built reachable-only
A5 Verdict matches Spot ltl2tgba + product on ≥50 pattern formulas 100% agreement
A6 All-init semantics holds ⟺ no init-state-rooted accepting cycle
A7 Determinism repeated check_ltl on same input → identical lasso (stable tie-break by ascending ProdId)

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
fixtures/check/Gp_holds.kripke G p, all states label p Ok (holds)
fixtures/check/Gp_violated.kripke G p, state s2 lacks p Err; lasso stem reaches s2
fixtures/check/GFp_fairness.kripke G F p, cycle never visiting p Err; cycle = the ¬p loop
fixtures/check/req_resp.kripke G(req -> F resp), deadlock state Err; lasso traps with req∧¬resp
fixtures/check/pUq_holds.kripke p U q, q reached on every path Ok
fixtures/check/empty_product.expect unsat ¬φ (e.g. φ=True) empty product → Ok immediately
fixtures/check/no_init.kripke (malformed) init=[] Err/error: vacuous; document chosen convention (empty init ⇒ holds vacuously)
fixtures/check/partial_relation.kripke (malformed) deadlock (no successor) rejected by Phase 3 validation OR self-loop convention; assert which

Golden: lasso printed as stem: s0,s1 | loop: s1,s2 (stable). Differential harness compares holds/CE-existence vs Spot.

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Detection / debug
Verdict silently flipped wrong-polarity automaton (φ instead of ¬φ), or guard-on-source vs guard-on-target mismatch A5 diff vs Spot; assert guard convention with a 2-state hand fixture
Missed accepting cycle inner DFS not seeded from every accepting node, or color reset between seeds check coloring invariants; CVWY proof: never reset Blue, inner only paints Red
Spurious cycle inner DFS closes on a non-accepting back-edge / wrong seed-membership test cycle must contain seed (accepting); assert seed ∈ a.accept
Stack overflow / deep recursion recursive DFS on large product (10^6 states) explicit Vec work-stack, not call recursion
Lasso decodes to non-violating trace product accepting state ≠ Kripke-violating; or cycle too short to expose eventuality A3 re-simulation; unroll cycle ≥ closure(φ).len()
Unreachable product blow-up eager full ` aut

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Product size ≤ |Nba| × |K|; build reachable-only → typically ≪ product bound. Target: 10^5–10^6 product states checked in <1 s; memory ~16 B/state (flat ProdId + color byte + edge offsets).
  • Nested DFS = O(|S| + |E|) time, O(|S|) space (color array + two stacks). Single byte/state color; avoid per-state HashSet.
  • guard_holds: bitset mask+care, O(words) not O(|AP|); precompute guard masks once at product build.
  • Phase 12 lazy on-the-fly product (build during DFS) is the scaling lever; keep emptiness agnostic to eager-vs-lazy via a successor-iterator trait.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • ¬φ unsatisfiable (e.g. φ=True, φ=G p ∨ F¬p) → Nba empty / no accept → product empty → Ok (holds) without DFS.
  • φ unsatisfiable / ¬φ valid → any infinite path is a CE; smallest lasso (single-state self-loop) returned.
  • Single Kripke state with self-loop: shortest possible lasso (stem=∅ or [s0], cycle=[s0]).
  • Multiple init states: accepting run from any → CE; pick lowest ProdId for determinism.
  • Pure-safety φ (no eventuality) → CE cycle is any back-edge after a finite bad prefix; cycle may be trivial.
  • Disconnected Kripke states (unreachable from init) → never materialized in product; correctly ignored.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Severity Mitigation
Wrong automaton/DFS flips holds↔CE silently (the GRAVEST class) Critical A5 differential vs Spot is the primary gate; A3 re-simulation rejects bogus CEs
Product guard convention drift across phases Critical one assert-backed convention (guard tests target label), 2-state regression fixture
Non-deterministic lasso → flaky goldens Medium ascending-ProdId tie-break; deterministic stack order
Reachable-only build omits a needed edge High invariant: every productized edge has a guard-satisfying target; unit test edge enumeration
Empty init mis-handled (vacuous true vs error) Low document + fixture no_init.kripke; fixed convention

8.10 Operational Notes

  • tl check '<φ>' --kripke f.kripke [--trace] → holds or lasso; --trace prints decoded State sequence with loop_start.
  • RUST_LOG=tl_automata::emptiness=debug traces DFS color transitions + seed discoveries; gated, off in release.
  • Feature flag cex-check enables A3 re-simulation in release builds (default debug-only) for high-assurance use.
  • Counterexample is the user-facing payload — must be minimal-ish (don't unroll cycle in output; mark loop_start) and re-runnable through tl eval as a Trace.
  • This phase makes check_ltl the load-bearing public API; downstream Phase 13 validation diffs its verdicts against Spot model-checking on pattern + random formulas.

21. Phase 9 — Runtime monitoring (progression, LTL3, RV-LTL)

Builds tl-monitor: an online Monitor that folds progress() over an event &State stream and emits stable LTL3 / RV-LTL verdicts as soon as the prefix is decisive. Bridges trace evaluation (Phase 4 progression) and automata (Phase 6 ltl_to_ngba): a definitive LTL3 verdict ⇔ the observed prefix is a good/bad prefix of φ, decided either by a residual-formula UNSAT/VALID test or by an emptiness-per-state lookup on the monitor automaton. RV-LTL refines Unknown into PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse via the finite (LTLf) tie-break of the current residual.

9.1 Objectives

  • Monitor::step(&mut self, &State) -> RvVerdict: incremental, O(|residual|) amortized per event, no per-step full re-evaluation; verdict monotone-once-definitive (latches True/False forever).
  • LTL3 3-valued: True (good prefix — every infinite extension ⊨ φ), False (bad prefix — none does), else Unknown. RV-LTL 4-valued refines Unknown → PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse.
  • Monitorability classification: φ monitorable ⇔ no prefix is "ugly" (a prefix from which no extension reaches a definitive verdict). Report per-formula at construction.
  • Optional monitor-automaton synthesis: deterministic Moore-style monitor (state → verdict) so steady-state per-event cost is one transition + table lookup, independent of residual blow-up.
  • Definitive verdicts agree with the automata-theoretic semantics (Phase 6–8): good/bad-prefix decisions match emptiness/language tests on φ and ¬φ. Differential guard vs Spot.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-14 (progression-based monitoring), ADR-15 (RvVerdict domain), P5/BLS-11 (LTL3 good/bad prefix, RV-LTL 4-valued, monitorability), P6/Roşu-Havelund-05 (progress recurrences), ADR-05 (NNF precondition for progress) -->

Crate tl-monitor (no_std + alloc); depends on tl-formula (Formula, nnf, closure) + tl-automata (Ngba, ltl_to_ngba, degeneralize, emptiness). Files under tl-monitor/src/:

File Contents
verdict.rs enum Verdict { True, False } (definitive); enum RvVerdict { True, False, PresumablyTrue, PresumablyFalse, Unknown }; fn is_definitive(RvVerdict)->bool; LTL3↔RV-LTL projection helpers.
progress.rs fn progress(f:&Formula, s:&State)->Formula (the LTL derivative; recurrences below); fn simplify_residual(&Formula)->Formula (constant-fold True/False, idempotence) — keeps residual bounded.
monitor.rs struct Monitor { residual: Formula, latched: Option<Verdict>, automaton: Option<MonAut>, cur: usize, mode: Mode }; impl Monitor { fn new(&Formula)->Monitor; fn step(&mut self,&State)->RvVerdict; fn reset(&mut self) }. enum Mode { Progression, Automaton }.
verdict_test.rs fn good_prefix_verdict(residual:&Formula)->Option<Verdict>: True if residual is a tautology (φ already satisfied on every continuation), False if UNSAT (no continuation), via ltl_to_ngba(residual)/ltl_to_ngba(¬residual) emptiness on tl-automata.
monitorability.rs enum Monitorability { Monitorable, NonMonitorable }; fn classify(&Formula)->Monitorability — over reachable residuals, ∃ ugly state ⇒ NonMonitorable.
synth.rs struct MonAut { nstates, init, delta:Vec<Vec<(Guard,usize)>>, verdict:Vec<RvVerdict> }; fn synth_monitor(&Formula)->MonAut — emptiness-per-state labeling of ltl_to_ngba(φ) & ltl_to_ngba(¬φ), then determinize via residual-subset states.

Algorithms (LOCKED progression recurrences; progress over NNF φ):

  • progress(True,s)=True; progress(False,s)=False; progress(Atom p,s)= if s.aps[p] {True} else {False}.
  • progress(Not f,s)=Not(progress(f,s)) (operand is an atom in NNF); progress(And a b,s)=And(progress a, progress b); Or dual.
  • progress(X f,s)=f; progress(F f,s)=Or(progress(f,s), F f); progress(G f,s)=And(progress(f,s), G f).
  • progress(U f g,s)=Or(progress(g,s), And(progress(f,s), U f g)); progress(R f g,s)=And(progress(g,s), Or(progress(f,s), R f g)).
  • Fold: residual ← simplify_residual(progress(residual, s)). step(): if latched set → return it; else progress, then good_prefix_verdict(residual) → if Some(v) latch + return; else compute RV-LTL refinement (LTLf finite-semantics eval of residual on the empty continuation: True→PresumablyTrue, False→PresumablyFalse) → Unknown if neither.
  • Mode::Automaton: step() follows delta[cur] on the AP-guard matched by s, sets cur, returns verdict[cur]; falls back to progression if residual mode chosen at new.
  • synth_monitor: build Aφ=ltl_to_ngba(φ), A¬φ=ltl_to_ngba(nnf(Not φ)); a residual r ⊨-decided True iff L(r over Aφ-style) covers all extensions (emptiness of ¬-side), False iff UNSAT; subset-construct deterministic states keyed by residual (canonicalized).

9.3 Deliverable

tl-monitor compiling no_std + alloc; tl monitor --formula F --stream FILE (Phase 0 tl-cli wired) reads newline-delimited AP valuations, prints one RvVerdict per event and a final verdict; Monitor::new/step/reset public; classify + synth_monitor available. cargo test -p tl-monitor green.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • LTL3 correctness: for F p on stream where p first holds at index k → emits Unknown×k then True latched; for G p → Unknown while p holds, False at first ¬p. ≥ 40 pattern formulas pass.
  • Latching: once True/False, every later step() returns the same value (property test, 10⁴ random streams).
  • RV-LTL refinement: residual ⊨ LTLf-true on prefix end → PresumablyTrue; dual → PresumablyFalse; verified on p U q, G(req→F ack).
  • Definitive verdict = good/bad-prefix per automata: good_prefix_verdict agrees with emptiness on ltl_to_ngba(¬residual) for 100% of fixtures.
  • Monitorability: G F p → NonMonitorable; F p, p U q, G p → Monitorable. Matches Bauer-Leucker-Schallhart table.
  • Steady-state cost: Mode::Automaton per-step is O(out-degree) ≤ 8 ops on the pattern set, independent of stream length.

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input → Expected
fixtures/mon/Fp_good.json F p, stream [{},{},{p}] → Unknown,Unknown,True; final True.
fixtures/mon/Gp_bad.json G p, stream [{p},{p},{}] → Unknown,Unknown,False; final False.
fixtures/mon/pUq.json p U q, stream [{p},{p},{q}] → Unknown,Unknown,True; with [{p},{}] → Unknown,False.
fixtures/mon/req_ack.json G(req→F ack), stream toggling req/ack → never True; PresumablyTrue when ack pending=∅, PresumablyFalse otherwise.
fixtures/mon/gfp_nonmon.json G F p → classify=NonMonitorable; every step returns Unknown.
fixtures/mon/latch_idem.json F p after first True, feed {},{},{} → all True.
Malformed bad_event_ap.json (AP id ∉ alphabet) → MonitorError::UnknownAp; empty_stream.json → final verdict = good_prefix_verdict(φ) (e.g. True for True, Unknown for F p); ctl_formula.json (a CTL φ) → MonitorError::NotLtl (monitoring is LTL-only).

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Residual blow-up: naive progress on G(F p) grows the residual unbounded → root cause: missing simplify_residual constant-folding + no structural hashing. Debug: log |residual| per step; assert bounded by closure size; canonicalize via NNF + sorted And/Or.
  • Premature True on finite prefix: treating LTLf-true residual as LTL3-True confuses RV-LTL with LTL3 → root cause: conflating finite vs infinite semantics. True requires good-prefix (all infinite extensions), only PresumablyTrue for finite tie-break. Debug: compare against emptiness(ltl_to_ngba(¬residual)).
  • Non-latching verdict: re-deriving verdict each step can oscillate if simplification is non-canonical → root cause: equivalent residuals not recognized equal. Fix: latch in Monitor.latched; never recompute after definitive.
  • G F p reported Monitorable: ugly-state detection omitted → root cause: only checking good/bad prefixes, not reachability of a definitive state from every residual. Debug: in classify, confirm ∃ residual with no path to a True/False-labeled state.
  • Empty-stream verdict wrong: returning Unknown unconditionally ignores formulas decided at length 0 (e.g. True, p∨¬p) → root cause: skipping good_prefix_verdict(φ) at new. Fix: seed latched at construction.
  • Automaton/progression disagreement: Mode::Automaton verdict ≠ progression verdict → root cause: determinization subset key not canonical, or verdict-labeling used wrong emptiness side. Debug: cross-check both modes on every fixture.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Progression mode: per-step O(|residual|); keep residual ≤ |closure(φ)| via simplify_residual + structural interning (HashMap<canonical Formula, id>). Memory O(|closure|).
  • Automaton mode (synth_monitor): one-time synthesis O(2^|closure|) worst case (deterministic monitor), but per-event cost O(out-degree); preferred for high-throughput streams (>10⁶ events).
  • good_prefix_verdict calls ltl_to_ngba + emptiness per residual; cache by canonical residual id → amortize repeated residuals on cyclic streams. Target: ≤ 1 automaton build per distinct residual.
  • Verdict latching short-circuits all later cost to O(1); definitive monitors are free after the deciding event.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty stream → final = good_prefix_verdict(φ) (definitive if φ is True/False/decidable at 0, else Unknown).
  • φ = True/False → step returns True/False immediately, never reads events.
  • Tautology residual mid-stream (p ∨ ¬p) → True (good prefix) at that step.
  • Pure-past φ (Y/O/H/S) → reject with MonitorError::NotLtl or route to Phase 10 DP monitor (LTL3 monitoring is future-time; document the boundary).
  • Single-event stream that is a bad prefix (G p, event {}) → False at index 0.
  • Alphabet mismatch: event sets an AP outside closure(φ) atoms → UnknownAp (strict) vs ignore (lenient) — strict by default.
  • X p at end of stream: residual p undecided on infinite continuation → Unknown (not False); only definitive if continuation forced.

9.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest: a verdict-correctness bug silently flips True/False (wrong good_prefix_verdict side, or buggy synth_monitor labeling) with no crash → mirrors the project's gravest class (a wrong verdict). Guard: differential test definitive verdicts vs Spot's monitor / vs emptiness on ¬φ.
  • Liveness-as-safety error: emitting False for G F p (non-monitorable, never decidable) → over-claiming; assert no definitive verdict is ever emitted for a NonMonitorable φ.
  • Recovery: monitor has no persistent state corruption — reset() re-seeds from φ; verdicts are pure functions of the prefix, replayable for post-mortem.
  • Detection: invariant checks — (a) verdict monotone-definitive, (b) |residual| ≤ |closure(φ)|, (c) automaton mode ≡ progression mode on every test event.

9.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tl monitor --formula "G(req -> F ack)" --stream events.txt [--mode auto|prog] [--rvltl]; stdout = one verdict per line, exit code = 0 if final definitive True, 1 if False, 2 if Unknown/non-monitorable.
  • --mode auto triggers synth_monitor for long streams; --mode prog forces incremental progression (lower setup latency).
  • Diagnostics: --trace-residual prints the residual Formula (pretty-printer from tl-syntax) per step for debugging; --explain prints the deciding prefix and good/bad-prefix justification.
  • Observability: counters for events consumed, distinct residuals seen, step at which verdict latched, monitorability class; emit on stream end.
  • Integration: monitor automaton exportable to HOA/dot (reuse Phase 6 to-buchi writer) for inspection; verdict-labeled states annotated.

22. Phase 10 — Past-time LTL (Y/O/H/S) by dynamic programming

Past-time LTL extends Formula with Y (previous), O (once), H (historically), S (since); evaluated over a finite Trace prefix in a single forward pass (tl-eval), each state's past-obligation vector derived from the previous state's. Past-LTL is strictly finite-trace here (no lasso loop unrolling); reference: standard past-LTL DP / monitoring with past, distinct from future-LTL progression (Phase 4) which folds forward residuals.

10.1 Objectives

  • Add past operators Y/O/H/S to evaluation: each holds at index i purely as a function of the prefix t.prefix[0..=i].
  • DP recurrence: state i's past-vector pv[i] (one bool per past-subformula) computed from pv[i-1] + current State.aps in O(1) per subformula -> total O(n·|sub|), n = prefix.len().
  • Public API pub fn eval_past_ltl(f: &Formula, t: &Trace) -> Vec<bool> (per-index truth) + pub fn holds_past(f: &Formula, t: &Trace) -> bool (truth at last index, the "now"). Single forward pass, no recursion over time.
  • Kind::PastLtl classification (Phase 2) gates input; mixed past+future rejected (out of scope, future = Phase 4/8); Y at i=0 is False (no previous state) — locked anchor, H at i=0 = right-operand-vacuous = its operand at 0.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-16 (past-LTL DP evaluation strategy), §5.4/Lichtenstein-Pnueli-Zuck-85 (Y/O/H/S semantics), INV-3 (Y at position 0 is False), ADR-04 (Kind::PastLtl gates eval_past_ltl entry) -->
  • File: tl-eval/src/past.rs (new); re-export eval_past_ltl, holds_past from tl-eval/src/lib.rs. Depends on tl-formula, tl-trace only (no automata).
  • Closure & indexing: call tl_formula::closure(f) (Phase 1) -> Vec<Formula>; assign each past-relevant subformula a dense usize slot via BTreeMap<&Formula, usize> keyed structurally; bottom-up order (children before parents) so a parent reads child slots in the same pass.
  • Past-subformulas = Y(_), O(_), H(_), S(_,_) plus boolean/atom subterms needed by them. Future ops absent (rejected by Kind).
  • Vectors: prev: Vec<bool> (= pv[i-1]), cur: Vec<bool> (= pv[i]), len = #slots. Re-used across iterations (swap, not realloc).
  • Per-state eval of non-temporal nodes (Atom/Not/And/Or/True/False) computed inline from State + already-filled cur slots (bottom-up guarantees readiness).
  • DP recurrences (locked):
op recurrence at index i i = 0 base
Y a pv[i][Ya] = prev[a] False
O a pv[i][Oa] = cur[a] || prev[Oa] cur[a]
H a pv[i][Ha] = cur[a] && prev[Ha] cur[a]
S(a,b) pv[i][Sab] = cur[b] || (cur[a] && prev[Sab]) cur[b]
  • Identities (assert in tests, not rewritten): O a ≡ True S a; H a ≡ ¬O¬a; weak-Y Ỹ not in Formula (only strong Y, False at 0).
  • Loop skeleton (≤2-line illustrative):
DP loop (pseudocode, not compilable):
  for each state t.prefix[i]:
    fill_slots(state=t.prefix[i], prev=prev_vec, cur=cur_vec, order=topological_order)
      // bool/atom nodes: read cur[child] and State.aps
      // Y slot: cur[j] = prev[child_j]
      // O slot: cur[j] = prev[child_j] || cur[grandchild_j]
      // H slot: cur[j] = prev[child_j] && cur[grandchild_j]
      // S(a,b) slot: cur[j] = cur[b_slot] || (cur[a_slot] && prev[j])
    out.push(cur[root_slot])
    swap(prev_vec, cur_vec)
  • fill_slots: iterate order bottom-up; match slot's Formula; Y/O/H/S read prev[...], others read cur[child] / State.aps.
  • loop_start IGNORED (warn once): past-LTL semantics here = over the finite prefix; document that lasso evaluation of past is out of scope.
  • tl-cli: tl eval dispatches to eval_past_ltl/holds_past when parsed Kind == PastLtl.

10.3 Deliverable

tl-eval past-LTL evaluator: eval_past_ltl(f,t) -> Vec<bool> (per-index) + holds_past(f,t) -> bool (last index), single O(n·|sub|) forward pass; wired into tl eval; fixture suite under tl-eval/tests/past/; cargo test -p tl-eval past green.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • holds_past(parse("Y p"), single_state_trace) = false (i=0 base); 2-state trace where p∈s0 -> at i=1 = true.
  • O p monotone non-decreasing along the prefix; H p monotone non-increasing; both verified on a 16-state random trace (property test).
  • O a equals True S a per-index on 1000 random (formula,trace) pairs (operand a a random boolean over ≤4 APs).
  • Single pass: instrument a counter; per-state work ≤ |slots| slot-updates, zero re-scans of earlier indices (assert counter == n·|slots|).
  • holds_past = eval_past_ltl(..).last() exactly, for all fixtures.
  • Mixed past+future formula -> tl eval errors with Kind mismatch (no silent eval).

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Dir tl-eval/tests/past/. Each .tl = formula; each .trace = JSON {prefix:[["p"],["q","p"],...], loop_start:null}; .expected = per-index bool vector (then final bool).

fixture formula trace (prefix) expected per-index
prev_base Y p [{p},{p}] [F,T]
once_latch O p [{},{p},{}] [F,T,T]
hist_break H p [{p},{p},{}] [T,T,F]
since_basic p S q [{q},{p},{p},{}] [T,T,T,F]
since_reset p S q [{p},{q},{p}] [F,T,T]
nested_yy Y (Y p) [{p},{},{}] [F,F,T]
oh_dual H p vs ¬O¬p random 8-state identical vectors

Malformed: mixed.tl = O (X p) -> reject (Kind::Ltl/mixed, not PastLtl); empty.trace = {prefix:[],loop_start:null} -> eval_past_ltl returns [], holds_past -> error EmptyTrace; bad_loop.trace = loop_start:Some(0) -> warn-once + evaluate prefix-only.

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Y at i=0 = True (weak-next confusion) — root cause: importing future weak/strong-next intuition; here strong Y is False at start. Debug: assert eval(Y True)[0]==false. Flips every since/once built on Y.
  • Reading cur before bottom-up fill — parent slot evaluated before child slot in order -> stale/garbage bool. Root cause: order not topologically sorted by subformula depth. Debug: in fill_slots, debug_assert! child slot index < parent in order.
  • Aliasing prev/cur after swap — forgetting to overwrite all cur slots each iteration leaves last-iteration values for boolean nodes -> spurious latch. Fix: fill_slots writes every slot unconditionally; never read uninitialized cur.
  • S(a,b) operand order — a S b = "a held since b became true" -> b is the trigger (right). Swapping a/b silently passes some fixtures (when a≡b). Debug: since_reset fixture isolates it (asymmetric a,b).
  • Closure dedup by Box identity not structure — two structurally-equal subterms get distinct slots -> wasted work + a parent reading the wrong twin. Fix: structural key (BTreeMap<&Formula,usize> with Ord/Eq on Formula).
  • loop_start honored accidentally — evaluating into the loop double-counts past obligations. Root cause: copying future-LTL lasso handling. Fix: explicitly slice prefix only; warn-once.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Time O(n·|sub|), space O(|sub|) (two reused vectors) — independent of n for memory; target ≥10M state-updates/s single-thread. Slot count |slots| = #distinct past+boolean subterms ≤ |closure(f)|; dense Vec<bool> (1 byte) over BitVec for branch-predictable O(1) indexing at this size.
  • Reuse prev/cur via swap -> zero per-state allocation; pre-size out to prefix.len().
  • Atom lookup: State.aps is Bitset -> AP membership O(1) bit-test; cache Ap ids in slot descriptors to avoid map lookups in the hot loop; 1M-state trace, |sub|=8: target <15 ms; no recursion -> no stack growth with trace length (only with formula nesting, bounded by closure build).

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty prefix -> [] / holds_past error EmptyTrace; single state -> all Y-rooted = False, O/H/S = base case.
  • Deeply nested Y^k p: false for first k indices, then mirrors p shifted by k (k > prefix.len() -> all False); H over all-true prefix = all True, one false flips it False from that index onward (monotone down).
  • p S q with q never true -> all False; q true at 0 -> True while a holds, drops when a breaks (post first q).
  • Pure-boolean past formula (no temporal op) -> degenerates to per-state propositional eval, Y/O/H/S slots empty; duplicate APs in a State valuation are idempotent (Bitset set semantics) — no double-count.

10.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong base case (i=0) -> entire vector offset; caught by prev_base/nested_yy fixtures asserting exact i=0 values. Off-by-one prev/cur swap (swap before reading root) -> root reads next-iteration garbage; assert out[i] recorded before swap — detection: monotonicity property tests on O/H fail immediately.
  • Mixed-Kind eval (future op slips through) -> fill_slots hits an X/U arm -> unreachable!()/panic; preferred over silent wrong answer. Kind gate in tl eval is the primary guard.
  • Severity: a past-LTL verdict flip is a monitoring correctness bug (feeds Phase 9 RV-LTL with past); cross-check O a ≡ True S a and H ≡ ¬O¬ as cheap internal oracles every eval in debug builds.

10.10 Operational Notes

  • tl eval --formula "p S q" --trace t.json prints per-index vector + final verdict (--last prints only holds_past); debug build --check-past-identities re-derives via True S a / ¬O¬a and asserts equality (oracle), off in release.
  • loop_start != None emits one stderr warning: "past-LTL evaluated over finite prefix; loop ignored".
  • Telemetry: log n, |slots|, elapsed, updates/s; feeds Phase 13 benchmarks (past_eval_throughput); forward-pass design composes with Phase 9 Monitor — a past-LTL monitor stores only prev (O(|sub|)) and updates per event, O(1) amortized, no history buffer, documented as the streaming-past extension hook.

23. Phase 11 — LTLf to DFA (finite-trace decision)

11.1 Objectives

  • Construct a minimal DFA D(φ) from an LTLf formula s.t. L(D(φ)) = { finite words w | eval_ltlf(φ, w) = true } — exact equivalence to Phase 4 eval_ltlf, not approximation.
  • Two interoperable construction paths (LOCKED choice: progression-to-DFA primary; alternating-automaton AFA path documented as cross-check): (a) progression subset construction — DFA state = a representative progressed Formula (mod equivalence); (b) AFA → NFA → DFA for differential validation.
  • Apply Hopcroft minimization (final partition refinement) → canonical, language-unique DFA; size target ≤ 2^|closure(φ)| worst case, typically O(|φ|)–O(2^|φ|).
  • Enable bounded / finite model checks: D(φ) runs in O(|w|) per trace; bounded Kripke check = synchronous product D(¬φ) × K↾bound, emptiness over reachable finite paths (no Büchi/lasso — finite acceptance).
  • Distinct from Phase 8 (infinite-trace, Büchi, nested DFS): here acceptance is a reaching set of accepting DFA states on a finite word, deterministic, no fairness.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-17 (progression-to-DFA for bounded/repeated LTLf checks), P7/De-Giacomo-Vardi-13 (LTLf→AFA→NFA→DFA construction), B5/Sipser (Hopcroft minimization), ADR-13 (progression shared with eval_ltlf in Phase 4), ADR-05 (NNF precondition) -->
  • Crate: tl-eval, new module tl-eval/src/ltlf_dfa.rs; re-uses progress (LOCKED home: tl-monitor::Monitor::progress, pub fn progress(f: &Formula, s: &State) -> Formula) and tl-formula::{nnf, closure, simplify}. New public surface:
// tl-eval/src/ltlf_dfa.rs
pub struct Dfa { pub nstates: usize, pub init: usize,
    pub edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard, usize)>>, // Guard reused from tl-automata
    pub accept: Vec<bool> }              // per-state finite acceptance flag
pub fn ltlf_to_dfa(f: &Formula) -> Dfa;          // progression subset construction + minimize
pub fn dfa_accepts(d: &Dfa, t: &Trace) -> bool;  // run prefix; accept = accept[final reached state]
pub fn check_ltlf_bounded(f: &Formula, k: &KripkeStructure, bound: usize) -> Result<(), Vec<usize>>;
  • Step 1 — End-of-trace progression semantics. LTLf needs a finite-word progression: define progress_eot(f) -> bool = truth of f evaluated at trace end (weak/strong next collapse per Phase 4): X g→false (strong) / g already consumed; G g→progress_eot(g); F g→progress_eot(g); g U h→progress_eot(h); True→true, False→false, Atom→false (no more states). DFA accept[q] = progress_eot(repr(q)).
  • Step 2 — Subset/derivative construction. State 0 = simplify(nnf(f)). Worklist over reached Formula reps. For each state q and each AP-valuation class (the letters = minterms over closure-atoms): q' = simplify(progress(repr(q), letter)). Edge (Guard(letter), idx(q')). Canonicalize reps (Step 4) before allocating a new index → dedup.
  • Step 3 — Letter symbolization. Do not enumerate 2^|AP| letters; group by the care-set = APs syntactically in φ. Build edges as Guard (mask+care DNF, LOCKED tl-automata::Guard); merge edges with identical target into one disjoined Guard. Result: a symbolic DFA, ≤ 2^care outgoing guards but stored compactly.
  • Step 4 — Formula canonicalization (equivalence key). Hash-cons reps via: nnf → simplify (idempotent/absorption/True,False propagation) → fixed AC-ordering of And/Or operands (sort by structural hash) → intern. Key = canonical form's hash. Two semantically-equal progressed formulas should map to one state (sound dedup; missed dedup only bloats, never wrong).
  • Step 5 — Minimization. fn minimize(d: &Dfa) -> Dfa = Hopcroft: initial partition {accept, ¬accept} × also split on the dead/sink class; refine by guard-labeled predecessors over the symbolic alphabet (split a block if some letter sends its members to ≥2 blocks). Yields the unique minimal DFA up to iso; reindex init→0.
  • Step 6 — Bounded model check. check_ltlf_bounded(φ, K, bound): build D(¬φ); DFS/BFS the product (K-state, DFA-state) for ≤bound steps; a reached state with accept[dfa]=true at depth ≤ bound = a finite counterexample path → Err(path of K-state indices); else Ok(()).

11.3 Deliverable

  • tl-eval/src/ltlf_dfa.rs exporting Dfa, ltlf_to_dfa, dfa_accepts, check_ltlf_bounded, minimize.
  • tl CLI: extend to-buchi with --ltlf-dfa flag → emit D(φ) as dot or HOA (acceptance Fin(∅)-style finite/f-acc), and eval honors LTLf via dfa_accepts as an alt engine (selectable --engine dfa).
  • DFA round-trips against Phase 4: dfa_accepts(ltlf_to_dfa(φ), t) == eval_ltlf(φ, t) for all fixtures.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

ID Criterion Target
A1 Equivalence to eval_ltlf 100% agreement over all corpus + ≥10k random (φ,t)
A2 Minimality ltlf_to_dfa output equals minimize output (already canonical); two equiv. formulas ⇒ isomorphic DFA
A3 Determinism/completeness every state has exactly one successor per letter (total δ); exactly one init
A4 Size bound states `≤ 2^
A5 Bounded check soundness check_ltlf_bounded Err-path replays through eval_ltlf(¬φ, path)=true; Ok ⇒ no acc. path ≤ bound
A6 Construction time `

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
ltlf_dfa_basic.txt G a, F a, a U b, X a, a & X b state counts {2,2,3,3,4}; accept flags listed
ltlf_dfa_eot.txt X true, X false, G a, F a over empty/1-state traces EOT verdicts: X* on len-1 → both false at end; G a/F a collapse to a
ltlf_dfa_equiv.json pairs (φ, t) mirroring Phase 4 corpus dfa_accepts == eval_ltlf each row
ltlf_dfa_minimal.txt `(a U b) (a U b), G a & G a, a & true`
ltlf_dfa_bounded.json (φ, K, bound, expect) triples Err(path)/Ok matches; path length ≤ bound
ltlf_dfa_malformed.txt infinite-only G F a as LTLf; CTL EX a; empty formula reject: Kind != Ltlf ⇒ ParseError/typed reject, never a wrong DFA

Malformed handling: feeding a non-LTLf Kind (CTL op, or LTL intended for infinite words) → hard error, not silent acceptance; G F a permitted syntactically but flagged as a trivial/degenerate finite property (warn: every nonempty trace satisfiable choices).

11.6 Pitfalls

  • EOT semantics divergence. Root cause: reusing infinite-trace progress without progress_eot; X weak/strong mismatch flips acceptance on the last letter. Symptom: off-by-one disagreement vs eval_ltlf only on traces where the formula's truth hinges on the final state. Debug: diff DFA accept[] against progress_eot table per state.
  • Incomplete canonicalization → state explosion (not wrong, but quadratic). Root cause: AC-unsorted And/Or reps create distinct keys for equal formulas; worklist balloons. Debug: log dedup hit-rate; expect ≥ near-Myhill-Nerode after simplify; if low, inspect canonical-key collisions.
  • Letter/minterm under-coverage. Root cause: building guards only for valuations seen, leaving δ partial → non-total transition function → dfa_accepts panics or mis-reaches on unseen AP combos. Fix: ensure a default/sink edge covers the complement guard (¬(⋁ existing guards)).
  • Sink-state acceptance. Root cause: forgetting False-rep is a non-accepting absorbing sink and True-rep is an accepting absorbing sink; merging them or mislabeling makes a universal/empty-language DFA. Debug: assert exactly one False-sink (accept=false, all self-loops) and True-sink (accept=true).
  • Minimization over symbolic alphabet. Root cause: Hopcroft refinement assumes concrete letters; splitting on Guard DNF requires guard intersection to test "block sends to ≥2 blocks". Naive guard equality misses overlaps → under-refinement (non-minimal) or over-split (still correct, larger). Debug: compare minimized size against AFA→NFA→DFA path on small φ.
  • Confusing LTLf-DFA with Phase 8 Büchi. Root cause: applying nested-DFS/lasso emptiness to a DFA. LTLf uses finite acceptance (reaching set), no cycle search. Symptom: spurious "holds" on properties with no finite witness.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • DFA construction worst-case O(2^|closure(φ)|) states; mitigate with hash-consed canonical reps (typical |φ|–2^care). Memory: arena-allocate reps; intern table keyed by canonical hash, ~32 B/state + edges.
  • Per-trace evaluation dfa_accepts: O(|w|) table lookups, branch-predictable, ~5–20 ns/step — the win vs re-progressing the formula each run (Phase 4 is O(|w|·|φ|)). Amortize DFA build across many traces in monitor/batch eval.
  • Hopcroft minimization O(|alphabet|·n·log n); symbolic alphabet keeps the |alphabet| factor at ≤ 2^care, not 2^|AP|. Target: 1000-state DFA minimized < 30 ms.
