  • Bounded check: product reachable states ≤ |K| · n_dfa; BFS to depth bound is O(bound · |K| · out-degree); cap memory via visited-set on (K-state, DFA-state).

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty trace (prefix=[], loop_start=None): DFA verdict = accept[init] = progress_eot(φ) (e.g. G a→true, F a→false, a→false). Must match eval_ltlf on the empty word.
  • Length-1 trace: single δ step then EOT; X φ always false (no successor state). Pinned in ltlf_dfa_eot.txt.
  • Pure boolean φ (a & b): DFA = 3 states (init, True-sink, False-sink); only first letter matters.
  • Tautology / contradiction: a | ¬a→universal DFA (1 accepting state, self-loop); a & ¬a→empty-language (1 non-accepting sink). Minimization must collapse to 1 state.
  • Trace with lasso (loop_start=Some): LTLf ignores the loop — dfa_accepts reads prefix only; document that LTLf is a finite-word logic (reject/warn if a caller expects infinite semantics).
  • Nested X deeper than trace: X X X a on len-2 trace → false; DFA encodes the countdown in states; verify no panic on running past a sink.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Severity Recovery
Wrong accept[] (EOT bug) A1 diff vs eval_ltlf critical (silent verdict flip) block release; bisect progress_eot per op
Non-total δ A3 assertion + fuzz unseen letters high (panic/UB-ish reach) add complement sink edge; re-run
Non-minimal DFA A2 cross-check vs AFA path medium (perf/size only) re-run Hopcroft; fix guard-intersection split
State explosion construction timeout / mem cap medium tighter simplify; abort + report `
Bounded false-Ok (bound too small) A5: Ok but full Phase 8 finds CE low (bound semantics, documented) report "no CE within bound", not "holds"

GRAVEST per spine: a construction bug silently flips an LTLf verdict with no crash — primary guard is A1 differential vs eval_ltlf + Phase 13 differential vs Spot's ltl2dfa/ltl2tgba on finite semantics.

11.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tl to-buchi "G a" --ltlf-dfa --format dot and tl eval --ltlf --engine dfa -f formula.txt -t trace.json; --engine progression (Phase 4) is the differential oracle.
  • Emit DFA in dot (default) and HOA with explicit finite/f acceptance; states labeled with their canonical Formula rep (pretty-printed) for debugging.
  • Feature-gate ltlf_dfa (default on); --dump-dfa-stats prints |states|, |edges|, dedup hit-rate, minimization ratio.
  • Determinism: construction is order-independent (canonical reps); golden-file the dot output for pattern formulas so size regressions are caught in CI.
  • Logging (RUST_LOG=tl_eval::ltlf_dfa): trace worklist growth, per-state EOT flag, minimization partition count; warn on Kind != Ltlf and on degenerate (universal/empty) DFAs.

24. Phase 12 — Optimizations: rewrite, caching, symbolic guards, on-the-fly product

12.1 Objectives

  • Cut automaton + product size/time >=10x on benchmark formulas (Phase 13 set) vs naive Phase 6–8 path, preserving L(.) exactly (language equivalence, differential-checked vs Spot).
  • Four orthogonal levers, all behind feature flags, all reversible to the naive path for A/B differential testing:
    • Rewrite/simplify Formula pre-ltl_to_ngba -> fewer subformulas -> fewer Ngba nodes.
    • State caching/memoization in GPVW + degeneralize -> dedup syntactically/canonically equal nodes -> no node blowup.
    • Symbolic edge Guards (bitset mask+care, optional BDD) -> compact edges, fast Guard conjunction/sat during nested DFS.
    • On-the-fly product -> product states materialized lazily inside emptiness nested DFS; unreachable product states never built.
  • Invariant: every optimization is language-preserving (or holds/Lasso-preserving for the model check); a counterexample Lasso must still be valid in the un-optimized product.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-18 (on-the-fly product as Phase 12 optimization), P5/P1 (GPVW on-the-fly motivation), P9/Gastin-Oddoux-01 (simplification ideas), P10/Somenzi-Bloem-00 (rewrite rules, state merging), ADR-06 (GPVW lazy expansion), INV-2 (optimization must be language-preserving) -->

  • tl-formula/src/rewrite.rs — pub fn simplify(f: &Formula) -> Formula; fixpoint of local rewrite rules until no change (cap 32 passes), applied post-nnf:

    Rule (LTL identity) Rewrite Why
    X(True),X(False) True/False const fold
    f U True, F True True; G False->False absorbing
    f U False False (if f != True); True U g=F g absorption
    F F g,G G g,X over const F g,G g,fold idempotence
    F G F g F G g; G F G g->G F g collapse FG/GF towers
    X f U X g X(f U g) pull X out (fewer eventualities)
    `(f U g) (f U h)` `f U (g
    a & a,`a a,a & !a` a,a,False
    R/F/G duals normalize via NNF so simplify sees canonical ops one form per class
    • Hash-cons subformulas (Ap-keyed) so equal subtrees compared by id; rule matching is match on Formula shape, children already simplified (bottom-up).

  • tl-automata/src/cache.rs — node memo for GPVW: key = canonical(now_set, next_set) (sorted-by-id Vec<Formula> pair, or a u64 hash of it); HashMap<NodeKey, usize> -> Ngba state index. ltl_to_ngba checks cache before allocating a new node; identical obligation sets reuse the state -> kills exponential duplication. Same memo applies in degeneralize: key (ngba_state, counter).

  • tl-automata/src/guard.rs — Guard = { mask: Bitset, care: Bitset } (positive APs in mask, constrained APs in care; AP i required true iff care[i]&mask[i], false iff care[i]&!mask[i], don't-care iff !care[i]). Ops: pub fn conj(&self, o: &Guard) -> Option<Guard> (None = contradiction false guard, pruned), pub fn sat(&self, s: &State) -> bool ((s.aps ^ mask) & care == 0). Optional feature="bdd": Guard -> shared reduced BDD (tl-automata/src/bdd.rs) for many-AP edges; bitset is default (faster <=64 APs).

  • tl-automata/src/product_otf.rs — replaces eager product in the model-check path. emptiness takes a lazy successor closure instead of a built Nba:

    pub fn emptiness_otf(succ: impl Fn(ProdState)->Vec<ProdState>,
                     init: Vec<ProdState>, is_accept: impl Fn(ProdState)->bool) -> Option<Lasso>;

    ProdState = (usize /*Nba*/, usize /*Kripke*/). succ expands one product state on demand: for each (Guard, q') edge of the Nba and each Kripke successor k', emit (q',k') iff Guard::sat(K.label(k')). Outer/inner DFS color tables keyed by ProdState in a HashMap/dense map; only visited product states ever allocated. check_ltl calls emptiness_otf with Nba(simplify(nnf(Not(f)))).

12.3 Deliverable

  • Feature-flagged optimized pipeline: simplify + cached GPVW + symbolic Guards + emptiness_otf; CLI flag tl ... --opt={none|rewrite|cache|symbolic|otf|all} (default all).
  • A benchmark harness tl-automata/benches/opt.rs reporting, per formula, naive vs optimized Ngba/Nba/product state counts + build/check time + peak product states materialized.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric (benchmark formula set, Phase 13 patterns + 1000 random) Target
Median Nba state-count reduction (opt vs naive) >=2x; tail formulas >=10x
Median product peak states (otf vs eager) **<=10%** of eager (>=10x)
End-to-end check_ltl time, opt vs naive >=5x median speedup
Language equivalence opt vs naive (per formula) 100% (differential, all formulas)
simplify idempotence: simplify(simplify(f)) == simplify(f) always
Guard::conj correctness vs truth-table enumeration (<=8 APs) 100%

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
simplify_const.txt X(p & !p), F True, True U q False, True, F q
simplify_idemp.txt F F p, G G p, `(p U q) (p U r)`
cache_dedup.txt G(p->X q) & G(p->X q) Ngba states(opt) < states(naive); same L
guard_conj.txt guards {p},{p,!q},{!p}&{p} {p}; {p,!q}; None (contradiction)
otf_unreach.dot tiny Kripke (3 states) x Nba(8 states), only 4 product states reachable otf materializes 4, eager 24
opt_equiv.json 200 random formulas per-formula L_opt == L_naive (all true)
Malformed --opt=foo; cyclic rewrite would-loop formula CLI error unknown opt level; simplify halts at 32-pass cap, logs rewrite-cap-hit, result still equivalent

12.6 Pitfalls

  • Non-confluent / oscillating rewrite (F G f <-> G F f ping-pong) -> infinite loop; root cause: rules not normalizing to one direction. Fix: orient every rule by a strict size/operator order; cap 32 passes + assert no-change at cap; debug by logging rewrite trace per pass.
  • Cache key collision / over-merge: hashing obligation sets without canonical sort merges distinct nodes -> wrong language (silent verdict flip). Fix: key on sorted-by-Ap-id canonical form, not raw hash; assert key_eq => set_eq in debug; differential vs naive catches it.
  • Guard mask w/o care: forgetting care makes don't-care APs read as "must be false" -> spurious edge pruning -> lost runs -> false holds. Fix: sat = (aps^mask)&care==0; fixture guard_conj.txt; test against full valuation enumeration.
  • Otf cycle extraction wrong: lazy DFS finds an accepting cycle but reconstructs Lasso from the expansion order, not the back-edge path -> invalid counterexample. Fix: record predecessor ProdState on the DFS stack; Lasso.stem/.cycle rebuilt from outer+inner stacks; validate Lasso replays through eager product (Phase 8 check).
  • Simplify before vs after NNF: simplifying pre-NNF leaves negations that GPVW assumes absent -> panic/UB in tableau. Fix: contract simplify consumes NNF, returns NNF; assert NNF on entry+exit.
  • BDD var-order blowup: bad AP ordering makes BDD Guards larger than bitset -> regression. Fix: BDD only when #APs>64 or feature="bdd"; static AP-interning order; bench gate refuses BDD path if larger.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • simplify is O(passes * formula_size), passes <=32, size shrinks monotonically -> cheap vs downstream automaton blowup it prevents.
  • Cache HashMap lookups dominate GPVW; use FxHash on canonical u64 key; arena-allocate Ngba nodes for cache locality.
  • Guard::conj/sat are 2–3 bitwise ops on Bitset (u64 for <=64 APs) -> O(1); BDD path is O(bdd_size) so reserved for wide-AP.
  • Otf peak memory = reachable product states only; target peak <=10% eager. Watch HashMap rehash; pre-size to estimated reachable count (|Nba| * branching heuristic).

12.8 Edge Cases

  • simplify(True)/simplify(False) -> identity; constant formula -> Nba of 1–2 states; otf product still correct (trivial).
  • Formula with no Until -> zero generalized accept sets -> degeneralize is no-op; ensure cache + otf handle empty accept.
  • All-don't-care Guard (care==0) = true edge -> every Kripke successor matches; otf must not treat as no-edge.
  • Random formula that simplify collapses to True/False: check_ltl returns Ok/immediate Lasso without building automaton.
  • Kripke with self-loops only -> otf cycle = single state; Lasso.cycle length 1; verify nested-DFS accepting self-loop detection.
  • Optimization flags composed (--opt=rewrite alone, cache alone) must each individually preserve L.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Symptom Detection Recovery
Rewrite changes language opt vs naive L differ on some formula opt_equiv.json differential bisect offending rule; revert rule; gate on per-rule equivalence test
Cache over-merges smaller automaton, wrong verdict naive-vs-opt verdict mismatch disable cache (--opt=rewrite); fix canonical key
Otf misses reachable accepting cycle reports holds falsely cross-check vs eager emptiness on small models fall back to eager product; fix DFS coloring
Guard prunes valid edge lost counterexample fixture otf_unreach/guard_conj recompute care; enumeration test
Rewrite cap hit rewrite-cap-hit log, slow metrics counter inspect non-confluent rule pair; re-orient
  • GRAVEST: any opt path that flips holds/L silently — primary guard is the per-formula naive-vs-opt differential + Spot cross-check (Phase 13); CI fails the build on a single mismatch.

12.10 Operational Notes

  • tl ... --opt=<level> selects level; --bench emits the size/time/peak table; --no-opt alias for --opt=none (golden naive path kept forever for differential testing).
  • Metrics logged: ngba_states, nba_states, product_peak_states, rewrite_passes, cache_hits/misses, guard_contradictions_pruned, otf_states_materialized.
  • Feature flags: bdd (off by default), every optimization independently togglable via Cargo features opt-rewrite/opt-cache/opt-symbolic/opt-otf so a regression can be isolated to one lever.
  • Determinism: rewrite + cache must produce a canonical automaton (stable state numbering) for reproducible HOA/dot output and golden tests; seed any iteration order (BTree over canonical keys, not HashMap, for output paths).

25. Phase 13 — Validation: differential vs Spot, fuzzing, benchmarks

The "done" gate. No phase-14. Closes the GRAVEST failure class (silent verdict flip from a wrong GPVW/degeneralization/nested-DFS) by differential cross-checking every Nba/Ngba and every model-check verdict against Spot (ltl2tgba), plus fuzzing, a conformance ledger, and gated criterion benchmarks.

13.1 Objectives

  • Differential tl ↔ Spot: automaton language-equivalence (cross-product emptiness, both directions) on LTL pattern + random formulas; check_ltl verdict + Lasso agreement on random Kripke structures.
  • CTL: CtlChecker::sat/holds vs hand-computed + literature fixtures (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla); no Spot for CTL (Spot = LTL-only).
  • Fuzz targets parse, ltl2buchi, eval, monitor to a fixed CI budget → zero crashes / panics / hangs.
  • Conformance buckets (LTL, CTL, LTLf, past, monitoring) with a pass/fail/known-fail ledger (CI fails on any unexpected fail or known-fail that started passing); criterion benches (formula→automaton size+time; model-check time; monitor throughput) with committed baselines + regression gates, publish BENCH_REPORT.md.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- anchors: ADR-21 (Spot as differential oracle), ADR-22 (cargo-fuzz infrastructure), ADR-23 (Criterion benchmarks), P3/CVWY-92 (nested DFS correctness validated here), P1/GPVW-95 (automaton language equivalence cross-checked vs ltl2tgba), INV-1 (correctness contract: holds iff product empty) -->

New top-level dirs/crates (not workspace members; dev-only or feature-gated):

Path Role
xtests/ (crate tl-difftest) differential harness; tl lib + spot subprocess via HOA
xtests/src/spot.rs shell out ltl2tgba -H (HOA), autfilt; parse Spot HOA → local Nba/Ngba
xtests/src/hoa.rs HOA v1 reader (reuse Phase 6/12 to-buchi --hoa writer for the inverse)
xtests/src/equiv.rs nba_lang_equiv(a,b) -> Equiv via cross-product emptiness, both inclusions
xtests/src/patterns.rs Dwyer/Spot pattern LTL families (response/precedence/absence/...)
xtests/src/randltl.rs random Formula gen (depth/AP-count knobs) + Spot randltl import
xtests/src/randkripke.rs random KripkeStructure (n states, edge prob, AP labeling), forced-total
fuzz/ (cargo-fuzz, libFuzzer) targets fuzz_parse, fuzz_ltl2buchi, fuzz_eval, fuzz_monitor
conformance/ bucket YAML/JSON cases + ledger.toml (status per case-id)
benches/ (criterion) bench_automaton.rs, bench_modelcheck.rs, bench_monitor.rs

Key interfaces:

pub enum Equiv { Equal, ALessB(Lasso), BLessA(Lasso) }       // counterexample word on mismatch
fn nba_lang_equiv(a: &Nba, b: &Nba) -> Equiv;                 // L(a)=L(b) iff A\B and B\A both empty
fn check_vs_spot(f: &Formula) -> Result<(), Mismatch>;        // ngba/nba acceptance cross-check
fn modelcheck_vs_spot(f: &Formula, k: &KripkeStructure) -> Result<(), Mismatch>;
fn ctl_vs_oracle(case: &CtlCase) -> Result<(), Mismatch>;     // sat-vector compare
  • nba_lang_equiv: complementation-free — build Nba(¬φ_tl) and Nba(φ_spot), etc.; equivalence checked as L(a)\L(b)=∅ ∧ L(b)\L(a)=∅ using the SAME product+emptiness (nested DFS) the engine ships. Difference A\B realized by intersecting a with a Buchi for the complement of b; since complement is expensive, instead cross-check both as automata for φ and ¬φ from each tool and assert L(tl(φ)) ∩ L(spot(¬φ)) = ∅ and symmetric — emptiness of intersection, no complementation.
  • modelcheck_vs_spot: feed identical (φ,K) to check_ltl and to Spot (K exported as HOA Kripke / explicit product); compare Ok/Err; on Err, replay tl's Lasso against φ via eval_ltlf-style lasso evaluation to confirm it truly violates (lasso self-check, independent of Spot).
  • Fuzz: each target wraps a panic-catch; structured input via arbitrary (e.g. fuzz_eval: (Formula, Trace) then assert eval_ltlf total + progress-fold agrees with eval_ltlf).
  • Conformance runner reads ledger.toml, runs each bucket, diffs status; emits JUnit XML for CI.

13.3 Deliverable

cargo xtask validate runs: differential suite (patterns + N random), CTL oracle suite, conformance buckets, and a 60 s fuzz smoke per target; cargo bench regenerates baselines; xtask bench-report writes BENCH_REPORT.md. CI job green ⇒ project "done": green conformance, zero fuzz findings in budget, published benchmark report within gates.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
LTL differential (patterns) 100% language-equivalent vs Spot; 0 Mismatch
LTL differential (random) ≥ 50 000 formulas (depth ≤ 8, ≤ 4 APs), 0 mismatch
Model-check differential ≥ 10 000 (φ,K) pairs, verdict agree 100%; every tl Lasso self-validates
CTL oracle 100% of literature + hand cases, exact sat bit-vectors
Fuzz CI budget 4×15 min (or 50M execs/target), 0 crashes/OOM/timeouts; corpus committed
Conformance all buckets pass; known-fail set documented + non-empty-justified
Bench regression no metric > +10% time / +15% size vs committed baseline

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Content / expected
conformance/ltl/patterns.toml Dwyer response G(p→F q), precedence, absence; per-case expected Nba.nstates range + Spot-equiv=true
conformance/ctl/cesa86.toml mutual-exclusion / AG(req→AF ack) over named Kripke; expected sat bit-vector + holds
conformance/ltlf/finite.toml eval_ltlf(F p, [{}, {p}])=true; eval_ltlf(X p, [{p}])=false (weak-next at end)
conformance/past/dp.toml O p, H p, p S q over traces; expected per-state truth
conformance/monitor/ltl3.toml stream → RvVerdict sequence; G p on …,{¬p} ⇒ False; F p good-prefix ⇒ True
conformance/ledger.toml `[case.id] status = "pass"
malformed: bad_hoa.hoa truncated HOA → xtests reports parse error, not silent equiv-pass
malformed: nontotal.kripke dead-end state → loader rejects (Phase 3 validation)
malformed: oracle_mismatch.toml deliberately-wrong expected sat → runner must FAIL (meta-test the harness)

13.6 Pitfalls

  • AP-naming skew with Spot: Spot relabels/orders APs; mapping tl Ap id ↔ Spot proposition by string is essential — mismatched bit positions ⇒ phantom non-equiv. Debug: dump both Guard truth tables per edge.
  • ltl2tgba operator-syntax drift (<>,[],W,M): translate Formula→Spot syntax via one printer; a wrong R/W mapping silently yields a different formula that may still pass on weak fixtures. Debug: round-trip φ→spot-str→spot-parse→HOA→equiv(φ).
  • Trivially-empty masking: if BOTH sides build the empty/false automaton from a bug, cross-product emptiness passes vacuously. Guard: include positive-language assertions (L≠∅ for satisfiable φ) and check accepting-run existence.
  • Fuzz finds reductions, not equivalence: a fuzz panic in ltl_to_ngba may be a real GPVW eventuality bug. Triage: cargo fuzz tmin then replay through to-buchi --hoa + equiv.
  • Spot version nondeterminism: automaton size varies across Spot releases ⇒ never assert exact Spot state counts, only language equivalence + tl's own size bounds (pin Spot version in Docker for the size-regression baseline only). Lasso false-positive: a Lasso whose cycle isn't actually accepting (inner-DFS color bug) self-validates wrong if the replayer reuses buggy code; replayer must be an independent lasso evaluator over φ (NNF).

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Differential cross-product is O(|Nba_φ|·|Nba_¬φ|); cap random φ size (closure ≤ ~20) so each pair < 50 ms, budget total random suite < 10 min on CI; Spot subprocess spawn dominates — batch via one ltl2tgba invocation over a formula file, parse N HOA automata, amortize fork cost.
  • Fuzz corpus pruning (cargo fuzz cmin) keeps coverage corpus < 5 MB, commit minimized corpus to seed CI; criterion warm-up 3 s, 100 samples, isolate to a dedicated runner / pin CPU governor to make the +10% gate stable (else noise → flaky fail).

13.8 Edge Cases

Case Expected
φ = True / False universal / empty Nba; equiv vs Spot; holds trivially true/false
Tautology G(p ∨ ¬p) language = all words; product-with-¬φ empty ⇒ holds on every K
Pure-past φ in LTL bucket routed to past-DP, not GPVW; differential N/A (Spot future-only path) — bucket-skip with reason
Single-state K, self-loop smallest model-check; lasso = stem [], cycle [0]
Random φ that is unsatisfiable Nba empty; L≠∅ positive-check correctly skipped; equiv still asserted
Empty trace / loop_start at 0 LTLf + monitor fixtures cover; harness must not divide-by-zero on length 0

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Response
Verdict flip (holds↔CE) modelcheck_vs_spot disagree OR lasso self-check fails block release; minimize φ,K; bisect GPVW/degeneralize/nestedDFS
Automaton non-equiv Equiv::ALessB/BLessA word emit witness ω-word lasso; reduce φ via fuzz tmin
Fuzz crash/hang libFuzzer artifact / timeout commit repro to fuzz/artifacts, open regression test, fix before merge
Known-fail regressed ledger diff: known_fail→pass unannounced fail CI (forces ledger update — keeps ledger honest)
Bench regression criterion > +10%/+15% fail gate; attach flamegraph; either fix or re-baseline with justification

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Dockerfile.validate pins Spot (e.g. 2.12), cargo-fuzz, nightly-for-fuzz; CI matrix: stable (tests/benches) + nightly (fuzz); CI gating: PR runs conformance + patterns + 5 min fuzz + bench-no-gate, nightly runs 50k random + 4×15 min fuzz + bench-with-gate, main requires both green.
  • BENCH_REPORT.md published as a build artifact + committed under docs/; includes formula→Nba.nstates/time table, model-check time per pattern, monitor events/sec.
  • tl --version embeds the validated Spot version + git SHA so a verdict is traceable to the oracle it was differentially checked against; reproducibility: random suite seeded (TL_DIFF_SEED), seed logged on failure, fuzz corpus + artifacts committed so any finding re-runs deterministically.

Threat Model

Scope: tl is an offline/embeddable evaluator — no network listener, no auth, no privileged syscalls. The asset under protection is verdict integrity (a correct holds/counterexample/RvVerdict) and bounded resource use under adversarial input. STRIDE collapses to: Tampering (wrong verdict), Denial-of-Service (blowup/hang/OOM), and supply-chain Tampering. Spoofing/Repudiation/EoP are out-of-scope (no identity, no privilege transition). Threat model = explicit-state engine; symbolic/BDD attack surface belongs to #281, not here.

25.1 Trust boundaries

                 ATTACKER-CONTROLLED                         TRUSTED CORE
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐   B1   ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ formula text (CLI arg / file / stdin) │ ─────► │ tl-syntax Lexer/Parser ──► tl-formula  │
  │ trace file (.json/.txt -> Trace)      │ ─────► │ nnf/closure/Kind  (Result-only, no     │
  │ Kripke file (-> KripkeStructure)      │   B2   │ panic, fuel-bounded)                   │
  │ event stream (monitor: stdin lines)   │ ─────► │ tl-automata / tl-eval / tl-monitor     │
  └──────────────────────────────────────┘        └───────────────────────────────────────┘
        │ B3 (build/CI)                                          │ B4 (output: untrusted sink)
        ▼                                                        ▼
   crates.io deps (deny.toml gate)                   stdout HOA/dot/JSON, exit code, lasso CE
  • B1 formula text — untrusted, unbounded length, arbitrary nesting/operators. Highest-leverage surface: drives parser + automaton size.
  • B2 trace / Kripke / event stream — untrusted structured data: state count, edge count, AP count, prefix length, loop_start index, successor indices.
  • B3 build inputs — Cargo dependency graph; transitively executes build scripts. Guarded by deny.toml + pinned Cargo.lock.
  • B4 outputs — HOA/dot/JSON written to an untrusted sink; must not be an injection vector for the next tool (no shell metachar emission, escaped dot labels).
  • In-scope inputs cross B1/B2 only; everything inside the trusted core trusts itself (no re-validation between tl-* crates — invariants asserted once at the boundary, see §7.17).

25.2 Threat register (attack → mitigation → detection → telemetry → recovery)

# Threat Vector (B) Severity Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Automaton-blowup DoS — ltl_to_ngba worst-case 2^O(|cl(φ)|) states; crafted nested U/X explodes mem/time B1 --max-states (default 2^20), --max-closure (default 1024 subformulas), wall-clock --timeout (default 10 s), incremental state-count check inside the GPVW lazy expansion loop state counter exceeds cap mid-construction; deadline elapsed states_built, closure_size, elapsed_ms, cap_hit=states|time abort construction, return Err(EvalError::AutomatonTooLarge{built,cap}); never partial-OOM
T2 Parser DoS / panic — adversarial formula (deep nesting ((((…)))), unbalanced, huge AP names) triggers stack overflow or panic B1 iterative/explicit-depth parser or --max-depth (default 4096) checked per recursion; Result-only API (ParseError/ParseErrorKind); cap raw input bytes (--max-formula-bytes, default 1 MiB) depth counter exceeds cap; lexer rejects oversize token parse_depth, input_bytes, ParseErrorKind typed ParseError with byte offset; process exits non-zero, no crash
T3 Pathological Kripke / huge trace — KripkeStructure with nstates×edges dense (n² blowup) or Trace.prefix length ≈ ∞ B2 --max-kripke-states (default 2^20), --max-edges (default 2^24), --max-trace-len (default 2^24); validate before labeling/product allocates counts exceed cap at parse/validate; allocation guarded kripke_states, kripke_edges, trace_len Err(EvalError::ModelTooLarge); reject before any O(n²) work begins
T4 Wrong-verdict (silent correctness) — GPVW/degeneralize/product/nested-DFS or NNF bug flips holds↔counterexample with no crash. GRAVEST class. B1+B2 NNF + GPVW + labeling share one LOCKED operator set; debug-mode structural invariants (§7.17); two independent oracles for LTL (automata check_ltl vs LTLf progression on lasso unrolling) differential testing vs Spot (ltl2tgba) on pattern + random formulas (Phase 13); CTL vs Clarke-Emerson-Sistla known results; verdict-cross-check oracle disagreement oracle_mismatch{formula,kripke,ours,spot} corpus entry; coverage % of pattern set mismatch → CI hard-fail + frozen regression fixture; engine never ships on a known mismatch
T5 Monitor unbounded memory — progress() residual Formula grows per event on a long/adversarial stream → OOM B2 (stream) canonicalize+simplify after each progress step; bound residual node-count (--max-residual, default 2^16); prefer monitor-automaton (finite states) over raw progression for long streams residual node-count exceeds cap; same residual recurs (cycle) residual_nodes, events_seen, verdict_emitted_at switch to automaton mode or Err; emit last definitive RvVerdict then stop
T6 Integer overflow in indexing — Ap=u32, state/successor usize indices; product nstates × counter (degeneralize) or Nba × Kripke (product) overflows usize core checked_mul/checked_add on every product/degeneralize dimension; Ap interning bounded to u32::MAX; #![deny(arithmetic_overflow)], release builds overflow-checks=true for index math checked-arith returns None; debug overflow panic in tests product_dim, ap_count Err(EvalError::IndexOverflow); no wraparound → no OOB indexing
T7 Supply-chain Tampering — malicious/yanked transitive dep or build script B3 minimal deps (no_std + alloc for tl-syntax/-formula/-trace/-automata/-eval/-monitor; std only in tl-cli); deny.toml (advisories=deny, unmaintained=warn, license allowlist, banned crates); pinned Cargo.lock; cargo vet/cargo-deny in CI cargo deny check fails; lockfile diff in review advisory IDs, new-dep diff block merge; revert lockfile
T8 Output injection — emitted dot/HOA label echoes attacker formula text into a downstream renderer/shell B4 escape dot string labels; HOA per-spec quoting; never shell-interpolate; APs printed as interned ids or quoted names golden-output diff includes adversarial AP names n/a (offline) reject unrenderable label at serialize time
T9 Fuzzing-corpus / conformance-ledger poisoning — a PR commits a crafted conformance/ fixture or ledger.toml edit encoding an incorrect expected verdict, later trusted as ground truth B3 fixture-diff review requires a re-derived expected value (Spot output attached) for any expected/ledger.toml edit; corpus content-hashed in CI to flag out-of-band edits fixture-diff bot flags an expected-field change with no accompanying Spot-output diff fixture_expected_changed_total revert the fixture edit; treat as a P3 (oracle-trust) incident, block merge
T10 Subprocess argument injection into Spot — xtests shells out to ltl2tgba; a crafted formula string could inject shell metacharacters if invocation is not argv-safe B1 (formula flows into the Spot subprocess) invoke via std::process::Command argv array, never a shell string; formula passed by temp file above a length threshold fuzz_spot_invoke target (feature-gated, requires Spot) asserts no shell metachar reaches a shell; static lint bans Command::new("sh") spot_invoke_argv_len, spot_invoke_failures_total reject oversized/unexpected-byte formulas before invoking Spot; never fall back to a shell string

25.3 DoS budget — quantified caps (defaults; all --flag overridable, capped at hard ceilings)

Resource Soft default Hard ceiling Enforced in Error on breach
formula bytes 1 MiB 16 MiB tl-syntax Lexer ParseError(InputTooLarge)
parse depth 4096 65536 Parser ParseError(NestingTooDeep)
closure size |cl(φ)| 1024 4096 closure() EvalError::ClosureTooLarge
Ngba/Nba states 2^20 2^24 ltl_to_ngba/degeneralize EvalError::AutomatonTooLarge
Kripke states 2^20 2^24 KripkeStructure validate EvalError::ModelTooLarge
Kripke edges 2^24 2^26 validate EvalError::ModelTooLarge
trace prefix len 2^24 2^26 Trace validate EvalError::TraceTooLarge
monitor residual nodes 2^16 2^20 Monitor::step EvalError::MonitorDiverged
wall-clock per op 10 s none (op-level) tl-cli deadline EvalError::Timeout
peak RSS n/a OS ulimit -v (CI: 2 GiB) OS / container OOM-kill (CI red)
  • Why op-level deadline, not global: nested DFS + on-the-fly product (Phase 12) interleave construction and search — the deadline is polled at the outer-DFS state-pop and inside the GPVW expansion loop (every 2^14 states) so a hang is bounded without per-edge overhead.
  • Caps are pre-allocation gates (T1/T3/T6): counts validated before the Vec reservation that would otherwise OOM. No "allocate then check".

25.4 The silent-verdict threat (T4) — defense in depth

T4 is gravest because it produces a plausible wrong answer, not a crash. Layered defense:

  1. Single LOCKED operator set + NNF — GPVW, CtlChecker labeling, and progression all consume NNF over the same Formula enum; one normalization path, not three, removes divergent-rewrite bugs.
  2. Structural invariants (§7.17) assert internal consistency in debug/CI builds (e.g. every Ngba.accept set ⊆ states; degeneralized counter ∈ 0..k).
  3. Two independent LTL deciders — check_ltl (automata: product + nested-DFS emptiness) cross-checked against LTLf-progression on a finite unrolling of any candidate Lasso (the lasso is a finite-checkable witness): a reported counterexample must actually violate φ on its own unrolling, else CI fails.
  4. Differential oracle vs Spot (Phase 13, the done-gate): to-buchi automaton acceptance and check verdicts compared to ltl2tgba on the standard LTL pattern corpus + ≥10^5 random formulas; any mismatch is frozen as a regression fixture.
  5. CTL ground truth — CtlChecker sat labelings compared to Clarke-Emerson-Sistla worked examples + small hand-checked Kripke structures.

Detection without #4 would be near-impossible — there is no panic, no leak, no telemetry signal from a logically-wrong-but-well-formed automaton. Differential testing is the only reliable detector for T4 and is therefore the project "done" gate, not optional hardening.

25.5 Fuzzing surface (continuous, Phase 13)

Target Harness input Oracle / assert
Parser (T2) random bytes + grammar-guided formulas no panic; Result always; round-trip parse∘pretty-print = identity on success
ltl_to_ngba/degeneralize (T1,T4) random NNF formula ≤ depth cap no panic; state cap honored; degeneralized Nba language = Ngba language on sampled words
eval_ltlf/progress (T4,T5) random formula × random Trace progression verdict = direct finite-semantics eval; residual stays bounded
emptiness nested DFS (T4) random Nba reported Lasso is a real accepting cycle; no Lasso ⇒ exhaustive-search agrees (small n)
Monitor::step (T5) random formula × long random stream residual node-count bounded; RvVerdict monotone toward definitive

25.6 Memory-safety posture

  • #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in all seven tl-* crates — automaton graphs are arena/Vec-indexed, no raw pointers, so the C++-Spot manual-memory hazards (on-the-fly construction use-after-free) are eliminated by construction (the Rust rationale, restated).
  • All indexing of edges/states/accept/prefix via checked access in debug; release relies on cap-validated indices (T6) — index provenance is internal (built from validated counts), never directly attacker-supplied as a raw usize into an array.

25.7 Out-of-scope / residual risks (explicit)

  • Side channels / timing: verdict timing leaks formula structure — out-of-scope (offline tool, no secret formula).
  • Floating point: none used (no probabilistic/MTL/TCTL — those are non-goals); no FP nondeterminism risk.
  • Concurrency: single-threaded core; if a future parallel product is added, the nested-DFS coloring invariant becomes a data-race surface — flagged for that phase, not present now.
  • Residual T1: caps bound resource use but a legitimate large model can hit them → false "too large"; mitigated by override flags up to hard ceilings, documented in tl-cli help.

Failure Modes and Recovery

Taxonomy of every way the tl engine fails, ordered by severity. Verdict soundness is the gravest invariant: holds/violated, eval_ltlf bool, and RvVerdict must NEVER be silently wrong. A crash is recoverable; a flipped verdict is a correctness breach. Detection-of-last-resort for the soundness classes is differential testing vs Spot (ltl2tgba) + lasso re-simulation.

Severity ladder

Sev Class Silent? Blast radius
S0 Wrong verdict (holds/check_ltl/eval_ltlf/CtlChecker::sat flipped) YES model-check answer is a lie; downstream trusts it
S0 LTL3 verdict instability (definitive RvVerdict later changes) YES RV monitor un-soundness; alarms recalled/spurious
S0 Nondeterministic verdict (same input → different answer) YES reproducibility + soundness gone
S1 Lasso fails to re-simulate (extraction bug) partial counterexample untrustworthy; verdict suspect
S2 Automaton-size-cap overflow (Ngba/Nba/product blowup) NO (capped) OOM/timeout; query unanswerable
S2 Monitor formula blowup (progressed Formula unbounded) NO (capped) streaming monitor stalls/OOM
S3 Ill-formed CTL (path-vs-state misuse) NO (rejected) query rejected pre-eval
S3 Parse error (ParseError) NO (rejected) input rejected, no eval
S2 Spot differential-oracle unavailable/version-mismatched NO (job fails loud) Phase 13 differential gate cannot run; release blocked, not silently skipped
S3 Malformed Kripke/Trace input (validation rejection) NO (rejected) query rejected pre-eval; no evaluator ever sees an ill-formed model

S3 — Parse errors

  • Symptoms: tl-syntax returns Err(ParseError{kind, span}); tl exits nonzero before any eval.
  • Root cause: bad token (ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken), unbalanced parens, unknown AP charset, precedence-ambiguous input (e.g. p U q U r associativity), EOF mid-formula.
  • Detection: total — Lexer/Parser reject; never reaches tl-formula.
  • Automatic recovery: none (correct behavior is rejection); CLI prints caret-underlined span + expected-token set.
  • Manual recovery: operator fixes formula text; re-run.
  • Operator actions: consult grammar EBNF (Phase 1/2); check operator spelling (X F G U R future; Y O H S past; EX AX EG AG EF AF CTL).
  • Forensic artifacts: ParseError{kind, span}, byte offset, offending token, expected set.
  • Long-term mitigation: fuzz Parser (Phase 13); golden error-message corpus; never panic! on bad input — always Result.

S3 — Ill-formed CTL (path-vs-state distinction)

  • Symptoms: tl-formula well-formedness rejects; Kind classification ≠ Ctl though CTL operators present.
  • Root cause: bare path operator without fused quantifier (X p, p U q directly under a CTL context — CTL requires Ex/Ax/Eu/Au/...); mixing LTL path subformula inside a CtlChecker query; nesting a state formula where a path formula is required.
  • Detection: closure() + Kind check classify before CtlChecker::sat is ever called; mismatch → reject. NEVER feed a non-Ctl Formula to CtlChecker (it would mis-label).
  • Automatic recovery: reject with kind-mismatch diagnostic; suggest the fused operator (e.g. X p → did you mean Ex(p)/Ax(p)?).
  • Manual recovery: rewrite as a legal CTL state formula.
  • Operator actions: pick check (CTL) vs to-buchi/check-ltl (LTL) deliberately; the binary keys the algorithm off Kind.
  • Forensic artifacts: classified Kind, the offending subformula node, expected category (state vs path).
  • Long-term mitigation: well-formedness unit fixtures for every operator slot; reject early, before NNF.

S2 — Automaton-size-cap overflow

  • Symptoms: ltl_to_ngba / degeneralize / product exceed a configured state cap (default --max-states 200_000); CLI aborts with AutomatonTooLarge{phase, states} rather than OOM.
  • Root cause: GPVW worst case is 2^O(|cl(f)|) Ngba states; degeneralization multiplies by k accept sets; product multiplies by |Kripke.states|. Deeply nested U/R or huge formula → blowup.
  • Detection: arena counter checked at each node allocation against the cap; NOT silent — hard stop with phase + count.
  • Automatic recovery: run formula rewrite/simplification (Phase 12) first; enable on-the-fly product (Phase 12) so unreachable product states are never materialized — often avoids the cap.
  • Manual recovery: simplify formula; raise --max-states; emit the Ngba via to-buchi (HOA/dot) to inspect structure.
  • Operator actions: prefer on-the-fly nested-DFS product; check whether nnf+simplify shrank closure.
  • Forensic artifacts: phase (ngba/degen/product), state count at abort, |closure(f)|, k (accept-set count), |Kripke|.
  • Long-term mitigation: state caching/merging (Phase 7/12); benchmark automaton size vs Spot targets (Phase 13); document scaling table.

S2 — Monitor formula blowup

  • Symptoms: Monitor::step latency grows per event; progressed Formula size (post-progress) unbounded; eventual cap hit MonitorFormulaTooLarge{nodes}.
  • Root cause: progress(f U g, s) = progress(g,s) OR (progress(f,s) AND (f U g)) re-embeds f U g each step; without simplification the residual grows linearly/exponentially in events.
  • Detection: node-count cap per step; degenerate-growth assertion (residual not shrinking under simplify).
  • Automatic recovery: apply NNF + simplification rewrite after every progress (idempotent dedup of And/Or); switch to synthesized monitor-automaton (Phase 9) — bounded state, O(1)/event.
  • Manual recovery: monitor with --automaton mode; bound formula complexity.
  • Operator actions: prefer monitor-automaton synthesis for long/infinite streams; progression mode only for short traces.
  • Forensic artifacts: residual Formula node count per step, event index, original formula.
  • Long-term mitigation: canonicalize residuals (sorted, deduped subformulas); cache progression results; equivalence-merge residual states.

S1 — Lasso fails to re-simulate (extraction bug)

  • Symptoms: emptiness returns Some(Lasso{stem, cycle}) but re-running the trace stem · cycle^ω against the original Formula does NOT exhibit the claimed violation (the lasso is not a genuine accepting run of product(Nba(¬φ), K)).
  • Root cause: nested-DFS path reconstruction off-by-one (cycle does not close on the seed accepting state); stem/cycle boundary wrong; product-state→Kripke-state projection mislabels.
  • Detection: mandatory re-simulation gate — every Lasso is replayed as a Trace{prefix=stem++cycle, loop_start=Some(stem.len())} through eval_ltlf-style / monitor progression of ¬φ; if it does NOT accept, FAIL the query rather than emit a bad counterexample.
  • Automatic recovery: on re-sim failure, re-run emptiness under a verified-reference inner-DFS path tracker; if still failing, escalate to S0 (verdict suspect).
  • Manual recovery: dump product automaton (HOA/dot); manually trace the accepting cycle.
  • Operator actions: never trust a non-re-simulating lasso; treat as bug, file with artifacts.
  • Forensic artifacts: Lasso{stem, cycle}, projected Kripke state sequence, the ¬φ progression trace, the seed accepting state.
  • Long-term mitigation: re-simulation is a permanent post-condition of check_ltl; property test "every emitted lasso re-simulates" (Phase 13).

S0 — Wrong verdict (GRAVEST)

  • Symptoms: check_ltl returns Ok(()) when a counterexample exists (false "holds"), or Err(lasso) when K ⊨ Aφ (false violation); or CtlChecker::sat mis-labels states; or eval_ltlf flips a finite-trace bool. No crash, no error — a silent lie.
  • Root cause (per stage): NNF dualities wrong (not(p U q)=(not p) R (not q) etc.); GPVW obligation split ("now" guard vs "next") wrong; generalized acceptance set per-U missing/extra (eventuality not enforced → spurious empty); degeneralization counter advances incorrectly (visits single accept set without covering all k); nested DFS misses a reachable accepting cycle (color/seed bug) or invents one; CTL EG SCC computation or E[pUq] lfp/EX preimage wrong; progress recurrence or weak/strong-next boundary wrong.
  • Detection: differential testing vs Spot (Phase 13) — compare ltl_to_ngba→degeneralize automaton acceptance and check_ltl verdicts against ltl2tgba on standard LTL patterns + random formulas; lasso re-simulation (S1 gate) cross-checks any reported violation; CTL labeling vs known-result fixtures (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla); LTLf vs De Giacomo-Vardi semantics.
  • Automatic recovery: NONE possible at query time (the answer is already wrong and unflagged) — which is exactly why the offline differential + re-sim gates exist. CI BLOCKS release on any divergence.
  • Manual recovery: shrink the diverging formula (delta-debug) to minimal witness; bisect across NNF / GPVW / degeneralize / nested-DFS stages by dumping intermediate automata (HOA) and diffing vs Spot at each stage; re-simulate Spot's counterexample and ours.
  • Operator actions: treat ANY Spot divergence or non-re-simulating lasso as a release-blocking S0; never ship.
  • Forensic artifacts: minimal divergent Formula, our Ngba/Nba (HOA) vs Spot's, both verdicts, both lassos, the re-simulation trace, the failing stage.
  • Long-term mitigation: differential vs Spot is the project "done" gate (Phase 13); stage-by-stage automaton diffing; expand pattern + random-formula corpus; assertion: emptiness result is consistent with at least one re-simulated witness/proof of emptiness.
  • Real-world analogue — Spot TGBA emptiness bug (2019). Spot issue tracker, reported by Maximilien Colange (ltlcross run on random formulas). A nondeterminism in Spot's on-the-fly SCC computation caused autfilt to report a non-empty automaton as empty on a specific TGBA shape when compiled with aggressive optimization flags. The bug was silent: no crash, no error code — ltlcross surfaced it via acceptance disagreement between runs. Resolution: deterministic edge-traversal order enforced in the inner SCC loop. Lesson for this engine: the differential harness must run to-buchi + Spot cross-check as a mandatory CI gate — not an optional check — because optimizer-induced nondeterminism can produce verdict divergence that survives unit testing.
  • Real-world analogue — SPIN nested-DFS coloring bug (pre-3.4). Reported in the SPIN verification workshop proceedings (Holzmann, 1997 SPIN workshop notes). An early SPIN version used a single visited-set for both the outer and inner DFS of the nested-DFS emptiness check, causing the inner DFS to terminate prematurely at outer-visited states and miss reachable accepting cycles. The result: SPIN reported "no errors found" on a Promela model that demonstrably violated its LTL property. The fix (distinct color sets, post-order launch) became the canonical algorithm. Lesson for this engine: Phase 8's two-color invariant and post-order inner-DFS launch are not implementation details — they are the correctness invariant. emptiness must be audited against P3/CVWY exactly, and the Phase 13 differential harness is the backstop.

S0 — Nondeterministic verdict (determinism bug)

  • Symptoms: identical formula + Kripke/trace yields different holds/Lasso/RvVerdict across runs.
  • Root cause: iteration over HashMap/HashSet of automaton states in unspecified order leaking into DFS visit order and thus which cycle is found; uninitialized arena reuse; data race (must be none — single-threaded core).
  • Detection: determinism harness runs each fixture N=100× and asserts identical output; CI fails on variance.
  • Automatic recovery: none at runtime (already nondeterministic); fix is deterministic ordering.
  • Manual recovery: replace hash containers with insertion-ordered/BTreeMap or sorted index vectors on all state-enumeration paths (ltl_to_ngba, product, emptiness).
  • Operator actions: pin seeds; report the two divergent runs with full state dumps.
  • Forensic artifacts: two run transcripts, container iteration trace, DFS color/seed log.
  • Long-term mitigation: ban nondeterministic iteration order from verdict-affecting paths; determinism test permanent in Phase 13.

S0 — LTL3 verdict instability

  • Symptoms: Monitor::step emits a DEFINITIVE RvVerdict::True/False at event i, then a later step contradicts it (changes the definitive verdict). Definitive verdicts MUST be irrevocable.
  • Root cause: progression incorrectly concludes a residual is True/False (good/bad prefix decided too early); monitorability misjudged; weak/strong-next mishandled so a non-good prefix is called good.
  • Detection: monotonicity assertion — once step returns True/False, all subsequent steps on any extension MUST return the same (else BUG); differential vs LTL3 reference (Bauer-Leucker-Schallhart) on prefix corpus.
  • Automatic recovery: none — instability means an earlier alarm was unsound; only a fix resolves it.
  • Manual recovery: verify good-/bad-prefix detection: a residual is True only if EVERY infinite extension satisfies φ, False only if NONE does, else Unknown/Presumably* (RV-LTL).
  • Operator actions: do not act on a definitive verdict from an unverified monitor; report the prefix where it flipped.
  • Forensic artifacts: event-indexed residual Formula sequence, the verdict-emission log, the contradicting extension.
  • Long-term mitigation: irrevocability property test (definitive ⇒ stable under all extensions); monitorability classification validated against monitor-automaton reachability (Phase 9/13).

S2 — Spot differential-oracle unavailable/version-mismatched

  • Symptoms: the Phase 13 differential CI job fails to start or exits nonzero because ltl2tgba/autfilt is missing, uninstallable, or reports a version other than the pinned one (ADR-21/Dockerfile.validate).
  • Root cause: base-image drift, an unpinned apt/tarball install, or a Spot release that changes CLI flags/HOA quirks the harness depends on.
  • Detection: xtests checks spot --version against the pinned string at job start; mismatch or absent binary fails the job immediately, before any formula is compared.
  • Automatic recovery: none — a missing oracle can never be silently treated as "all differential checks passed"; the gate fails closed, not open.
  • Manual recovery: rebuild Dockerfile.validate pinning the documented Spot version/digest; re-run the job.
  • Operator actions: verify the pinned version in Dockerfile.validate against the CI log's reported spot --version.
  • Forensic artifacts: CI job log, reported vs pinned Spot version string, Docker build log.
  • Long-term mitigation: pin Spot by digest (not tag) in Dockerfile.validate; alert on base-image rebuild drift; treat oracle absence as equivalent-severity to a differential mismatch for release-gating purposes.

S3 — Malformed Kripke/Trace input (validation rejection)

  • Symptoms: KripkeStructure::validate() / Trace::validate() returns Err (DeadState, LoopStartOutOfRange, EdgeTarget out of range, InitOutOfRange) before any evaluator runs.
  • Root cause: a hand-authored or generated model violates the Phase 3 well-formedness contract — non-total transition relation (INV-15) or an out-of-range lasso loop_start.
  • Detection: validate() is mandatory at every tl-cli entrypoint (Phase 3 Operational Notes) and asserted again at each evaluator boundary before check_ltl/eval_ltlf/CtlChecker::sat runs.
  • Automatic recovery: reject with the typed KripkeError/TraceError naming the offending state/index; never auto-repair (e.g. silently adding a self-loop) since that would silently change the model's semantics.
  • Manual recovery: fix the input file per the Phase 3 trace/Kripke text-format spec.
  • Operator actions: consult Phase 3 §3.10 Operational Notes for the format grammar; re-run tl check --stats to confirm state/edge counts.
  • Forensic artifacts: KripkeError/TraceError variant, offending state/edge/index, the input file.
  • Long-term mitigation: malformed-input fixture corpus (Phase 3 §3.5: bad_lasso_oob, bad_dead_state, bad_init_oob, bad_edge_oob); fuzz the loader alongside the parser (Phase 13).

Cross-cutting recovery doctrine

  • Soundness > liveness > performance: prefer to ABORT (S2/S3) over emitting a possibly-wrong answer (S0/S1).
  • Two independent oracles guard every verdict: differential-vs-Spot (offline, CI gate) + lasso re-simulation (online, per-query post-condition).
  • No silent panic!/unwrap on user input: all input failures are Result-typed (ParseError, well-formedness, size caps).
  • Every S0/S1 finding is release-blocking and reduced to a minimal Formula witness for regression.
  • Forensic bundle for any escalation: minimal formula, NNF, Ngba+Nba (HOA), product, lasso, re-simulation trace, Spot reference output, failing stage.

Operational Readiness

Framing: tl is an evaluator tool + library, not a long-lived service. "Operations" = batch model-checking jobs, long-running stream monitors, CI differential-testing runs, and developer-facing observability of the pipeline Formula -> NNF -> Ngba -> Nba -> product -> emptiness/verdict. No request/RTT, no replicas, no on-call rotation; the "incidents" are wrong verdicts, state-space blow-ups, and monitor stalls. Library surface stays silent (returns values, never prints, never panics on bad input); CLI owns all I/O, logging, and progress.

27.1 Observability principles

Principle Rule Rationale
Library = pure tl-formula/tl-automata/tl-eval/tl-monitor emit no logs, no stdout; return Result/verdicts embeddable; caller owns telemetry policy
Tracing opt-in tracing spans behind feature trace; off => zero overhead (compiled out) no cost on the hot nested-DFS path
CLI = chatty tl logs progress + per-stage timings to stderr; results to stdout scripts pipe stdout; humans read stderr
Determinism first every metric reproducible given (formula, model/trace, seed, caps) bug triage needs replay, not sampling
Quantities, not adjectives report state counts/visited/time, never "large"/"slow" thresholds + regression gates need numbers
Verdict + witness a verdict alone is unactionable; always carriable Lasso/automaton dump counterexample is the artifact engineers debug

27.2 Metrics to expose (per invocation)

Emitted as one structured record (JSON line on --metrics); names LOCKED to pipeline stages.

Metric Type Source stage Target / cap
formulas_parsed counter tl-syntax Parser batch totals
parse_errors counter ParseError/ParseErrorKind 0 in CI corpus
formula_size_pre_nnf / _post_nnf gauge tl-formula NNF ≤ ~2x pre-size
closure_size gauge closure(f) = #subformulas; drives Ngba bound
ngba_states / ngba_accept_sets gauge ltl_to_ngba (GPVW) ≤ 2^closure_size (worst); 1 accept set per Until
nba_states gauge degeneralize ≈ ngba_states × accept_sets (counter)
nba_states_after_simplify gauge Phase 7 prune/merge < nba_states (reduction %)
product_states / product_states_explored gauge / counter product (eager or on-the-fly) explored ≤ ngba×
ndfs_visited_outer / ndfs_visited_inner counter emptiness nested DFS ≤ product_states each
verdict enum holds/check_ltl/eval_ltlf {holds, counterexample} / bool
lasso_stem_len / lasso_cycle_len gauge Lasso minimal-ish; cycle ≥ 1
ctl_sat_iterations counter CtlChecker lfp/gfp ≤
peak_states gauge (high-water) arena alloc across stages the OOM predictor
peak_rss_bytes gauge allocator hook matched to --mem-cap
stage_time_us{stage} histogram per stage see SLO §27.5
Monitor only: monitor_events_total counter Monitor::step stream length
monitor_events_per_sec gauge rolling ≥ 10^6 ev/s simple φ
monitor_current_verdict enum (RvVerdict) latest step sticky once definitive
monitor_progress_formula_size gauge progressed Formula bounded => monitorable; growth => leak/blow-up
monitor_step_latency_us histogram per step p99 see §27.5

Stage label set (LOCKED): parse | nnf | closure | ngba | degeneralize | simplify | product | emptiness | ctl_label | ltlf_progress | past_dp | monitor_step.

27.3 Structured tracing

tracing span tree mirrors the canonical architecture diagram; one span per stage, fields = the §27.2 metrics for that stage. Off unless --trace / feature trace.

span check_ltl{ formula, kind=Ltl }
 ├─ nnf{ size_pre, size_post }
 ├─ ltl_to_ngba{ closure_size, ngba_states, accept_sets, ms }      # negated φ
 ├─ degeneralize{ nba_states, ms }
 ├─ simplify{ nba_states_after, removed_unreachable, merged, ms }
 ├─ product{ product_states_explored, on_the_fly=true, ms }
 └─ emptiness{ visited_outer, visited_inner, result, ms }
       └─ event lasso{ stem_len, cycle_len }                       # only if Err(Lasso)
  • CTL path: holds -> spans nnf, ctl_label{ op, sat_iterations, ms } (one per EX/EU/EG subgoal).
  • LTLf path: eval_ltlf -> ltlf_progress{ trace_len, residual_size_max, ms }.
  • Monitor: long-lived root span monitor{ formula }; step is an event (not a span) to avoid per-event span cost — emit one summary event per N=10^4 steps with events_per_sec, current_verdict, progress_formula_size.
  • Span IDs let a single blow-up be traced end-to-end: closure_size=40 -> ngba_states=10^6 -> product abort.

27.4 Logging philosophy

Layer Emits Channel Levels
tl-* libs nothing (values only); Result/Option<Lasso>/RvVerdict — —
tl-cli progress "parsed N formulas", "ngba: 2.1k states", per-stage ms stderr INFO
tl-cli warnings nearing caps (≥80%), formula not monitorable, degeneralized blow-up stderr WARN
tl-cli errors parse error w/ span, cap exceeded, timeout stderr ERROR
Results verdict / holds / counterexample / automaton (HOA, dot) stdout —
Dumps `--dump-automaton hoa dot, --dump-lasso, --dump-nnf` stdout / --out FILE

Rules: results never mixed into log stream (stdout vs stderr); --quiet silences INFO/WARN, keeps results + ERROR; --metrics json adds the §27.2 record to stderr; secrets none (no network, no creds). Counterexample Lasso printed as readable stem; (cycle)^ω with the AP valuation per state.

27.5 SLOs

Two SLO classes: correctness (hard, gates release) and performance (soft, regression-gated). Hardware baseline: 1 core, 2020-class x86-64; formulas from the standard LTL pattern set (Dwyer); Kripke up to 10^6 states.

SLO Target Window Action on breach
Verdict correctness vs Spot (ltl2tgba) 100% agreement on language-emptiness / model-check verdict every CI run, full corpus block release (gravest class §27.7)
CTL labeling vs known results 100% on golden CTL fixtures every CI run block release
Parser: no panic, no false-accept 0 on fuzz corpus + grammar suite nightly fuzz block release
Automaton size: pattern φ (≤ ~10 ops) ngba_states ≤ Spot size × 1.5 per benchmark WARN; investigate if 5+ formulas regress
ltl_to_ngba time, φ ≤ 15 p95 < 50 ms
Full check_ltl, K = 10^5 p95 < 2 s
Monitor per-event latency, simple φ p99 < 1 µs; ≥ 10^6 ev/s continuous-stream bench perf-regression issue
Monitor progressed-formula size bounded (no monotone growth) over 10^7 events soak bug: progression not simplifying => leak
eval_ltlf, trace len 10^6 p95 < 100 ms benchmark perf-regression issue
Peak memory ≤ --mem-cap (default 1 GiB) enforced abort w/ state-count trace

Correctness SLOs have no error budget (any disagreement = incident). Performance SLOs allow a small regression budget (e.g. ≤5% on a single benchmark) before failing the gate.

27.6 Dashboards

Not a live service dashboard — two offline/operational views built from the §27.2 metric records.

  • Batch model-check view (CI / regression corpus): table per formula { id, kind, closure_size, ngba_states, nba_states, product_states_explored, ndfs_visited, verdict, stage_time_us, peak_states }; sorted by peak_states desc to surface blow-up candidates; diff column = vs previous run + vs Spot automaton size. Aggregates: total formulas, % agreement, p50/p95/max per stage.
  • Long-running monitor view: live tail of { events_total, events_per_sec, current_verdict (RvVerdict), progress_formula_size }; sparkline of events_per_sec; alert line if progress_formula_size trends up (monitorability/leak) or events_per_sec drops below floor; "verdict became definitive at event K" marker => monitor may detach.
  • Emitted as JSONL -> consumable by any plotter; no bespoke UI required (CLI prints a summary table on --report).

27.7 Incident response + runbooks

Incident = a wrong verdict, a state-space blow-up, a monitor stall, or a cap/timeout abort. Each has a deterministic, replayable runbook (re-run with fixed seed + caps).

Symptom Likely cause Runbook
Verdict disagrees with Spot (gravest) GPVW node/guard bug; degeneralize counter bug; nested-DFS coloring bug flips emptiness 1) --dump-automaton hoa for Nba(¬φ), diff vs ltl2tgba HOA; 2) --dump-nnf confirm NNF correct; 3) minimize φ to smallest disagreeing subformula (delta-debug); 4) check emptiness lasso is a real accepting cycle (replay run, verify accept-set visited in cycle); 5) add the shrunk φ to the differential corpus as a permanent fixture
Automaton explodes (ngba_states/product_states >> expected) nested U/X, redundant subformulas, simplification off 1) inspect stage trace closure_size -> ngba_states; 2) enable Phase 12 rewrite/simplify + state caching; 3) prefer on-the-fly product (lazy build in nested DFS) so unreachable product states never materialize; 4) raise --state-cap only if growth is justified, else treat as bug
OOM / --mem-cap hit product or Ngba materialized eagerly; pathological φ abort emits peak_states + per-stage state counts; switch to on-the-fly product; raise --mem-cap; if still blows up, escalate as blow-up bug
--timeout exceeded huge Kripke; expensive EG SCC pass; degeneralization explosion report last completed stage + partial state counts; raise timeout or reduce model; profile the named slow stage
Monitor stalls / latency p99 climbs progressed Formula growing (progression not simplifying); event parse hot check monitor_progress_formula_size trend; ensure progress result is simplified each step; switch to precompiled monitor-automaton synthesis (Phase 9) for O(1)/event
Monitor never reaches definitive verdict φ not monitorable (no good/bad prefix reachable) run monitorability classification; if non-monitorable, RvVerdict stays Unknown/Presumably* by design — report to user, not a bug
Parser panic / hang grammar/precedence edge, deep nesting reproduce from fuzz seed; ParseError must be returned not panicked; add seed to fuzz corpus
Counterexample doesn't replay Lasso stem/cycle indices wrong validate stem ends where cycle starts; re-simulate trace on K, confirm ¬φ holds along the ω-word

Postmortem rule: every correctness incident MUST end with a new minimized fixture committed to the Phase 13 differential/golden corpus, so the same flip can never silently recur.

27.8 Deployment, versioning, configuration

  • Distribution: seven tl-* crates published to a registry + the tl binary; consumers depend on tl-formula/tl-eval/etc. directly. Semver strict: Formula enum variants, Ngba/Nba/Lasso/Verdict/RvVerdict shapes, and public fn signatures (ltl_to_ngba, degeneralize, product, emptiness, check_ltl, eval_ltlf, progress, step) are the API contract — any change is a major bump. MSRV 1.78 bump = minor.
  • Feature flags: trace (tracing spans), serde (HOA/JSON IO), hoa/dot (dumps), std (CLI only; libs no_std + alloc). Default features minimal so embedded/no_std users pay nothing.
  • Configuration knobs (CLI flags + lib builder options): --state-cap (max automaton/product states before abort; default e.g. 5×10^6), --mem-cap (default 1 GiB), --timeout (per invocation), --on-the-fly (lazy product, default on), --simplify (rewrite level), --seed (random-formula differential runs), --dump-{automaton,lasso,nnf}, --metrics, --report, --quiet, --trace. Library mirrors caps as explicit params (no env magic) so embedders get deterministic limits.
  • Caps are guard rails, not correctness: hitting a cap aborts with a diagnostic (state-count trace), never returns a wrong/partial verdict. A capped run is Inconclusive, distinct from holds/counterexample.
  • Release gate = §27.5 correctness SLOs green (Spot differential 100%, CTL golden 100%, fuzz clean) — this is the same "done" gate as Phase 13; ops readiness and validation share one bar.

Compatibility Guarantees

Scope: stability contract for the seven tl-* crates, the tl binary, the formula input syntax, and the automaton/verdict outputs. Governs what consumers may rely on across releases and what may change silently.

28.1 Versioning model

  • SemVer 2.0.0 per crate; workspace ships a single synchronized minor (tl-syntax..tl-cli advance together) — divergent patch levels allowed, divergent minor/major not.
  • Pre-1.0 (0.y.z): 0.y is the breaking axis, 0.y.z patch is additive/fix-only. Stability promises below apply at >= 1.0; before that they are intent, enforced best-effort.
  • A workspace VERSION constant (tl_formula::VERSION) is the canonical release; tl --version prints it plus the pinned Spot parity version (28.8) and the syntax-grammar version (28.3).

28.2 Primary compatibility surfaces (ranked)

Surface Crate Stability tier Breakage = major bump of
Formula enum + variants tl-formula frozen public tl-formula + all dependents
Ap, Kind, nnf, closure tl-formula stable tl-formula
Nba, Ngba, Guard, Lasso field layout tl-automata stable tl-automata, tl-eval, tl-monitor
ltl_to_ngba/degeneralize/product/emptiness sigs tl-automata stable tl-automata + dependents
State/Trace/KripkeStructure fields tl-trace stable tl-trace + dependents
CtlChecker, eval_ltlf, check_ltl sigs tl-eval stable tl-eval
Verdict / RvVerdict taxonomy tl-monitor frozen public (28.6) tl-monitor
Monitor::step/progress sigs tl-monitor stable tl-monitor
tl CLI grammar (subcommands/flags/exit codes) tl-cli stable (28.5) tl-cli (major)
Formula input syntax tl-syntax stable (28.3) independent grammar version
HOA/dot output tl-automata/tl-cli interop-stable (28.4) none (additive only)

The Formula enum and RvVerdict are the two exhaustively matched types most consumers match on → adding a variant is breaking for them (28.7). All others are field/signature surfaces.

28.3 Formula input-syntax versioning

  • Grammar pinned by a SYNTAX_VERSION (MAJOR.MINOR), reported by tl --version and accepted by eval/check/monitor via --syntax=<ver> (default = newest).
  • MINOR: purely additive tokens/aliases (e.g. new S/H spellings, ASCII↔Unicode operator synonyms) — old formulas still parse identically.
  • MAJOR: any change that alters the parse tree of a previously valid formula (precedence, associativity, operator meaning) — gated behind an explicit --syntax opt-in; the prior MAJOR remains selectable for ≥ 2 releases.
  • Operator spellings frozen at 1.0: X F G U R (future), Y O H S (past), EX AX EG AG EF AF E[_U_] A[_U_] (CTL). New synonyms may be added (deprecation never removes the canonical spelling).
  • Deprecation policy: a token is marked deprecated → still parses + emits a ParseError-adjacent warning (not a hard error) for ≥ 2 minor releases → removable only at the next syntax MAJOR. ParseErrorKind variants are additive; existing kinds are never repurposed.
  • Pretty-printer (tl-syntax) output is not byte-stable across minors (formatting may improve); it is semantically stable — round-trip parse(print(f)) == f (modulo NNF/simplify) is an invariant tested in Phase 13.

28.4 HOA / dot automaton output stability + interop

  • to-buchi emits HOA v1 (Hanoi Omega-Automata) and Graphviz dot. HOA is the interop contract; dot is rendering-only (layout/cosmetics may change freely, never a breaking change).
  • HOA guarantees: valid against the HOA v1 spec consumed by Spot's autfilter/ltl2tgba; --ABORT never emitted on success; AP order = interning order from tl-syntax and stable for a fixed input.
  • HOA semantic stability (language of the automaton) is an invariant; HOA structural stability (state count, numbering, transition split) is not guaranteed — Phase 7 simplification / Phase 12 caching may shrink or renumber states between minors. Consumers must treat HOA structurally as opaque and compare by language (Spot autfilter --are-isomorphic/--equivalent-to), never by literal diff.
  • Lasso { stem, cycle } is a witness, not canonical: any valid accepting lasso is acceptable; the specific stem/cycle may change between releases. Consumers assert "is a counterexample," never "is the counterexample."
  • Bumping the emitted HOA version (v1 → future) is additive: old --hoa-version=1 selectable for ≥ 2 releases.

28.5 CLI grammar contract (tl)

  • Stable: subcommand names eval/to-buchi/check/monitor, their positional argument order, long-flag names, and exit codes. New subcommands/flags = minor; renaming/removing/reordering = major.
  • Exit-code map (frozen):
Code Meaning
0 check/eval holds = formula satisfied / verdict True; to-buchi ok
1 check counterexample found (Lasso printed); eval verdict False
2 monitor ended Unknown / Presumably (non-definitive RV verdict)
64 usage / CLI-grammar error
65 ParseError (bad formula syntax)
70 internal error
  • Machine output: --json schemas (verdict, lasso, automaton metadata) are additive-only (new fields appended; existing keys/types never change/remove). Human (non---json) text output is not stability-guaranteed.

28.6 Verdict / RvVerdict taxonomy stability

  • Verdict { True, False } (2-valued definitive) and RvVerdict { True, False, PresumablyTrue, PresumablyFalse, Unknown } are closed taxonomies — frozen at 1.0; adding a variant is a major bump (consumers exhaustively match).
  • Semantics pinned: True/False = good-prefix/bad-prefix (LTL3 definitive, irrevocable); PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse = RV-LTL finite-semantics tie-break; Unknown = LTL3 inconclusive. A definitive True/False from Monitor::step is monotone — never reverts on later events (invariant, Phase 9).
  • RvVerdict is intentionally a superset of LTL3 ({True,False,Unknown}); LTL3-only consumers must treat the two Presumably-* variants and Unknown together as "inconclusive."

28.7 Adding new operators without breaking consumers

  • Risk: the Formula enum is #[non_exhaustive] from 1.0 → adding a temporal operator (e.g. MTL U[a,b], TCTL — stretch, see NON-GOALS) is a minor (compile-still-works) but any match Formula without a wildcard arm breaks. #[non_exhaustive] forces downstream wildcard arms, making variant addition non-breaking by contract.
  • Past-time operators (Y O H S) are already in the enum at 1.0 → not "new"; their evaluator (tl-eval DP, Phase 10) and progression coverage are additive features, not enum changes.
  • New operators ship with: NNF dualities (nnf), closure entries (closure), parser spellings (28.3 minor), and either a ltl_to_ngba/labeling rule or an explicit ParseError/Kind-mismatch if unsupported on a given path. An operator unsupported by a given evaluator returns a typed error, never a silent wrong verdict.
  • MTL / TCTL / timed-trace support, if ever added, lands as a new sibling crate (tl-timed) + new Kind variants — never by repurposing existing Formula variants → existing LTL/CTL consumers are untouched.

28.8 Differential-parity guarantee vs pinned Spot

  • A pinned Spot version (recorded in Cargo.toml/CI as SPOT_VERSION, surfaced by tl --version) defines the parity oracle (Phase 13). Guarantee: for every formula in the standard LTL pattern set + random corpus, ltl_to_ngba→degeneralize produces an Nba language-equivalent to Spot ltl2tgba (checked --equivalent-to), and check_ltl verdicts match Spot model-checking verdicts.
  • Parity is on language / verdict, not automaton size or state numbering — tl may legitimately produce larger or differently-numbered automata than Spot.
  • Bumping SPOT_VERSION is a test-infrastructure change (not an API break); a parity regression introduced by a Spot bump blocks release until re-baselined. A parity divergence in tl is a release-blocking defect (the gravest failure class: silent verdict flip).
  • No guarantee of parity against unpinned/newer Spot; consumers needing a specific oracle pin the matching tl release.

28.9 MSRV policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78 (edition 2021), stated in every crate's rust-version. Crates are no_std + alloc except tl-cli (std); this no_std boundary is itself a stability surface — moving a library crate to std is a major bump.
  • MSRV raise = minor bump, allowed only on a 6-month-minimum cadence, never in a patch; documented in the changelog with rationale. CI runs the MSRV toolchain on every PR to prevent accidental raises.
  • No reliance on nightly features; no build.rs codegen affecting the public API.

28.10 Out-of-contract (explicitly NOT guaranteed)

  • Internal arena layout / state numbering of Ngba/Nba; SCC iteration order; nested-DFS visit order; the specific Lasso returned; on-the-fly product materialization order (Phase 12).
  • Pretty-printer byte output; human-readable CLI text; benchmark numbers; Guard internal DNF/bitset encoding (only its boolean semantics are stable).
  • Anything behind a #[doc(hidden)] item or a cfg(feature = "unstable") gate. Depending on these voids all guarantees above.

Testing Strategy

Test pyramid (by count): unit ~70% / property ~18% / differential ~7% / integration ~4% / fuzz (continuous). Verdict soundness (§7 INV-1: check_ltl/CtlChecker::holds/eval_ltlf never flip truth) is the apex invariant — every class below ladders up to it. A green suite that does not catch a verdict flip is worthless; the differential + property tiers exist precisely because the GRAVEST failure class is silent and crash-free.

29.1 Unit tests (per-crate, white-box)

One test module per source unit; assert smallest observable contract. Targets:

Crate Unit under test Cases (representative) Asserts
tl-syntax Lexer/Token each token kind; whitespace; comments; bad char exact Token stream; ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedChar w/ span
tl-syntax Parser (per production) one test per grammar rule: atom, !, &/|, X/F/G, U/R, EX..A[U], parens exact Formula tree; precedence (a U b U c right-assoc; X a U b = (X a) U b)
tl-syntax Parser (errors) unbalanced paren, dangling U, EX w/ no arg, empty input ParseError variant + byte span; no panic
tl-formula nnf !X, !(p U q), !F, !G, !EX, !Eu, double-neg, !!p=p negation only on Atom/True/False; idempotent nnf(nnf f)==nnf f
tl-formula closure nested U inside G; CTL fused ops set membership = each subformula once; size ≤ |f|
tl-formula Kind pure LTL; CTL; mixed path/state misuse; past op correct Kind::{Ltl,Ctl,PastLtl,Ltlf}; reject CTL path-formula outside quantifier
tl-trace Trace/State validation empty prefix; loop_start in/out of range; well-formed lasso reject loop_start ≥ prefix.len(); bitset round-trip
tl-trace KripkeStructure validation total vs partial transition relation; empty init; dangling succ idx reject non-total relation; reject out-of-bounds successor
tl-eval CtlChecker::sat per op EX (preimage), Eu (lfp), Eg (gfp/SCC), derived EF/AG/AF/AX/Au per-state Vec<bool> vs hand-computed labeling on a fixed 4–6 state Kripke
tl-eval eval_ltlf / Monitor::progress progress(X f)=f; progress(f U g); progress(G f); atom by State structural equality of residual Formula (post-simplify)
tl-automata ltl_to_ngba (GPVW node) single node split now/next; eventuality → accept set; guard from "now" set node count; one accept set per U-subformula; guard mask+care correct
tl-automata degeneralize Ngba with k=0,1,3 accept sets Nba.nstates == ngba.nstates * max(k,1); counter cycles 0..k
tl-automata product Nba × tiny Kripke edge = guard∧label agreement; init = init×init
tl-automata emptiness (nested DFS) accepting cycle present / absent / self-loop / cycle off the stem Some(Lasso) ⇔ reachable accepting cycle; None ⇔ empty
tl-monitor Monitor::step good prefix→True; bad prefix→False; pending→Unknown; RV tie-break exact RvVerdict; monotone (no True→Unknown regress)

Fixtures: tl-formula/tests/fixtures/*.ltl (golden NNF), tl-trace/tests/fixtures/*.kripke. Each sat/progress case carries the by-hand expected value inline.

29.2 Integration tests (end-to-end, black-box per pipeline)

Whole-path, formula-text → verdict, exercising the four architecture lanes. Live in tl-cli/tests/ + per-evaluator e2e modules.

Lane Path Fixture → expected
LTLf/trace text → Parser → nnf → eval_ltlf(f, Trace) G p on lasso where p always holds → true; F q on finite prefix w/ q → true; p U q no q, no loop → false
CTL/Kripke text → CtlChecker::holds(f, K) AG EF reset on mutex Kripke → known holds/fails
LTL/Kripke text → check_ltl(f, K) → Result<(), Lasso> violated G(req→F ack) → Err(Lasso); satisfied → Ok(())
Monitor/stream text → Monitor::step over event seq G p then p-false event → False at that step

Cross-lane consistency oracle (INV-1 reinforcement): for any LTL f and lasso Trace t derived from a single-path Kripke K, assert eval_lasso(f,t) == (check_ltl(f,K).is_ok()) and == (Monitor fold reaching definitive verdict). Three independent engines must agree on the same word.

CLI golden tests: tl to-buchi "F p" --hoa byte-diff vs golden HOA; tl check f.ltl k.json exit code + counterexample lasso pretty-print stable; tl eval / tl monitor stdout golden.

29.3 Differential testing vs Spot (primary verdict-soundness guard)

Oracle = Spot ltl2tgba. Gated behind --features spot-diff (needs Spot installed); CI job diff-spot runs it. This is the load-bearing defense against the GRAVEST failure class (a GPVW/degeneralize/nested-DFS bug silently flipping a verdict).

Two oracles:

  1. Automaton language equivalence. For formula f: build Nba ours = degeneralize(ltl_to_ngba(f)); build Spot's Nba. Equivalence by cross-product emptiness in both directions: L(A_ours)=L(A_spot) ⇔ emptiness(product(A_ours, complement(A_spot)))==None ∧ symmetric. (Complement via Spot to avoid trusting our own complementation.) Mismatch → log f, both automata (HOA), the distinguishing lasso → KNOWN_FAIL ledger.
  2. Model-checking verdict. For (f, K): compare check_ltl(f,K).is_ok() against Spot product-emptiness on the same K. Disagreement is a P0 verdict flip.

Formula sources:

  • LTL pattern formulas — Dwyer/SPEC absence/existence/universality/response/precedence/chain patterns; the standard ltl2tgba bench set; common idioms G(p→F q), G F p, F G p, p U (q U r), G(p→(q U r)). ~120 curated.
  • Random formulas — randltl-style generator (or Spot randltl): vary AP count 1–5, size 1–30, operator weights, seed-logged for replay. ≥10⁴ per CI run; nightly ≥10⁶.

CTL has no Spot path-equivalent; CTL CtlChecker labeling is differentially checked vs hand-computed known results on a fixed catalogue (mutex, traffic-light, dining-philosophers-2) plus an independent reference labeler (naive bounded unrolling for finite Kripke) cross-checked per state.

29.4 Property-based testing (proptest/quickcheck)

Generators: arb_formula(kind, max_size, naps); arb_trace; arb_kripke(nstates, density). Shrinking on Formula (replace node by subtree / True/False), Trace, Kripke. Each property names the §7 invariant it pins.

Property Statement Invariant
NNF language-preserving eval_lasso(f,t) == eval_lasso(nnf(f),t) ∀ lasso t INV (NNF eq)
NNF shape nnf(f) has negation only on atoms; nnf∘nnf == nnf INV (NNF norm)
NNF size bound |nnf(f)| ≤ c·|f| (no blowup) perf inv
Product = intersection L(product(A,B)) == L(A)∩L(B) checked on sampled lassos INV (product)
Degeneralize preserves L L(degeneralize(g)) == L_gen(g) via shared lasso oracle INV (degen)
Progression ≡ LTLf semantics eval_ltlf(f,t) == fold of progress to True/False residual INV-1
Lasso re-simulation emptiness(A)=Some(L) ⇒ replaying L.stem+L.cycle^ω is an accepting run of A INV (CE valid)
CE soundness check_ltl(f,K)=Err(L) ⇒ the word read off L violates f (re-evaluated independently) INV-1
Holds/empty duality check_ltl(f,K).is_ok() ⇔ emptiness(product(degeneralize(ltl_to_ngba(nnf(!f))),K))==None INV-1
CTL gfp/lfp monotone sat(Eu)/sat(Eg) fixpoint iteration monotone, terminates ≤ |states| steps INV (fixpoint)
Monitor monotonicity verdict sequence never regresses from a definitive True/False INV (RV monotone)
Determinism repeat run of every API on same input → identical output (no HashMap iter-order leak) INV (determinism)

Lasso-equality oracle (eval_lasso) is the shared semantic ground truth backing the automaton/product/degen properties; it is itself unit-tested against hand-traced words so the properties do not rest on an untested oracle.

29.5 Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer, continuous)

Target Harness Oracle / what it catches
fuzz_parse bytes → Parser no panic / no UB; Ok ⇒ pretty-print round-trips reparse-equal
fuzz_nnf arb Formula → nnf terminates; output well-formed; nnf∘nnf stable
fuzz_ltl2buchi arb LTL → ltl_to_ngba→degeneralize no panic/OOM; Nba structurally valid (no dangling edge/accept idx)
fuzz_eval (arb Formula, arb Trace) → eval_ltlf no panic; bounded time vs trace len
fuzz_check (arb LTL, arb Kripke) → check_ltl no panic; nested-DFS terminates; Lasso re-simulates (differential mini-oracle)
fuzz_monitor (arb LTL, arb event seq) → Monitor::step no panic; verdict monotone

Corpus seeded from §29.3 pattern + random formulas. Run nightly ≥30 min/target; any crash/timeout → minimized repro committed to tests/regressions/ with a permanent unit test.

29.6 Conformance buckets + pass/fail/known-fail ledger

tests/conformance/LEDGER.md, machine-checkable, one row per case: id | bucket | input | expected | status{PASS|FAIL|KNOWN_FAIL} | ref. CI fails on any FAIL; KNOWN_FAIL is allowed only with a tracking issue ref + rationale (e.g. an unsupported MTL stretch case) and counts are asserted (no silent growth).

Buckets: ltl-pattern (Dwyer/SPEC), ltl-random-seeds, ctl-known (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla examples), ltlf-finite, past-ltl, monitor-rv (LTL3/RV-LTL prefix verdicts), automaton-hoa (golden HOA round-trip), cli-golden. A new operator/optimization may not merge until its bucket is populated. The §25 differential+CTL+fuzz results all report into this single ledger — it is the project "done" gate.

29.7 Performance regression gates (criterion)
