cargo bench (criterion) with committed baselines; CI gate fails on >10% regression vs baseline (median, 3 sampled CPUs noise-budgeted). Benches map to §24 targets:

Bench Metric Target / gate
ltl2buchi/{pattern,random} Nba.nstates, build time ≤ Spot state count ×1.5; gate on time +10%
degeneralize counter blowup nstates ≤ ngba·k; no super-linear surprise
check_ltl/scaling model-check wall time vs |K| within §24 curve; on-the-fly product allocates < eager
eval_ltlf ns / (formula-node × trace-state) linear in |f|·|t|
nested_dfs states visited, peak mem ≤ |product states| (each colored once)
monitor/step ns/event, residual size growth bounded; progression simplify keeps residual ≤ c·|f|

Regression artifacts (criterion HTML, automaton sizes) archived per run for trend tracking. A perf gate trip blocks merge equally with a correctness FAIL — silent state-count blowup in ltl_to_ngba is a known footgun.

Build and Release Engineering

Reproducibility contract

Artifact Mechanism Invariant
rust-toolchain.toml pinned channel = "1.86.0", components = ["clippy","rustfmt"], targets = ["x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu","aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu","thumbv7em-none-eabihf"] every dev + CI runner resolves identical compiler; MSRV 1.78 enforced by a separate matrix leg, not the pin
Cargo.lock committed for the workspace (CLI is the only [[bin]]) --locked in every CI job → no silent dep drift; library crates also resolve through it
Dockerfile FROM rust:1.86-slim@sha256:<digest>, --mount=type=cache off in release build bit-reproducible tl binary; digest-pinned base, no latest
Spot pin spot==2.12.1 (apt or built from tarball sha256) differential oracle frozen → reproducible automaton/verdict comparison
SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH exported from last git commit time deterministic timestamps in release archives
  • Two-toolchain split: 1.78 (MSRV gate) proves no edition-2021 feature beyond MSRV crept in; 1.86 (pinned) is the shipping compiler. Bump either only via PR touching rust-toolchain.toml + this table.
  • no_std + alloc crates (tl-syntax, tl-formula, tl-trace, tl-automata, tl-eval, tl-monitor) must build for thumbv7em-none-eabihf; only tl-cli is std. A #![no_std] core compiling under std is a false pass — the bare-metal target check is the real gate.

Build / test matrix

Leg Toolchain × target Command Gate
stable-build 1.86 × host (x86_64, aarch64) cargo build --workspace --locked --all-features must compile
stable-test 1.86 × host cargo test --workspace --locked --all-features 0 failures
msrv 1.78 × host cargo build --workspace --locked (default features) must compile; catches MSRV regressions
no_std 1.86 × thumbv7em-none-eabihf cargo build -p tl-formula -p tl-syntax -p tl-trace -p tl-automata -p tl-eval -p tl-monitor --no-default-features --features alloc --target thumbv7em-none-eabihf links w/o std; tl-cli excluded
fmt 1.86 cargo fmt --all -- --check no diff
clippy 1.86 cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings zero warnings
doc 1.86 cargo doc --workspace --no-deps -D rustdoc::broken_intra_doc_links no broken links
fuzz nightly cargo +nightly fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=300 no new crash (smoke); nightly soak nightly
bench 1.86 cargo bench -p tl-automata -p tl-eval -- --save-baseline ci criterion regression < 10% vs baseline
differential 1.86 + Spot 2.12.1 cargo test -p tl-automata --features spot-oracle --locked acceptance / verdict parity vs ltl2tgba
  • --all-features leg must enable bdd-guards (Phase 12 symbolic edge guards) so the optional path is type-checked.
  • Matrix is fail-fast: false — collect all leg failures per push, not just the first.

cargo-fuzz job

Targets (under fuzz/fuzz_targets/): fuzz_parser (arbitrary bytes → Parser never panics, only ParseError), fuzz_nnf (random Formula → nnf idempotent: nnf(nnf(f)) == nnf(f)), fuzz_progress (random Formula + State → Monitor::progress terminates, no stack overflow on nested U/R), fuzz_ltl_to_ngba (bounded formula → ltl_to_ngba → degeneralize → emptiness no panic / hang), fuzz_ctl_sat (random KripkeStructure + CTL Formula → CtlChecker::sat bounds-safe).

  • Corpus seeded from Phase 13 pattern formulas; minimized via cargo fuzz cmin. ASAN on in CI fuzz. A new crash → artifact uploaded, job red.

criterion bench job

Benches (benches/): bench_ltl_to_ngba (formula size → Ngba build time + nstates), bench_degeneralize, bench_emptiness (nested DFS over product), bench_check_ltl (end-to-end check_ltl on scaling Kripke), bench_eval_ltlf, bench_ctl_sat (EG gfp / EU lfp on parametric SCC graphs).

  • Targets asserted (regression budget 10%): pattern formula → automaton in < 5 ms at |closure| ≤ 30; check_ltl on 10⁴-state Kripke < 200 ms; eval_ltlf on 10⁶-event trace < 1 s. Baseline ci stored as artifact; critcmp ci pr posts delta to PR.

Quality + supply-chain gates

Gate Tool Policy
lint clippy -D warnings no #[allow] without // SAFETY/justification comment
format rustfmt edition = "2021", default style, checked-in rustfmt.toml
audit cargo-deny check deny.toml: advisories=deny, unmaintained=warn, licenses allowlist (MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD/Unicode-3.0; copyleft denied), bans duplicate-version + wildcard deps, sources=crates.io only
SBOM cargo-auditable build --release embeds dependency graph in the tl binary; cargo audit bin tl re-scans the shipped artifact post-release
unsafe cargo-geiger (advisory) core crates target 0 unsafe; any introduced must be in an // SAFETY: block and noted in Threat Model

deny.toml, rustfmt.toml, rust-toolchain.toml, Cargo.lock are all committed; CI runs cargo deny check with --locked.

crates.io publication

Strict dependency-DAG order (publish each, wait for index, then next — a dependent cannot publish before its dep is live):

tl-formula → tl-syntax → tl-trace → tl-automata → tl-eval → tl-monitor → tl-cli
            (syntax dep formula)   (automata dep formula+trace)
            (eval dep formula+trace+automata) (monitor dep formula+automata) (cli dep all)
  • Versions move in lockstep; [workspace.package] version = "X.Y.Z", each member version.workspace = true → single semver stamp. Path deps carry version = "=X.Y.Z" so a published crate pins its workspace siblings exactly (no float across the DAG).
  • Semver: pre-1.0 0.Y.Z (breaking ⇒ minor bump). Any change to a LOCKED public type/signature (Formula, Ngba/Nba, Lasso, CtlChecker, Verdict/RvVerdict, Monitor, eval_ltlf/check_ltl/ltl_to_ngba/degeneralize/product/emptiness) is a breaking change → bump + CHANGELOG entry. cargo-semver-checks runs in CI against the last published version to catch silent API breaks.
  • cargo publish --dry-run --locked for every crate on every tag before the real push.

tl binary release artifacts

Target triple Arch Linker / toolchain
x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu x86_64 host
aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu aarch64 cross or matrix runner
x86_64-apple-darwin x86_64 macOS runner
aarch64-apple-darwin aarch64 macOS runner
  • Built --release --locked via cargo-auditable (SBOM-embedded). Naming tl-vX.Y.Z-<triple>.tar.gz.
  • Signing: cosign sign-blob (keyless / OIDC) → .sig + .pem per archive; SHA256SUMS + detached SHA256SUMS.sig. Provenance via SLSA build attestation (actions/attest-build-provenance).
  • HOA/dot output (to-buchi) and verdict formats are CLI surface; a release smoke test runs tl eval, tl to-buchi --hoa, tl check, tl monitor on golden fixtures before publishing artifacts.

.github/workflows/ topology

ci.yml       on: [push, pull_request]
  jobs: fmt | clippy | doc | build(matrix: {1.86,1.78} × {x86_64,aarch64})
        test(1.86) | no_std(thumbv7em) | deny | semver-checks
        differential(needs: build; Spot 2.12.1 cached)
  gate: all green → mergeable

fuzz.yml     on: schedule(nightly cron) + pull_request(paths: tl-syntax/**, tl-automata/**, tl-eval/**, tl-monitor/**)
  jobs: cargo-fuzz(targets…, 5 min PR smoke / 30 min nightly soak, ASAN)
        corpus cache + crash-artifact upload

bench.yml    on: pull_request + push(main)
  jobs: criterion(save-baseline ci on main; critcmp pr-vs-ci on PR → comment); regression > 10% → red

release.yml  on: push(tags: 'v*.*.*')
  jobs: verify(reruns ci gate) →
        publish(crates.io, sequential DAG order, --locked, OIDC token) →
        build-artifacts(matrix: 4 triples, cargo-auditable) →
        sign(cosign keyless + SHA256SUMS + SLSA attest) →
        gh-release(upload archives + sigs + CHANGELOG slice)
  • release.yml re-invokes the full ci.yml gate as job verify before any publish — a tag never ships an un-CI'd commit.
  • crates.io + cosign use OIDC trusted publishing (no long-lived secrets). Concurrency group per-ref cancels superseded CI; release group is non-cancellable.

Performance Engineering

Quantified perf contract for tl. All targets restate or refine the Section-4 targets; numbers are single-core, M-class laptop / x86 server baseline, release build (-O3, lto=thin, codegen-units=1). Hot path = ltl_to_ngba (GPVW) -> degeneralize -> product -> emptiness (nested DFS). Cold/cheap = tl-syntax parse, tl-formula NNF/closure.

31.1 Performance budget (ties to Section-4 targets)

Op Input scale Target (p50) Hard ceiling (p99) Mem ceiling Notes
Lexer+Parser -> Formula 1 KB formula text, ~200 nodes < 50 µs 200 µs < 64 KB recursive descent, no backtracking
nnf(&Formula) ~200 nodes < 5 µs 20 µs ≤ 2× input structural, single pass
closure(&Formula) ~200 nodes < 20 µs 80 µs O(|f|) dedup hash-set of subformulas
ltl_to_ngba (GPVW) |f|=20 (typical pattern) < 2 ms 10 ms < 4 MB Ngba ≤ 2^O(|f|), usually ≪
ltl_to_ngba worst |f|=30 dense U/X nest < 200 ms 2 s < 256 MB exponential class; see 31.9
degeneralize (Ngba->Nba) k accept sets, N Ngba states < 0.5× construct time — ×(k+1) states counter blowup factor k+1
product(Nba, Kripke) |A|·|K| states < 1 ms / 10^5 product states — 24 B/state arena lazy preferred (Phase 12)
emptiness (nested DFS) 10^6 product states < 30 ms 150 ms 2 bits color/state linear in states+edges
CtlChecker::sat per op |K|=10^5 states, 10^6 edges < 5 ms/op — 1 Vec<bool>/op EG via SCC = 1 Tarjan pass
CtlChecker::holds (whole f) |f| ops over above K < |f|·5 ms — reuse label Vecs bottom-up subformula order
eval_ltlf (progression) trace len 10^4, |f|=20 < 2 ms 10 ms bounded by residual size residual Formula size key
Monitor::step per event steady-state < 1 µs 5 µs O(residual) per-event latency target
Monitor::step (automaton mode) steady-state < 100 ns 500 ns O(1) table lookup, no rewrite

Throughput corollaries: parse > 20 K formulas/s; monitor (automaton mode) > 10 M events/s; nested-DFS edge-visit rate > 50 M edges/s.

31.2 Benchmarking strategy (criterion micro-benches)

criterion benches under each crate's benches/; one bench group per hot fn. Black-box inputs (criterion::black_box) to defeat const-folding. Fixed RNG seed for synthetic formulas/Kripke (reproducible).

Bench id Crate Fn under test Parameter sweep
bench_parse tl-syntax Parser -> Formula formula size |f| ∈ {10, 50, 200, 1000}
bench_nnf_closure tl-formula nnf, closure |f| ∈ {20, 100, 500}
bench_ltl2ngba tl-automata ltl_to_ngba pattern families (31.3) + |f| sweep
bench_degeneralize tl-automata degeneralize k (accept sets) ∈ {1, 2, 4, 8}
bench_product_emptiness tl-automata product + emptiness |K| ∈ {10^3, 10^4, 10^5, 10^6}, empty vs lasso
bench_ctl_label tl-eval CtlChecker::sat (EX/EU/EG) |K| sweep; EG-heavy formula
bench_eval_ltlf tl-eval eval_ltlf trace len ∈ {10^2..10^5}; residual-growing f
bench_monitor_step tl-monitor Monitor::step (progress + automaton) both modes; |f| sweep

Each group reports time/iter + throughput (elements/s where N is the scale param). Criterion HTML + --save-baseline; CI compares to committed baseline, fails on > 10% regression on any hot bench (bench_ltl2ngba, bench_product_emptiness, bench_monitor_step).

31.3 Macro-benches: LTL pattern families + scaling Kripke

Standard Dwyer/Spot specification-pattern families (the same corpus used for differential testing vs Spot in Phase 13 / ## 25). Bench both automaton size and end-to-end check_ltl.

Family Schema (n = scale) Stresses Expected ltl_to_ngba size
Response G(p -> F q) eventuality accept set small, ~3 states
Until-chain p1 U (p2 U (... pn)) nested U obligations ~n states
Fairness (G F p) -> (G F q) generalized acceptance, 2 sets small, degeneralize ×3
R-nest (release) p R (q R (... )) dual eventualities ~n
X-tower X X ... X p (depth n) next-chain, no blowup linear n
U-of-U dense (a U b) U (c U d) ... exponential class up to 2^O(n) — tail bench
Counter phi_k G F p_0 & ... & G F p_k k generalized sets degeneralize ×(k+1)

Scaling Kripke structures (for check_ltl + CtlChecker): synthetic generators — (a) ring of N states; (b) random N-state graph, out-degree d ∈ {2,4,8}; (c) k-bit binary counter (2^k states, deterministic); (d) dining-philosophers-style product of m components (state explosion 2^m). Report check_ltl wall time + peak RSS vs N; verify linear-in-(|A|·|K|) scaling for emptiness, and that on-the-fly product (31.6) keeps memory sub-linear when the counterexample lasso is found early.

31.4 Profiling: where time and memory go

Tools: cargo flamegraph (perf-based, Linux); samply (cross-platform sampling); perf stat -e cache-misses,branch-misses,instructions; dhat-rs for allocation profiling; valgrind --tool=cachegrind for cache-miss attribution on the nested-DFS inner loop.

Expected profile (typical model-check workload):

ltl_to_ngba  (GPVW node expansion)   ~35%   <- closure walk + obligation-set hashing
product      (state pairing)         ~15%   <- absorbed into emptiness if on-the-fly
emptiness    (nested DFS)            ~30%   <- pointer-chasing edges, color lookups
degeneralize                          ~5%
CtlChecker SCC / fixpoint            (CTL path only) dominant there
parse + nnf + closure                 <5%

Memory: dominated by (1) Ngba/Nba edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard, usize)>> adjacency and (2) the product state set + DFS color array. Automaton construction is the peak-RSS driver on blowup formulas. dhat confirms allocation count target: < 3 allocations per Ngba node (node struct + obligation set + edge vec).

31.5 Allocator analysis: arena states + bitset guards

  • Arena automaton states. Ngba/Nba states allocated from a bump arena (bumpalo or a hand-rolled Vec-backed slab); state index = arena offset. Eliminates per-state Box/malloc churn (was the #1 alloc count in naive GPVW). Adjacency edges[i] is one Vec grown amortized-O(1); reserve capacity from a degree estimate. Free arena en masse at end of ltl_to_ngba — no per-state drop.
  • State AP bitset. State { aps: Bitset } is inline (u64 for ≤ 64 APs; [u64; W] / SmallVec beyond). Trace/Kripke labeling stored contiguously (Vec<State>) -> sequential scan, no indirection.
  • Guard (mask+care). Boolean over APs as (mask: u64, care: u64) (DNF-of-one-cube common case) — 16 B, Copy, no heap. Guard satisfaction (s.aps & care) == (mask & care) is one AND + compare, branch-free. Multi-cube guards = SmallVec<[Cube; 2]> (inline for the dominant ≤2-cube case).
  • Closure / obligation sets. Subformula closure interned to u32 ids; obligation sets are fixed-width bitsets over closure size -> set ops are word-parallel AND/OR, hash = the bitset words. Avoids Formula cloning in the GPVW inner loop (the gravest naive-impl alloc hazard).

31.6 On-the-fly + simplification wins

Quantified speedups vs the eager baseline (measured in bench_product_emptiness + macro-benches):

Optimization Mechanism Expected win
On-the-fly product (Phase 12 / ## 24) build product states lazily inside emptiness nested DFS; never materialize unreachable product states up to 100× memory + time on early-counterexample cases (lasso found in stem); bounds peak RSS to the explored frontier
On-the-fly GPVW ltl_to_ngba expands nodes lazily; prune nodes with unsatisfiable "now" guard immediately avoids whole unreachable subtrees; 2–10× on pattern families
Formula simplification (Phase 12) rewrite rules pre-construction: f U True -> True, True U g/F, G G f -> G f, idempotence, X push, boolean absorption shrinks |f| -> fewer closure subformulas -> exponentially smaller Ngba on redundant inputs
Automaton simplification (Phase 7 / ## 19) drop unreachable/dead states, merge equivalent states post-degeneralize 20–50% state reduction -> proportional product shrink
Guard symbolic edges (Phase 12) bitset mask+care guards merge parallel edges fewer edges in adjacency, faster DFS

Invariant: simplification + on-the-fly are semantics-preserving — verdicts/automaton language unchanged; guarded by differential testing vs Spot (## 25). A perf opt that flips a verdict is the gravest failure class — bench harness pairs every perf run with an acceptance check.

31.7 Nested-DFS cache behavior

  • Two DFS passes (outer = reachability/accepting-state discovery, inner = back-edge cycle search) over the same edges. Color state stored as 2 bits/state in a packed Vec<u8> (4 states/byte) -> 10^6 states = 250 KB color array, fits L2; minimizes cache-miss rate vs a Vec<enum> (would be 1–4 B/state).
  • Adjacency layout: CSR-style (offsets: Vec<usize> + flat targets: Vec<usize>) for the finalized Nba to make edge iteration sequential (cache-line friendly); the lazy on-the-fly product keeps a hash-map frontier instead (random access — accepted cost, offset by not materializing the whole graph).
  • Explicit DFS stack (Vec<(state, edge_idx)>), not recursion -> no stack-overflow on deep stems (10^6-long), predictable memory, no per-frame overhead.
  • Target: nested DFS branch-miss < 5%, last-level-cache miss < 1 per 4 edges on CSR layout. cachegrind gate on the inner loop.

31.8 Monitor per-event latency

  • Progression mode (Monitor::step -> progress): cost = O(residual Formula size). Risk: residual growth under nested U/G -> per-event work creeps up. Mitigation: simplify residual after each progress (boolean normalization + the 31.6 rewrite rules) to keep it bounded; target steady-state residual ≤ 2×|f|, per-event < 1 µs.
  • Automaton mode (monitor-automaton synthesis, Phase 9 / ## 21): precompile monitor automaton (deterministic monitor / LTL3 Moore machine) once; step = single transition state := delta[state][s.aps] + verdict lookup -> O(1), < 100 ns, zero allocation per event, constant memory. Preferred for high-rate streams (> 10^6 events/s).
  • Verdict latency: definitive RvVerdict::True/False must be emitted on the event that closes a good/bad prefix — no buffering delay (online monitorability requirement). Bench bench_monitor_step reports both modes; crossover: automaton mode wins once stream length × per-event-rewrite-cost > one-time synthesis cost (typically < 1 K events).

31.9 Tail behavior on exponential-blowup formulas

  • LTL -> Buchi is worst-case 2^O(|f|) states (intrinsic; e.g. U-of-U dense family, phi_k counter). tl does not "fix" this — it degrades predictably and stays correct.
  • Resource guards (Phase 12 / CLI): to-buchi/check accept --max-states <N> (default 10^6) and --timeout <ms>; ltl_to_ngba aborts with a typed BlowupError (state cap) rather than OOM-killing. CLI exit code distinguishes "verdict unknown — resource limit" from "holds"/"counterexample" — never silently report a wrong verdict.
  • Tail-quantified targets: |f|=30 dense-U may hit 256 MB / 2 s (31.1 ceiling); |f|=40 expected to trip --max-states — that is correct behavior, validated as a bench fixture (assert graceful abort, not crash, not wrong answer).
  • Mitigations that bound the tail: on-the-fly GPVW + early-pruning of unsatisfiable nodes (31.6) keeps the materialized automaton far below the theoretical bound on real specs; on-the-fly product means a counterexample in a short stem terminates before the full blowup is built. Macro-bench U-of-U dense + phi_k track materialized-state growth vs the 2^O(|f|) bound and confirm pruning ratio > 10× on pattern corpus.
  • Degeneralization tail: counter factor (k+1) is linear in accept-set count, not exponential — bench_degeneralize confirms state count = (k+1)·N exactly; no surprise blowup here.

31.10 Regression gating & CI integration

  • Criterion baselines committed per platform; bench-ci job runs the hot subset (bench_ltl2ngba, bench_product_emptiness, bench_monitor_step, bench_ctl_label) on every PR; > 10% slowdown or > 15% peak-RSS growth fails the gate.
  • Each perf bench is paired with the corresponding correctness fixture (differential vs Spot for automaton/check benches) so a perf change can never merge a verdict regression.
  • Flamegraph + dhat artifacts uploaded on the nightly full-corpus run; alloc-count target (< 3 allocs/Ngba node, 0 allocs/monitor-step in automaton mode) asserted programmatically via a dhat test, not eyeballed.

31.11 Quantified Performance Targets (CI Gates)

Canonical M#-tagged restatement of 31.1's per-op budgets, one row per CI-gated target; every M# is checked by the named phase's benchmark suite and re-checked by the Phase 13 regression gate (31.10) on every PR.

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 Lexer+Parser throughput ≥ 20 K formulas/s (1 KB text, ~200 nodes), p99 < 200 µs Phase 1
M2 ltl_to_ngba (typical pattern, |f|=20) p50 < 2 ms, p99 < 10 ms, < 4 MB Phase 6
M3 ltl_to_ngba (worst case, |f|=30 dense U/X) p50 < 200 ms, p99 < 2 s, < 256 MB Phase 6
M4 degeneralize overhead vs construction time < 0.5× ltl_to_ngba time Phase 7
M5 product+emptiness (10⁶ product states) p50 < 30 ms, p99 < 150 ms Phase 8
M6 CtlChecker::sat per op (|K|=10⁵ states, 10⁶ edges) < 5 ms/op Phase 5
M7 eval_ltlf (trace len 10⁴, |f|=20) p50 < 2 ms, p99 < 10 ms Phase 4
M8 Monitor::step (progression mode, steady-state) p50 < 1 µs, p99 < 5 µs Phase 9
M9 Monitor::step (automaton mode, steady-state) p50 < 100 ns, p99 < 500 ns, 0 allocs/event Phase 9
M10 Bench regression ceiling (all hot benches) no metric > +10% time or +15% peak-RSS vs committed baseline Phase 13

Scalability Analysis

Scope: cost vs four independent axes — formula size |phi|, Kripke size |K|, trace/stream length |t|, monitor formula growth — plus the explicit-state ceiling, the levers (on-the-fly + simplification), parallelism, memory, and per-stage bottlenecks. THIS engine is explicit-state/automata/trace based; the symbolic/BDD escape from state explosion is #281, not here.

Cost model per axis

Stage / API Time Space Dominant axis
nnf, closure (tl-formula) O(|phi|) O(|phi|) closure size ≤ 2·|phi| subformulas |phi|
ltl_to_ngba GPVW (Phase 6) worst-case 2^O(|phi|) nodes; built lazily 2^O(|phi|) Ngba states |phi| (exponential)
degeneralize Ngba→Nba (Phase 7) × (k+1), k = #accept sets = #Until-subformulas × (k+1) |phi|
product Nba×Kripke (Phase 8) O(|Nba| · |K|) states, edges = Σ deg O(|Nba| · |K|) |phi| AND |K|
emptiness nested DFS (Phase 8) O(V+E) of product = LINEAR in product O(V) two color bits/state product size
CtlChecker::sat (Phase 5) O(|phi| · (|S|+|E|)) — EU lfp / EG gfp each linear via SCC O(|phi| · |S|) labelings |K|
eval_ltlf progression (Phase 4) O(|t| · cost(progress)) residual formula size |t|
past-LTL DP (Phase 10) O(|t| · |phi|) O(|phi|) past-vector, O(1) lookback |t|
Monitor::step (Phase 9) per-event O(cost(progress)) or O(1) if monitor-automaton precompiled residual / one automaton-state |t| streaming
LTLf→DFA (Phase 11) construction 2^O(|phi|); run O(|t|) DFA size ≤ 2^2^O(|phi|) pre-min |phi|

Two regimes: construction (automaton/DFA) is exponential in |phi|; execution (DFS, labeling, progression) is linear/polynomial in the product / Kripke / trace once built.

Axis 1 — formula size |phi| (the exponential wall)

  • LTL→Nba is worst-case 2^O(|phi|) states (theoretically optimal lower bound; not a GPVW defect). Pattern formulas (G F p, response G(p -> F q)) stay tiny (≤ low tens of states); pathological nestings (deep U/X chains, fairness conjunctions) explode.
  • Degeneralization multiplies by k+1 where k = #Until-subformulas; minimize k via rewrite first (Phase 12) — fewer eventualities → smaller counter.
  • Lever — simplification before construction (Phase 12): rewrite/idempotence (F F p = F p, G G p = G p, X(p∧q)=Xp∧Xq, absorption) shrinks the closure → fewer obligation sets → smaller Ngba. Empirically 2–10× state reduction on real specs.
  • Lever — on-the-fly (lazy GPVW): nodes materialized only when reached; for emptiness, build the product lazily inside the nested DFS (Phase 12) so unreachable product states are never allocated. Often explores ≪ 2^O(|phi|) in practice because most worst-case states are unreachable from init under the Kripke guards.
  • Targets (Phase 13 benchmarks): standard pattern formulas (Dwyer/Spot suite) → Nba in < 50 ms, ≤ few hundred states; random |phi| ≤ 15 → < 1 s. Beyond that, no soft guarantee — exponential dominates.

Axis 2 — Kripke size |K| (state explosion)

  • product size = |Nba| · |S| (states) — multiplicative, the explicit-state core scaling.
  • nested DFS is linear in the product (O(V+E)) — algorithmically optimal; it is the product construction, not the search, that hurts.
  • CtlChecker labeling: O(|phi| · (|S|+|E|)) — linear per subformula in the Kripke; SCC pass (Tarjan) for EG gfp is O(|S|+|E|).
  • The state-explosion wall: |S| is itself exponential in the modelled system's variables/components (a synchronous product of n modules → 2^Θ(n) reachable states). Explicit enumeration of KripkeStructure.states materializes every reachable state — this is the hard ceiling of THIS engine.
  • Why #281 goes symbolic: a BDD model checker represents the transition relation + state sets implicitly, so it never enumerates individual states and can handle systems with 10^20+ states. THIS engine cannot — it is deliberately explicit-state (automata + trace) per its NON-GOALS. Practical explicit ceiling: |S| ≈ 10^6–10^7 reachable states before memory exhaustion (see below).
  • Lever — on-the-fly product (Phase 12): generate Kripke successors and product states lazily during the DFS; a counterexample is often found after exploring a small fraction of the product, never building the rest. Best case: error found in O(stem+cycle) ≪ full product.

Axis 3 — trace / stream length |t| (linear, streaming)

  • eval_ltlf, past-LTL DP, Monitor::step: all linear in |t|, single forward pass.
  • Past-LTL DP keeps only the previous state's past-obligation vector → O(|phi|) memory, independent of |t| — unbounded streams safe.
  • Future-LTL monitoring via progress (the LTL derivative): residual formula can grow, but is bounded by the (finite) set of reachable derivatives = the monitor automaton's state set — see Axis 4.
  • Throughput target (Phase 13): ≥ 10^6 events/s for fixed |phi| with a precompiled monitor automaton; progression-residual mode lower but still linear.

Axis 4 — monitor formula growth

  • Naive progress folding produces a residual Formula that can syntactically bloat across events. Bound: the set of distinct residuals (mod simplification) is finite — it is exactly the reachable monitor-automaton states, ≤ 2^O(|phi|).
  • Lever: apply Phase-12 simplification after each progress, and/or precompile to the monitor automaton (Phase 9) → step becomes O(1) state transition, residual no longer grows. Precompilation pays one-time 2^O(|phi|) construction (amortized over the stream).
  • Without canonicalization, residuals can grow super-linearly → mitigation: cache/hash-cons residuals, intern via closure membership.

Parallelism

Workload Parallelizable? Note
N independent formulas vs one trace/Kripke YES, embarrassingly thread-per-formula; KripkeStructure/Trace shared read-only (&)
One formula vs N traces (monitoring fleet) YES independent Monitor per stream
CtlChecker::sat over subformulas partial independent subtrees parallel; a node waits on its children (data dependency in the bottom-up labeling)
ltl_to_ngba GPVW node expansion limited lazy worklist can be sharded, but dedup/hash-cons of obligation sets is a contention point
emptiness nested DFS NO (sequential) DFS coloring is inherently sequential; parallel nested DFS (e.g. CNDFS) exists but is out-of-scope and complicates the Lasso witness

Conclusion: scale out across independent formulas/traces (linear speedup); the single product-emptiness check stays single-threaded — accept it, or shrink the product (on-the-fly + simplification) instead.

Memory — the hard wall

  • Explicit enumeration is space-bound before time-bound: the product (|Nba|·|S|) and the Kripke states/edges vectors must reside in RAM; nested DFS needs 2 color bits/state but the graph dominates.
  • Arena-allocated automaton states + AP Bitset valuations (per CORE TYPES) keep per-state overhead small (cache-friendly), buying a constant factor — not an asymptotic escape.
  • Practical envelope: product ≈ 10^7 states fits in ~GBs; beyond that → OOM. This is THIS engine's terminal limit and the explicit reason #281 (symbolic/BDD) exists.
  • Mitigations: on-the-fly product (never materialize unreachable states); state hash-consing/merging (Phase 7 — merge equivalent states); bit-packed State valuations; release the Ngba after degeneralization.

Bottleneck summary & mitigations

Bottleneck Trigger Mitigation
Exponential formula → huge Nba deep nesting / many fairness conjuncts rewrite-simplify first (Phase 12); on-the-fly GPVW; minimize Until count to cut degeneralization factor
Huge Kripke (state explosion) many concurrent components on-the-fly product; early-exit on first lasso; otherwise out of reach → use symbolic #281
Full product materialized eager product then DFS lazy product inside nested DFS (Phase 12)
Monitor residual bloat long stream, naive progression per-step simplification + hash-cons; precompile monitor automaton → O(1)/event
Sequential emptiness one large product not parallel; reduce product size; parallelize only across independent checks
Memory exhaustion product/Kripke > RAM bit-pack states, merge equivalents, drop intermediates; hard ceiling otherwise

One-line scaling law: linear/streaming in trace length; linear in the explicit product (|Nba|·|S|); exponential only in |phi| (construction) and in the modelled system's width (|S|) — the latter is the explicit-state wall that THIS engine does not cross by design.

Mental Models & First Principles

The eight mental models below are the minimum set needed to reason correctly about this engine's behavior. Each is stated as a falsifiable claim; violation of any of them leads to a specific, observable failure class.

MM1 — Model checking as language intersection. K ⊨ Aφ reduces to asking whether the ω-language of the system (K as a Kripke generator → all its infinite paths as ω-words) intersects the language of bad behaviors (L(Nba(¬φ))). Emptiness of the intersection = holds. Predicts: any bug in product/emptiness is automatically a soundness bug, never a mere performance issue. Bug class without it: engineers "patch" a wrong verdict locally instead of tracing it to the intersection contract. This is not just the design — it IS the correctness definition (INV-1). Applies at §20 Phase 8 and §7 System Invariants (INV-1).

MM2 — GPVW node = obligation set. Every GPVW node is precisely a set of obligations over the subformula closure — a now-set of things that must hold on the current edge plus a next-set of things to check next. Predicts: any node-construction bug is classifiable as either a now/next split error or a closure-completeness error. Bug class without it: a bug in the now/next split produces an automaton that accepts too much (soundness) or too little (completeness), with no principled way to localize which. Applies at §18 Phase 6.

MM3 — Degeneralization as fair progress. The k-counter in degeneralize implements fairness: it forces the automaton to demonstrate progress on each of the k eventualities (one per Until-subformula) in round-robin. Predicts: the degeneralized Nba's state count is exactly (k+1)×|Ngba|, never more. Bug class without it: an off-by-one in the acceptance wrap-around makes the degeneralized automaton accept runs that starve one eventuality — a silent language-overapproximation → missing counterexamples. Applies at §19 Phase 7 and §7 System Invariants (INV-5).

MM4 — Nested DFS post-order invariant. The inner DFS in CVWY is launched strictly in post-order from an accepting state. Predicts: launching in pre-order means the outer DFS may not have finished exploring a path that loops back to the accepting state. Bug class without it: the inner DFS then fails to close the cycle even though one exists — the most common nested-DFS implementation bug, and it is silent (returns None instead of a real lasso). Applies at §20 Phase 8 and §7 System Invariants (INV-7).

MM5 — Progression as formula derivative. progress(f, s) is the Brzozowski/LTL derivative of f with respect to the single event s. The residual is a formula that talks about all remaining events. Predicts: a residual that simplifies to True means every continuation satisfies f (good prefix), and False means no continuation can (bad prefix) — the foundation of LTL3 verdicts. Bug class without it: treating progress as an ad-hoc rewrite (rather than a derivative with a formal end-of-trace collapse) reintroduces the weak/strong-next confusion independently in every client. Applies at §16 Phase 4 and §21 Phase 9.

MM6 — CTL fixpoints: direction matters. EU is a least fixpoint (start from the terminal set sat(ψ), grow upward by adding φ-predecessors) because eventualities require minimal solutions. EG is a greatest fixpoint (start from sat(φ), shrink downward removing states with no φ-successor) because safety requires maximal solutions. Predicts: swapping either direction changes which states are labeled without any crash. Bug class without it: silently misclassified reachable states, invisible until a differential/known-result cross-check. Applies at §17 Phase 5 and §6 Design Principles (P13).

MM7 — Explicit-state is correct up to the hard ceiling. This engine is correct for all formula/model instances within the size caps (ADR-06): up to |φ|≤256-closure nodes, Ngba ≤ 2²⁰ states, Kripke ≤ 10⁷ states. Predicts: any input beyond the ceiling either succeeds slowly or fails with a typed error — never silently wrong. Bug class without it: treating a resource-cap failure as a correctness bug, or worse, silently truncating work near the ceiling instead of erroring. The symbolic/BDD engine (#281) is needed above that ceiling. Applies at §4 System Constraints and §6 Design Principles (P6).

MM8 — Differential testing is the primary correctness guard. Unlike a theorem prover, a temporal logic evaluator has no self-certifying output: check_ltl returns Ok(()) with no proof. The only ground truth is an external, independently correct tool. Predicts: any silent verdict flip is invisible to unit tests and fuzzing alike, and becomes visible only under a cross-tool comparison. Bug class without it: shipping on "all tests green" while a soundness bug survives because no test compares against an independent implementation. Spot (ltl2tgba) is that tool for LTL; the Phase 13 differential harness is the only structural guard against silent verdict flips. Applies at §25 Phase 13.

Comparative Architecture Table

How this engine compares to four other real temporal logic systems on the same design axes.

Dimension This engine (#283) Spot (twa/ltl2tgba) SPIN (Promela) NuSMV/nuXmv MonPoly / RV-Monitor
Language class LTL + CTL + past-LTL + LTLf LTL + LDL + ω-reg. LTL (never-claim) LTL + CTL + CTL* LTL (metric/temporal extensions)
State repr Explicit-state (Vec<State>) Explicit + symbolic (BDD for edge guards, TGBA internally) Explicit (C structs) Explicit (finite) + symbolic BDD Formula progression (derivative-based)
LTL→automaton GPVW on-the-fly Ngba GPVW + degeneralization + SCC reduction + simulation quotient LTL2BA-style LTL→Buchi via Vardi-Wolper N/A (monitoring, not model checking)
CTL check Iterative CES labeling, explicit Not CTL (LTL/ω-reg focus) Not CTL Symbolic CTL (BDD fixpoints) N/A
Emptiness algorithm Nested DFS (CVWY), two-color Couvreur one-pass SCC on TGBA Nested DFS (bitstate hashing) BDD fixpoint over symbolic product N/A
Finite-trace / RV eval_ltlf (progression) + LTLf→DFA ltl2dfa, ltl2tgba with finite acc. N/A Bounded MC via unrolling Monitor::step, LTL3/RV-LTL, RvVerdict
Past operators Y/O/H/S DP, one forward pass Y/O/H/S via backwards pass in Spot N/A Limited (BDD-based) First-order past operators (MTL)
Counterexample Lasso { stem, cycle } concrete Kripke path Accepting run in HOA; re-simulated Error trail (Promela path) Witness path (textual) Prefix of bad observable events
Scaling wall ~10⁷ explicit states; 2^O(|φ|) automaton Larger due to simplifications; BDD guards extend range Bitstate hashing extends to ~10⁹ states at the cost of completeness Symbolic: 10²⁰+ states possible Unbounded streams; bounded formula size
Correctness oracle Spot (Phase 13) Self (primary oracle for others) Spin reference semantics + Spin's own tests Literature + NuSMV cross-check RV literature + LTL3 reference semantics
Language + ecosystem Rust, no_std, zero BDD dep C++, BDD (BuDDy/CUDD) C (Promela DSL) C++ + CUDD BDD library OCaml / Java; metric extensions

Spot. The reference automata-theoretic LTL toolkit — TGBA internally, simulation-based reduction, mature C++/BDD-guarded implementation — and this project's differential correctness oracle. Its niche: production-grade LTL automaton manipulation and interoperability (HOA) where minimality matters more than a from-scratch pedagogical build.

SPIN. Explicit-state LTL model checking via never-claims and bitstate hashing, trading completeness (probabilistic state coverage) for reaching ~10⁹-state concurrent Promela models — well past this engine's exact ~10⁷-state ceiling. Its niche: large concurrent protocol verification where an occasional missed state is an acceptable price for scale.

NuSMV/nuXmv. Symbolic (BDD-based) LTL+CTL+CTL* model checking that sidesteps explicit-state blowup entirely, reaching 10²⁰+-state systems at the cost of concrete, replayable counterexamples and a heavy BDD-engineering burden (variable ordering). Its niche: hardware/control-software verification where state spaces are enormous but structurally regular.

MonPoly / RV-Monitor. Pure runtime verification — no model checking at all, only formula progression over an unbounded event stream, with metric/first-order extensions this engine deliberately defers (MTL; see the Deferred Features Register). Its niche: online monitoring of a live system where no model exists to check against, only an observed trace.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Derived numbers, traceable from first principles. Each is computable from the preceding formula+constant.

  1. NNF/closure size. nnf(f) is O(|f|), subformula closure ≤ 2·|f| nodes (each node + its negation). For |f| = 30 operators: closure ≤ 60 entries. Memory: 60 × 24 B/Formula-node ≈ 1.4 KB.

  2. GPVW Ngba states (worst case). Theoretically 2^O(|f|) = 2^closure_size. For closure 60: worst case 2^60 states — never realized in practice. On LTL pattern formulas (Dwyer suite): empirically ≤ a few hundred states for |f| ≤ 20. Rule of thumb: nstates ≤ 2^(#Until-subformulas) × 2^(#boolean-depth).

  3. Degeneralized Nba states. Exactly (k+1) × Ngba.nstates upper bound, where k = number of Until-subformulas. For k=3 eventualities and 200 Ngba states: ≤ 800 Nba states. Memory: 800 states × 32 B/state + edge lists ≈ 25 KB + edges.

  4. Product states. |Nba| × |S|. For Nba = 800 states × Kripke = 10⁵ states: product = 8×10⁷ states. At 32 B/state + 2-bit coloring for nested DFS: ~2.5 GB — near the 10⁷ Kripke ceiling. On-the-fly product (Phase 12) materializes only reachable pairs, often 10–100× fewer.

  5. Nested DFS memory. 2 color bits per product state in Vec<u8>. For 8×10⁷ product states: 2×10⁷ bytes = 20 MB for coloring alone. DFS stack depth bounded by product state count (worst case 8×10⁷ frames × ~40 B/frame = 3 GB) — in practice bounded by the counterexample depth (typically ≤ 10³ for real specs).

  6. Monitor residual size. The number of distinct residuals (up to simplification) is bounded by the monitor automaton state count, ≤ 2^O(|f|). For |f| = 10: at most ~1,000 distinct residuals; after canonicalization ≈ tens. Memory: tens of Formula nodes × 24 B/node ≈ a few KB per active monitor.

  7. LTLf DFA states. Subset construction over the progression closure: ≤ 2^|closure(f)|. For |f| = 15 (30-node closure): worst case 2^30 ≈ 10⁹ DFA states. After Hopcroft minimization, typical patterns yield ≤ few thousand states.

  8. CTL labeling cost. O(|f| × (|S| + |R|)). For |f| = 20 subformulas, |S| = 10⁶, average degree 4 → 5×10⁶ edges: 20 × 6×10⁶ = 1.2×10⁸ operations ≈ 1 sec at 10⁸ ops/s. SCC pass for EG is one O(|S|+|R|) Tarjan sweep.

  9. Past-LTL DP memory. Two vectors of size = #past-subformulas, swapped per state. For |f| = 20 past-subformulas: 2 × 20 × 1 bit ≈ 5 bytes per step. Trace length 10⁷ states: O(5 B × 10⁷) = 50 MB of computation — but only 5 B of state (single-pass DP, O(|sub|) space).

  10. Monitor automaton (pre-compiled). States = distinct LTL derivative classes ≤ 2^O(|f|). Transition table = nstates × 2^|AP| entries. For nstates = 100, |AP| = 10 (1024 AP combinations): 100 × 1024 × (1 state-id u32 + 1 verdict u8) = ~512 KB. Per-event cost: one table lookup = O(1).

  11. HOA file size. Each Ngba state emits one line per edge. For 800-state Ngba, average 3 edges/state: 2400 edge lines × ~20 B/line = ~50 KB. Spot parses this in milliseconds.

  12. CI differential test budget. 10,000 random formulas × (ltl2tgba run ~5 ms + our to-buchi ~1 ms + cross-product emptiness ~2 ms) = 10,000 × 8 ms = 80 s. Parallelized at 8 cores: ~10 s. Within the 30 min CI budget.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Questions a staff-level reviewer would ask. Answering each requires tracing through the blueprint's design decisions, invariants, and algorithms.

  1. Why is the gravest failure class a silent verdict flip rather than a crash? What property of GPVW and nested DFS makes this inevitable, and why does fuzzing alone not catch it? (synthesis: §7 System Invariants — INV-7; Threat Model — T4)

  2. GPVW builds nodes lazily. Under what exact condition does a node get materialized in the on-the-fly construction, and what invariant guarantees no node is generated twice? (synthesis: §18 Phase 6; §7 System Invariants — INV-4)

  3. The nested DFS launches the inner DFS in post-order from an accepting state. Give a concrete 4-state automaton where launching in pre-order would cause emptiness to incorrectly return None. (synthesis: §20 Phase 8; §7 System Invariants — INV-7)

  4. degeneralize maps (state, counter) to a flat usize id via s*k + c. Show that the single accept set of the output Nba is visited infinitely often if and only if all k generalized accept sets of the input Ngba are. (synthesis: §19 Phase 7; §7 System Invariants — INV-5)

  5. K ⊨ Aφ uses the negation Nba(¬φ) not Nba(φ). Why? What does the product language emptiness mean for the original model-checking question? (synthesis: §7 System Invariants — INV-13; §20 Phase 8)

  6. The EG fixpoint in CtlChecker is greatest. Why? Give a counterexample to using a least fixpoint for sat(EG p) on a specific 3-state Kripke structure. (synthesis: §6 Design Principles — P13; §17 Phase 5)

  7. eval_ltlf uses end_value to collapse the residual at the last position. What is end_value(G(p)) and end_value(F(p)), and why do they differ? (synthesis: §16 Phase 4; §6 Design Principles — P8)

  8. A Monitor::step call returns RvVerdict::PresumablyTrue instead of Unknown. What property of the current prefix residual justifies this under RV-LTL semantics, and how does it differ from a True (good-prefix) verdict? (synthesis: §21 Phase 9; §6 Design Principles — P11)

  9. Phase 12 adds on-the-fly product construction inside the nested DFS. Does this change the worst-case complexity of check_ltl? If not, what does it change in practice, and why? (synthesis: §24 Phase 12; §6 Design Principles — P5)

  10. tl-formula, tl-automata, and tl-eval are all no_std + alloc. Name three specific standard-library APIs that cannot be used in these crates, and how each is replaced. (synthesis: §10 Repository Layout; §9 Tech Stack ADR Table — ADR-03)

  11. The 7-crate workspace has a strict acyclic dependency DAG. Which two crates, if their dependency were reversed, would create the most severe problem? Explain why. (synthesis: §9 Tech Stack ADR Table — ADR-02, §9.1 layering invariant; §10 Repository Layout)

  12. The Spot differential oracle in Phase 13 uses HOA interchange. If there were a bug in the HOA serializer (but not in the automaton construction), what category of test failures would you see, and what category would remain invisible? (synthesis: §25 Phase 13; Glossary — HOA / dot)

  13. Explain why eval_ltlf ignores loop_start of a Trace. Under what circumstances would using loop_start be incorrect? (synthesis: §16 Phase 4; §15 Phase 3)

  14. An LTL formula φ is said to be monitorable. Does this mean every finite observation over the formula eventually reaches a definitive verdict? Precisely state what monitorability guarantees and what it does not. (synthesis: §21 Phase 9; §6 Design Principles — P11)

  15. The product construction uses label(s') (next-state guard convention). Explain what would go wrong if label(s) (current-state) were used instead, and give a concrete formula+Kripke pair where this matters. (synthesis: §7 System Invariants — INV-6; §20 Phase 8)

  16. The CtlChecker computes sat(Ax φ) as ¬EX(¬φ), not directly. This means sat(Ax φ) requires two calls. On a 10⁶-state Kripke, what is the exact operation count of computing sat(Ax φ) this way, and is there a cheaper direct implementation? (synthesis: §17 Phase 5; §6 Design Principles — P13)

  17. Lasso counterexamples are represented as { stem: Vec<usize>, cycle: Vec<usize> }. What invariant must hold for a Lasso returned by check_ltl to be a valid counterexample, and how does Phase 13's A3 criterion test this invariant? (synthesis: §7 System Invariants — INV-8; §25 Phase 13)

  18. The MSRV is 1.78. List three Rust features used in this codebase (by name) that are available in 1.78 and would not be available in, say, 1.70. (synthesis: §9 Tech Stack ADR Table — ADR-25; §10 Repository Layout)

  19. Why does tl-monitor depend on tl-automata (via the optional monitor-automaton synthesis path), but tl-automata does not depend on tl-monitor? What would break if the dependency went the other way? (synthesis: §9 Tech Stack ADR Table — ADR-02, §9.1 layering invariant; §21 Phase 9)

  20. Spot uses transition-based generalized Büchi acceptance (TGBA) internally rather than state-based. This engine uses state-based acceptance in Nba/Ngba. How does the product construction differ between the two, and what is the implication for the nested-DFS acceptance condition? (synthesis: Comparative Architecture Table; §19 Phase 7)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-1: Symbolic/BDD LTL Model Checking

Sketch. Instead of building an explicit-state Nba and synchronous product, represent the Kripke transition relation and the automaton acceptance sets as BDDs (Binary Decision Diagrams). The product becomes a BDD conjunction; emptiness becomes a fixpoint over symbolic state sets. This is project #281's core technique.

Where it wins over this engine. Systems with |S| ≥ 10⁸ reachable states — typical in hardware verification and concurrent software with many components. A symbolic representation can handle 10²⁰ states in moderate-size BDDs (depending on variable ordering) while this engine OOMs at ~10⁷. The BDD-based fixpoint for LTL (LTL → Büchi → symbolic product → EG/EF fixpoint) is NuSMV's core approach.

Why rejected here. (a) Correctness/pedagogical: the automata-theoretic path produces concrete counterexample lassos that replay on the Kripke structure; symbolic counterexamples require symbolic witness extraction (much harder). (b) Explicit separation: #281 is the symbolic engine; differential cross-checking between explicit (#283) and symbolic (#281) is a portfolio testing strategy. (c) BDD variable ordering is a separate, deep engineering problem that would dominate this project's scope.

The concrete thing that changes. Drop tl-automata's explicit product + emptiness. Replace with a BDD library (CUDD), a symbolic transition relation, and a symbolic EG/EU fixpoint. The check_ltl API surface stays identical from the caller's perspective.

Alt-2: One-Pass SCC-Based Emptiness (Couvreur) Instead of Nested DFS

Sketch. Couvreur's on-the-fly SCC algorithm (FM 1999) computes SCCs incrementally during a single DFS. It detects a non-empty accepting SCC without running a second (inner) DFS. Works directly on the generalized Büchi acceptance without degeneralization.

Where it wins. Generalized Büchi automata (Ngba) can be checked for emptiness without the degeneralize step, avoiding the (k+1) × nstates blowup. On formulas with many Until-subformulas (k large), this can yield a (k+1)× speedup in emptiness check time and memory. Couvreur also yields a direct witness in one pass.

Why rejected here. (a) Complexity: Couvreur's SCC management is more intricate than two-color nested DFS, making it harder to audit for the critical correctness property. (b) The degeneralize step is already designed and tested; keeping nested DFS as the single canonical emptiness check lets the codebase have exactly one correctness-critical graph algorithm. (c) Nested DFS is the standard RV/model-check teaching algorithm — the primary pedagogical goal.

The concrete thing that changes. Remove degeneralize (or make it optional). Rework emptiness to accept Ngba directly. Couvreur's Tarjan SCC + acceptance-set tracking replaces the two-color coloring. The Lasso extraction from SCC roots is more involved.

Alt-3: LTL2BA Very-Weak Alternating Automata (VWAA) Route

Sketch. LTL2BA (Gastin-Oddoux, CAV 2001) translates LTL to a very-weak alternating automaton (VWAA) — an automaton with universal (∧) branching — then converts to a generalized Büchi (GBA) through a subset construction, resulting in automata that are typically smaller than GPVW's output. The VWAA representation is more succinct (linear in |f|) before determinization.

Where it wins. State-minimality: on safety and liveness patterns from the Dwyer specification pattern suite, LTL2BA automata average 2–5× fewer states than GPVW automata. For deep verification where the automaton is queried millions of times (e.g., batchwise hardware model checking), smaller automata directly translate to faster emptiness checks.

Why rejected here. (a) Alternating automata require a separate codebase path (universal branching, powerset construction, different acceptance handling) adding significant complexity. (b) GPVW is the direct, auditable teaching construction — it maps 1:1 to the PSTV 1995 paper, making bug attribution straightforward. (c) The 2–5× size advantage disappears for random formulas and for the formula sizes (|f| ≤ 30) targeted by this engine.

The concrete thing that changes. ltl_to_ngba is replaced by a two-stage ltl_to_vwaa → vwaa_to_ngba pipeline. The Vwaa type needs universal branching (And(Vec<succ>) edges). The subset construction is an additional ~300 LOC. degeneralize and product remain unchanged downstream.

Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") used anywhere in this blueprint (§1.7) resolves to this table — the single register of everything explicitly out of scope for tl (#283), why, and what would pull it back in.

Feature Anchor Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
MTL / timed logics (TCTL); dense-time semantics Deferred Features Register post-#283 follow-on project (none in this blueprint) a consumer needs real-time deadlines / dense-time bounds beyond untimed LTL/CTL
LTL synthesis (formula → reactive controller / Mealy machine) Deferred Features Register post-#283 follow-on project a consumer needs a synthesized controller, not just a verdict or monitor
Probabilistic logics (PCTL) Deferred Features Register post-#283 follow-on project quantitative/probabilistic model-checking need emerges (e.g. randomized protocols)
Symbolic/BDD state-set encoding Deferred Features Register project #281 (symbolic/BDD model checker) the explicit-state wall (~10⁷ reachable states) is reached
Parametric / first-order temporal logic Deferred Features Register post-#283 follow-on project a specification needs parametrized/quantified atomic propositions

Glossary

Terms are defined for THIS engine: how each maps to a tl-* crate, a type, an algorithm, or an invariant. Synonyms are deliberately avoided; use these exact words.

Logics & formulas

Term Definition (implementation-relevant)
LTL Linear Temporal Logic. Formulas over a single linear path (an omega-word). Operators X/U/R/F/G + boolean. Model-checked via the automata-theoretic path (ltl_to_ngba → degeneralize → product → emptiness). Kind::Ltl.
CTL Computation Tree Logic. Each temporal op is fused with a path quantifier (E/A). Branching over the tree of runs from a state. Checked by CtlChecker labeling. Kind::Ctl. State formulas only at top level.
CTL* Superset unifying LTL path formulas + CTL quantifier nesting. Non-goal: this engine does NOT implement CTL*; only LTL, CTL, PastLtl, LTLf are parsed.
LTLf Finite-trace LTL (De Giacomo–Vardi). Same syntax as LTL but evaluated over a finite Trace; the next-operator splits into weak/strong at end-of-trace. Evaluated by eval_ltlf (progression) and Phase 11's LTLf→DFA. Kind::Ltlf.
Past-time LTL LTL extended with past operators Y/O/H/S. Evaluated by tl-eval dynamic programming (Phase 10), each state's past-obligation vector derived from the previous state. Kind::PastLtl.
Atomic proposition (AP) A boolean observable; pub type Ap = u32, an interned id assigned by tl-syntax. A State is the set of APs that hold there (a Bitset). Formula::Atom(Ap).
State formula A formula whose truth is evaluated at a single Kripke state (e.g. any CTL formula, an AP).
Path formula A formula whose truth is evaluated along a path/omega-word (the inside of a CTL quantifier, or any LTL formula). The state-vs-path distinction is enforced by Phase 2 well-formedness.
Negation normal form (NNF) Form where Not appears only on atoms; produced by nnf(&Formula) -> Formula using dualities (not X = X not; not(p U q) = (not p) R (not q); not F = G not; not G = F not; not Ex = Ax not; …). GPVW and the labeling algorithm assume NNF.
Subformula closure The set of subformulas (and their negations, in NNF) of a formula; closure(&Formula) -> Vec<Formula>. Indexes GPVW tableau obligations and CTL labeling.
Formula The typed AST enum in tl-formula: True/False/Atom/Not/And/Or, future LTL X/U/R/F/G, past Y/O/H/S, CTL Ex/Ax/Eg/Ag/Ef/Af/Eu/Au. Boxed children.
Kind Well-formedness classification enum Kind { Ltl, Ctl, PastLtl, Ltlf } assigned to a parsed Formula; gates which evaluator is legal.

Temporal operators (future)

Op Variant Reading Semantics (on a path)
X X(f) next f holds at the next position.
U U(f,g) until g holds at some position; f holds at all earlier positions. Generates an eventuality (one Ngba accept set).
R R(f,g) release dual of U: g holds up to and including the first position f holds (or g holds forever).
F F(f) eventually f holds at some future position. F f ≡ True U f.
G G(f) globally f holds at every position. G f ≡ False R f.

Temporal operators (past)

Op Variant Reading Semantics (relative to current position, DP-evaluated)
Y Y(f) previous (yesterday) f held at the immediately preceding position (false at position 0).
O O(f) once f held at some past or current position. O f ≡ True S f.
H H(f) historically f held at every past and current position. H f ≡ False S' f (dual of O).
S S(f,g) since g held at some past/current position; f held at every position after that up to now.

CTL operators (path quantifier fused with one temporal op)

Op Variant Reading CtlChecker labeling rule
EX Ex(f) exists-next sat(EX f) = states with a successor in sat(f) (preimage).
AX Ax(f) all-next derived: AX f ≡ ¬EX ¬f.
EG Eg(f) exists-globally greatest fixpoint: f-states on a cycle within the sat(f) sub-Kripke, via SCC quotient.
AG Ag(f) all-globally derived: AG f ≡ ¬EF ¬f.
EF Ef(f) exists-eventually derived: EF f ≡ E[True U f].
AF Af(f) all-eventually derived: AF f ≡ ¬EG ¬f.
E[U] Eu(f,g) exists-until least fixpoint: seed sat(g), add f-states with a successor already in the set.
A[U] Au(f,g) all-until derived from EU/EG by duality.

Models & traces

Term Definition
State pub struct State { pub aps: Bitset } — a single valuation; which APs hold. A node of a Kripke structure or one position of a trace.
Trace pub struct Trace { pub prefix: Vec<State>, pub loop_start: Option<usize> }. loop_start = None → a finite word (LTLf, monitoring prefix); Some(i) → a lasso-shaped infinite word that repeats prefix[i..] forever.
Omega-word An infinite sequence of States over APs; the semantic object LTL/Buchi automata range over. Represented finitely as a lasso Trace.
Kripke structure pub struct KripkeStructure { states, init, edges, /* labeling via State */ }. Total transition relation (every state has ≥1 successor); init = initial state indices; edges[s] = successor indices. Input to CtlChecker, product, check_ltl.
Labeling The AP valuation of each Kripke state (carried by State); also the name of the CTL labeling algorithm.

Automata

Term Definition
Buchi automaton (Nba) pub struct Nba { nstates, init, edges: Vec<Vec<(Guard,usize)>>, accept: Vec<usize> }. One accept set; an omega-run is accepting iff it visits some accept state infinitely often. Output of degeneralize and product.
Generalized Buchi (Ngba) pub struct Ngba { …, accept: Vec<Vec<usize>> }. A set of accept sets; a run is accepting iff it visits each set infinitely often. Output of ltl_to_ngba (one accept set per Until eventuality).
Guard pub struct Guard(/* boolean over APs, small DNF / bitset mask+care */). The edge label: the AP constraint that must hold in the current State to take the transition.
Alternating automaton A succinct automaton allowing universal (∧) branching; used in Phase 11 as one route LTLf→DFA. Linear in formula size before determinization.
Accepting run An infinite run of an Nba/Ngba over an omega-word satisfying the acceptance condition (Buchi: accept state infinitely often; generalized: all accept sets infinitely often).
Language emptiness Whether an automaton accepts no word; emptiness(&Nba) -> Option<Lasso>. None = empty; Some(lasso) = a witness accepting run. K ⊨ A φ iff L(product(Nba(¬φ),K)) is empty.
Nested DFS The Courcoubetis–Vardi–Wolper–Yannakakis emptiness algorithm: an outer DFS finds accepting states; from each, an inner DFS searches for a path back to it (a reachable accepting cycle). Two color sets, O(states+edges). Implements emptiness.
Lasso pub struct Lasso { pub stem: Vec<usize>, pub cycle: Vec<usize> }. A finite representation of an accepting omega-run: a stem reaching the cycle plus the cycle repeated forever. The shape of a counterexample.
Strongly connected component (SCC) A maximal set of mutually reachable states; used by sat(EG) (a state with an f-cycle lies in a nontrivial SCC of the sat(f) sub-Kripke).

Constructions

Term Definition
On-the-fly construction Building automaton/product states lazily, only as reached, never materializing the full state space. GPVW (ltl_to_ngba) and Phase 12's on-the-fly product both use it.
GPVW tableau Gerth–Peled–Vardi–Wolper on-the-fly construction ltl_to_ngba. Each Ngba node = a set of obligations split into "now" (atomic/boolean constraints → the Guard) and "next" (the successor node's obligations) over the subformula closure.
Degeneralization degeneralize(&Ngba) -> Nba. Counter construction: Ngba with k accept sets → Nba on state × (counter 0..k); the counter advances through accept sets; the single Nba accept set is visited infinitely often iff all k Ngba sets are.
Product automaton product(&Nba, &KripkeStructure) -> Nba. Synchronous product: states (automaton, kripke) synchronized on AP valuations; accepting iff the automaton component is. Its emptiness decides model checking.

Evaluation & runtime verification

Term Definition
Labeling algorithm Clarke–Emerson–Sistla explicit-state CTL model check; CtlChecker::sat(&Formula) -> Vec<bool> (per-state truth), holds = every init state labeled. Computes EX/EU/EG then derives the rest.
Least/greatest fixpoint (lfp/gfp) lfp = smallest set closed under a monotone operator, iterated from ∅ to convergence (used for sat(E[p U q]) and EF eventualities); gfp = largest such set, iterated from the full set downward (used for sat(EG p) and AG safety/invariance).
Formula progression / derivative Monitor::progress(f, s) -> Formula: the LTL "derivative" rewriting f after observing State s. E.g. progress(X f,s)=f; progress(f U g,s)=progress(g,s) ∨ (progress(f,s) ∧ f U g); progress(G f,s)=progress(f,s) ∧ G f; progress(Atom p,s)=True/False. A residual of True/False is definitive. Drives eval_ltlf and the monitor.
Runtime verification (RV) Checking a running system against a temporal property by consuming an event stream; tl-monitor. Online, prefix-based, no full model.
Monitor pub struct Monitor holding the current progressed Formula (or monitor-automaton state); step(&mut self, &State) -> RvVerdict consumes one event and emits the current verdict.
Verdict / RvVerdict pub enum Verdict { True, False } is the 2-valued definitive answer (a reached residual); pub enum RvVerdict { True, False, PresumablyTrue, PresumablyFalse, Unknown } is the richer RV-LTL 4-valued / LTL3 verdict type.
LTL3 / RV-LTL Bauer–Leucker–Schallhart 3-valued semantics over a finite observed prefix (True good prefix, False bad prefix, else Unknown); RV-LTL is its 4-valued refinement, splitting Unknown into PresumablyTrue/PresumablyFalse via a finite-semantics tie-break on the current prefix.
Good/bad prefix A good prefix is a finite prefix all of whose infinite extensions satisfy φ (→ definitive True); a bad prefix is one none of whose extensions satisfy φ (→ definitive False).
Monitorability A property/prefix is monitorable if some continuation can still reach a definitive (True/False) verdict; non-monitorable prefixes are permanently Unknown. Classified in Phase 9.

Output & validation

Term Definition
HOA / dot HOA (Hanoi Omega-Automata) is the normative text serialization emitted by tl to-buchi for an Nba/Ngba, enabling differential comparison with Spot; dot is the accompanying Graphviz render for human inspection of an automaton/Kripke structure.
Counterexample A witness that K ⊭ A φ: the Lasso returned by check_ltl (Err(Lasso)) / emptiness, projecting the product run onto a Kripke path violating φ.
Differential testing The Phase 13 "done" gate: compare this engine's automaton acceptance / model-checking verdicts against Spot (ltl2tgba) on LTL pattern + random formulas; primary guard against silently flipped verdicts.

.github/workflows/bench.yml Criterion benches on tag; regression diff vs stored baselines.

Total: 30.0 weeks, matching the §1 timeline.

B.1 Definition of "done"

Phase-0 done (the entry gate) = the workspace exists and is alive: all seven tl-* crates compile under their declared no_std+alloc/std profiles, CI + Makefile + Dockerfile build green, and the single smoke test passes end to end (tl-syntax parses G p → Formula, tl-eval::eval_ltlf returns true on a one-State Trace where p holds). No real algorithm is implemented; type shells (Formula, Trace, KripkeStructure, Ngba, Nba, Verdict) and error types (ParseError/ParseErrorKind) exist only as scaffolding. Phase-0 done proves the skeleton and toolchain, nothing about temporal-logic correctness.

Full done (the project gate, earned at Phase 13 ## 25) = every evaluator path is correct against an independent oracle and meets the §1 performance targets: ltl_to_ngba (GPVW) → degeneralize → product → emptiness agrees with Spot ltl2tgba on all standard LTL patterns plus 10k random formulas with zero acceptance/verdict disagreements; CtlChecker labeling matches Clarke-Emerson-Sistla reference results; eval_ltlf, the LTLf→DFA decision procedure, past-LTL DP, and the Monitor (LTL3 / RV-LTL) all match their references; 24h fuzzing yields no panic/UB across parser, automaton construction, and evaluation; and benchmarks hit the budgeted formula→automaton size/time (≤ Spot×2) and model-check-time targets. Crucially, full done requires the gravest failure class neutralized — no silent verdict flip: every reported counterexample Lasso independently replays to violate the formula, and every "holds" survives the differential oracle. Only then is the blueprint-derived engine production-credible; the — <Language> ✅ tag remains out of scope for this (blueprint) phase.
